- **Runtime (`run/effects.rs`).** `Effects` is a small list of `ActiveEffect { kind, left, magical, dispel, .. }` on every `Player` and `Monster` (one entry per kind and magic, see "Magic and non-magic effects"), with the stats resolved through it: `speed_mul()`, `sight(base)`, `res(base)`, `effect_res(base)`, `knock_taken()`, `no_move()`, `no_act()`, `no_turn()`, `bits()`. Adding a kind that is already on (with the same `magical`) keeps the longer time and sends no second event. The API other code uses is on `Run` and takes a `Creature` (`Player(i)` or `Monster(i)`): `apply(c, Apply)` (the one apply path: scales a harmful effect's time by the target's save, see §11a, then applies the effect resistance, refuses the dead, the sinking and a Neutral creature, and returns whether the effect is on; `apply_effect` and `apply_effect_from` are test-only shorthands for it), `remove_effect`, `has_effect`, `hit_effect(c, OnHit)` and `wake_teammate`. `tick_effects` runs first in every step (timers count down; the dead and sinking lose everything) and `damage_wakes` after a hit that does damage.
- **Hits carry effects.** `Damage` has `on_hit: Vec<OnHit>` (#197; `Damage::with_effect` appends, so `Damage` is `Clone`, not `Copy`): a hit carries any number of effects, with no cap. `OnHit { kind, secs, magical, power, look }` is always applied (the `chance` field and its rolls were removed in plan 3b). `hurt_player` and `hurt_monster` apply the split's damage-type effects first (`type_effect_hit`), then `Run::hit_effects(c, &Damage)` (`run/effects.rs`) applies the listed ones in list order, each through the one `Run::apply` path with its own power and its own save; two that cancel each other (heat and cold) are decided by the later one. A hit that did no damage (a pure effect hit, even with several effects, shows no number and no flash) applies only the listed effects, not the split's damage-type ones (the code calls `hit_effects` alone). A killing hit, an effect tick (`from_effect`) and a bodiless pass-through apply none. **The bestower:** `Run::bestower(dmg)` is `dmg.why.by` only when it is a player or a monster (barrels, other objects and terrain give none); it becomes `Apply.source` of the listed effects and of the split's damage-type effects, so a coated weapon's Poisoned remembers who hit. **Ticks name it:** a damage-over-time tick passes the effect's source as its `Why.by`, so the rules log reads "Ayla ▸ Poisoned ▸ Goblin 1" and the line also shows in the bestower's log (§11d); kill credit (`last_hit_by`) and the keystones, which skip ticks, do not change. `defs/enemies.rs::on_hit` and `defs/abilities.rs::on_hit` are the hooks for a monster's or a hero ability's effect; both return `None` today (hero abilities get theirs from weapons, `own.effects` and the Staff of Rot's 50 % poison share).
- **`Apply`, looks, sources and saves (`run/effects.rs`).** `Apply { kind, secs, power, magical, look, source }` is the one description every source builds (`Apply::plain`, `Apply::from_hit(OnHit)`), and `Run::apply(c, Apply)` is the one path: cancel, refuse when a stronger tier is on, scale by the save (the effect's own `save`, else WIL or CON), then the effect resistance, water and the time, add, remove the weaker tiers. `apply_effect` is a thin wrapper (unscaled); a source builds an `Apply` with its `source`, and a hit goes through `hit_effects`. An `ActiveEffect` also keeps its `look`, its `source` (the id and position of whoever applied it) and `from`. A refresh keeps the longer time and takes the new look; it takes the new source only when it has one (`Effects::add_with`), so a refresh without a bestower (a barrel's fire) keeps the one already recorded. **Looks (`EffectLook`):** `Plain` (0) or `Vines` (1) says how the effect is drawn, chosen by the source (Entangle: Vines); the client reads it from the snapshot (below). **Fear** (`flees`): a creature with it runs from `fear_from`, the source's live position while the source is at least 1 px away, else the last known spot; it does not attack (`ai::flee`, called in `run/mod.rs` after `no_act` and before the ally check; no rng, and it moves by `gait()`, so a frightened mimic hops). A frightened monster on a blocked tile still skips `escape_terrain`.
- **Attribute drains (#192).** Weakened (STR), Clumsy (DEX) and Frail (CON) lower an Attribute during the run: `EffectDef.attr: Option<Attr>`, `amount` (-1) and `stacks`, base 20 s (first guesses, `balancing`). **Amount:** `attr_amount(kind, power)` is `-max(1, floor(1 x factor(power, ATTR_EFFECT_PCT 0.05)))`; `power` is the application's power (no power counts as 10), so a source with a high power drains more per touch. Applying again **stacks** (it adds to the one `ActiveEffect`'s `amount`; no cap) and the refresh takes the longer time. The save and effect resistance shorten only the time; `effect_res` >= 1 refuses the effect, so nothing is drained. `Effects::attr_delta()` sums the amounts per Attribute. **`Attributes::with_delta`** (called only by `AttrSources::total`; the sum is the `effects` field): `max(1, base + sum)`, summed first and floored once; an Attribute already <= 1 is never lowered; a positive delta just adds. **`Run::refresh_attrs(c)`** runs on every start, stack and end of an Attribute effect (and when `tick_effects` wipes a dead creature's effects): a hero gets `sheet::run_sheet(class, build, effects, muted)`, a monster `sheet::monster_numbers(&m.base, effects, muted)` (both set `sources.effects`). Max HP is floored at 1 and current HP clamped to it (never below 1, never raised back when the drain ends, so a drain cannot kill). One rules-log line per changed Attribute (`rules_log::attrs_changed`).
- **Magic and non-magic effects (#187, `run/magic.rs`).** Every source says whether what it causes is magic, and a magic effect can be paused: by **anti-magic** (a state on the creature, `Effects.anti_magic`) or by a **Dispel** (a timer). Nothing in a normal run does either yet (the zones and the Dispel spells are #216 and #217); the debug keys (§10) are the only callers.
  - **Entries.** `Effects` keeps one `ActiveEffect` per (kind, `magical`), so a magic and a non-magic Accelerated run side by side. `ActiveEffect.dispel` is the seconds the entry is still paused by a Dispel; it is paused while that is above 0 or `anti_magic` is on and the entry is `magical`. **A paused entry counts for nothing:** `has`, `bits`, `kinds`, `looks`, the stat reads and the attribute amounts all use unpaused entries only (amounts add up over them), and damage over time ticks once per kind, from the unpaused entry with the most time left. The removal paths (`present_kinds`, `has_present`: cancels, water, `remove_effect`) see paused entries too. **Timers keep running while an entry is paused**, so a paused effect can run out. The start event fires only when a kind turns on (its first unpaused entry), the end event when it turns off; a pause or unpause that flips the kind's bit sends the same `Effect` events.
  - **Lasting sources.** Worn items and bought nodes are `sheet::Lasting { Item, Node }`. `Effects.lasting` holds the Dispel timers on them. `muted_list(c)` is every magic source under anti-magic, else those under a Dispel; `refresh_attrs` resolves the sheet again with it. `magic_sources_hero`/`_monster` list the sources that are magic: an item with a magical property (an item whose only magic is `GrantAbility` is not listed, as that stays on) and Radiant (`NodeDef.magical`). A race's nature and a stat block's `Adjust` are never magic and never paused.
  - **Flags at the sources.** `Apply.magical` and `OnHit.magical` are the flag of an effect; an ability's own effects take it from the ability's category (`matches!(d.category, Spell | Innate)` in `sheet.rs`), not from `AbilityDef.magical`; the two are separate fields that agree today. Damage-type effects, War Cry, Poison Coat and Scorched Earth are not magic; Haste and Purify (`use_self_area`), Charm's Charmed, Vanish and the Invisibility trait, the Touch properties and the debug F8 effect are.
  - **API (`Run`).** `set_anti_magic(c, on)`, `pause_lasting(c, src, secs)`, `pause_effect(c, kind, secs)` and `dispel(c, want, n, secs) -> Vec<Pick>`. All go through `repause`: take a `PauseState` (bits, muted list, attribute delta), change the effects, then `settle_pause` sends the on and off events of the kinds whose bit changed and calls `refresh_attrs` once when the muted list or the delta changed.
  - **The pick rule (`dispel_picks(fx, sources, want, n)`).** `Want::Positive` (on an enemy) or `Want::Negative` (on an ally). It picks at most `n` magic things that are not already under a Dispel: lasting sources first (they count as positive and never run out), in `Lasting` order, then effects of the wanted side (`def(kind).positive`) by time left, longest first, ties by effect id. Each result is a `Pick::Effect(kind)` or `Pick::Lasting(src)`.
  - **Client.** The HUD shows one icon per kind. When every entry of the kind is paused it is greyed (`grayscale(1)`, opacity 0.5) and the timer still counts down; a paused item or node is a grey chip with its name (`ui/hud.ts`, `ui/effectIcons.ts`). The entity bitmask leaves out an all-paused kind, so the figure's marks (`StatusView`) go off with it.
- **`run_sheet` during the run.** The hero's numbers from the base build and the effects. **Item** requirements are met by `usable_items(class, b, effects)` (see "Weapons, items and equipment"): built up from the Attributes without items plus the effect delta, so a drain can turn off a chain of items (the item another item enabled goes off with it) and it comes back when the drain ends; **pick** requirements (`resolve_pick` / `check_pick`) against the Attributes **with the usable items** plus the delta, the same way the lobby checks them. An item whose requirement fails gives nothing (no base, no properties); an unusable melee weapon is replaced by the class starter (not drawn differently; the drawn weapons in `Player.weapons` stay). A pick whose requirement fails, or whose weapon is unusable, is marked in `Sheet.disabled` (`[Option<(Attr, i32)>; 2]`, `None` = usable): `Some((attr, n))` is the Attribute requirement that fails, found by `disabled_by`, which follows the error: the pick's own Attribute requirement, or, when the pick failed because an item was dropped (`usable_items` records each unusable item's slot and first unmet Attribute requirement; `resolve_pick` turns such an error into that `Requirement` before `run_sheet` reads it), that **dropped item's** requirement, from the slot the error names (the weapon slot the ability uses, the `NoWeapon` slot, or the slot of the item granting the version); no match is a `debug_assert!` failure and a `STR 1` fallback in release. Kept from the start of the run: keystones, sight, the wolf mods (`refresh_attrs` copies `wolf` from the old sheet). The **light** follows pauses, not drains: `refresh_attrs` takes the hero's light from the build with the muted list and no Attribute delta (`hero_sheet_with_light`), so a muted light item drops its light and a drain that makes one unusable does not (#192); it swaps the hero's light with `swap_entity_light` when that value changes. `run_sheet`, `monster_numbers`, `kit_for` and `monster_kit` take a `muted: &[Lasting]` list (empty in normal play): a muted item loses its magical properties (`live_props`; `GrantAbility` stays) and a muted magic node counts as not bought (a non-magic node in the list stays bought, so a Proficiency is never muted). **`try_use`** refuses a disabled slot without starting the cooldown, except that a ranged primary used in melee still falls back to Strike (`Sheet.fallback`).
- **`MonsterBase`.** A monster's spawn base (`sources: AttrSources`, `max_hp`, `speed`, `kit: KitSource`), kept on `Monster.base`; `monster_numbers(base, effects, muted)` sets the `effects` field of `sources` and re-resolves attrs (`sources.total()`), max HP, speed and kit (`kit_for(KitSource, &Attributes)`) from it, so a drained monster hits softer and moves slower the same way a hero does.
- **`HeroChanged`.** `ServerMsg::HeroChanged { speed, abilities: [ResolvedAbility; 2], fallback, disabled: [Option<(Attr, i32)>; 2] }` goes to the player when `refresh_attrs` changed speed, abilities, fallback or disabled. The client feeds `Predictor.setNumbers` (speed and ability numbers for prediction, so movement does not rubber-band) and `slotUsable` (`game/world.ts`: whether a use is sent and predicted at all); `Hud.setAbilities(abilities, disabled)` greys the slot and sets its `title` to "Needs DEX 14" (no hover: the HUD ignores the pointer). Pressing a disabled slot sends nothing (and predicts nothing), but `RunSession.fixedStep` calls `Hud.needs(slot, req)`, which shows the same "Needs DEX 14" in place of the ability name for 0.8 s (the `hint` class, shared with "Too close"). Stacking keeps one `ActiveEffect` per kind and magic and adds to its `amount`; the server sends each entry's amount and paused flag in the `SelfW` effects tuples, the HUD sums the unpaused entries of a kind (`effectRows`) and the chip shows it as "-N" with the arrow glyph (`ui/effectIcons.ts`); the tooltip reads "Weakened (STR -2)". A drained figure drops a grey-violet mote (`game/status.ts`).
- **Where the flags and stats are enforced.** `no_move`, `no_turn`, `no_act` and `speed_mul` for heroes in `apply_input` (`run/mod.rs`); `speed_mul` for monsters in `ai.rs`; `no_act` for monsters at the top of their turn (`run/mod.rs`) and for a boss encounter in `levels/boss.rs` (a petrified boss is frozen). `res` in `hurt_player` and `hurt_monster`, `knock_taken` in both knockback functions. `sight` through `sight_now` on `Player` and `Monster`, so `perception::sees`, the snapshot filter and the `SelfW` vision all use the effect's vision.
- **The lich** (`run/bosses/lich.rs`) casts three spells from its `SPELLS` table (`StoneGaze`, `SleepMist`, `BlindingCurse`, tried in that order when their cooldown is up and a hero it sees does not already suffer the effect; delete an entry to balance). Blinding Curse: a dark orb (`EntityKind::BlindOrb` 61) that does 6 damage and blinds for 3 s. Sleep Mist: a purple orb (`SleepOrb` 62) that puts a hero to sleep for 4 s. Stone Gaze (`Anim::Gaze` 14): a 1 s wind-up locked on one hero, and if the lich still sees that hero when it ends, the hero is petrified for 3 s. The numbers are the abilities' (kit) numbers; the script owns only the opening delays (`LICH_GLOOM_FIRST` 2, `LICH_NOVA_FIRST` 6, `LICH_BLIND_FIRST` 4, `LICH_SLEEP_FIRST` 7, `LICH_GAZE_FIRST` 9, `LICH_RAISE_FIRST` 8 s) and the Nova's extra 0–2 s wait.
- **Wire.** `EntSnap` has a trailing `u32` bitmask `effects` (creatures only). The own hero also gets `SelfW` elements 14 to 17: `vision` (tiles, through the effects), `effects` (`[id, seconds left, amount, paused]` tuples), `speed_mul` and `no_act`; elements 19 and 20 are `paused_items` and `paused_nodes` (#187, see "Magic and non-magic effects"). `Ev::Effect{id, kind, on, look}` (wire code 13; `look` is the effect's look on a start and 0 on an end) says an effect started or ended on an entity; the client does not read the event's look (see Client); for a hero it is an `event_on_hero`, for a monster it is FOV-filtered. `ClientMsg::DebugEffect{kind}` (F8) is honoured only when debug is allowed and marks the run as debug. **Looks on the wire:** the snapshot has a tenth element `looks` (`SnapW`), a list of `[entity id, effect id, look]` for the entities in view that carry a non-plain look (`visibility::looks_of`, players and monsters); the client reads looks from snapshots only, so it covers both a start and a late view (the user ruled, 2026-10-21: `Ev::Effect.look` stays on the wire but is unused client-side). The `EffectLook` table is exported as `EFFECT_LOOK`, and `flees` per effect in `EFFECTS`.
- **Waking.** E next to a teammate with a `wakes_on_use` effect ends it (`wake_teammate`, within `USE_REACH`); waking beats using an object. The mimic's old `ASLEEP` flag became `dormant` and is now `hiding` (see "Hiding and dormant"), so "asleep" means the effect only.
- **Client (`game/status.ts`, `anim/EntityView.ts`).** `has(bits, kind)`, `lyingPose` and `lieLayout` (a prone figure lies rotated 90° towards its facing, the floor side cropped), `frameBounds`, and `StatusView`, the marks around one figure, owned by its `EntityView`: blind (three dark motes circling the head), asleep (a rising "z" and the lying pose), accelerated (fading afterimages), burning (three flickering flames that hop around the body), frozen (a blue additive haze and snowflakes that fade in and out), poisoned (a green tint that pulses, and bubbles rising), chilled (a pale blue body tint, legs at 0.7 pace, an occasional snowflake), singed (small rising embers), tainted (a slow faint green pulse, 0.3 at most against Poisoned's 1.0), fear (a bobbing "!" above the head in the sound-text style), coated (a green glint at the hand, `StatusAnchor.hand`), entangled (bands at the feet; a `Record<Look, ...>` table, so a new look fails to compile) and warded (a thin pulsing gold ellipse). `lookOf(looks, id, effect)` (in the Phaser-free `game/looks.ts`) reads a look from a snapshot's `looks` (`Plain` when absent); the `Interpolator` sets `EntState.entangled` from the same snapshot that supplies the entity's `effects`, so the look never runs ahead of the effect. `EntityView` itself draws petrified (greyscale on body and weapon, frozen on the frame it was turned to stone in; WebGL only; the PreFX controller is removed with `preFX.disable(true)` when the stone ends, see §9 "Per-frame costs") and slowed (a blue tint, legs at half pace); frozen also holds the animation frame and the weapon pose it started on (no greyscale). `Predictor` takes `speedMul`, `noAct` and the no-turn flag from `me`; `LightView` takes its vision from `me.vision` instead of a constant; the lich's gaze is a thin grey beam drawn while `ANIM.Gaze` shows (`PhaserRunView.drawGaze`). Hints: "E Wake" over a sleeping teammate and "Asleep. A teammate can wake you" with an E key cap (the first is the board's `KeyCap`, the second `Hud.setAsleep`). HUD icons (`ui/hud.ts`; one drawn symbol per kind in `ui/effectIcons.ts` `EFFECT_ICON`, see §9 "UI"): the own hero's with the whole seconds left, the party's without; the other heroes' also sit beside their name tags (`game/tagEffects.ts`). `?gallery` has a row per effect and `?lich` shows the three spells.

**Hiding and dormant.** Two waiting states of a `Monster`, both skipped by the AI until they end. `dormant` (`FLAG.DORMANT`): the boss before the party enters its hall, the lich's channelling disciples. `hiding` (`FLAG.HIDING`): a mimic that looks like a closed chest; `touch_chests` or a hit ends it. Heroes sense neither; the wolf notices neither and bites neither. A later concealed creature (an invisibility effect, a ghost) would use `hiding` and decide itself whether the wolf senses it. (The Assassin's Hide ability and `flags::HIDDEN` are gone; Hide may come back as an invisibility effect.)

**Sides (`run/targets.rs`).** Every `Player` and `Monster` has a `side: Side`: `Hostile` (enemies and bosses), `Friendly` (heroes, the wolf, recruited companions) or `Neutral`. `foes(a, b)` is true only for a Hostile and a Friendly; the friend-or-foe checks (enemy perception, area attacks, hazards, chains, the rules log's `Skip`) go through `fights` (below), which is `foes` plus grudges, reading each side by `side(run, creature)`. **Heroes can change side** (#224): `Player.side` is Friendly except while a hero is charmed, when it is the charm's side (see "Charm"). `targets::may_hit(run, side, creature)` is the one per-victim question for any attacker's side: monsters through `heroes_may_hit`/`enemies_may_hit`, heroes through `foes(side, hero.side)`, Neutral hits nothing. Melee (`melee_targets`, with `MeleeTarget::Player`), swings, rings, dashes and the overkill pierce, projectiles and blasts (Chain Spark's jumps included), hero and enemy hazards, area effects (War Cry's Fear, Frost Nova, Holy Light, Haste, Purify: they reach the user's own side), the dodge's threats and the rules log all go through it or `fights`, never a "charmed" check, so enemy attacks and hazards skip a hero on their side and a hero's hits reach a hero on another side. A shot keeps the side it was cast for when it credits or hurts (`shot_credit`), so a hero turning back mid-flight does not change whom the shot belongs to. `Hazard.side` is a `Side` (the old `HazardSide` is gone) with an `owner: Option<u32>`. A **Neutral** creature is nobody's foe and nobody's friend. One that is not wild (the unrecruited knight) is untouchable: `hurt_monster` refuses all damage (also a hit that would pass through), `Run::apply` refuses every effect, `knock_monster` gives no push, `heal_monster` no heal, it is never picked as a target, and it idles through `Role::Waiting`. A wild animal is Neutral too but takes hits and effects and runs its AI (see Wildlife). It is sent to a hero only when in view (`visibility.rs`); Friendly creatures are always sent to the party.

- **Grudges (#227).** The side table is not the whole truth: a `Monster` also has `grudge: Grudge { heroes, enemies }`, the sides it fights for good. `stance(run, c)` gives a creature's `Stance { side, grudge, me }`, and `fights(a, b)` is the side table (`foes`) plus either one's grudge against the other's side. Target choice (`ai::find_visible_foe`, area attacks, hazards, chains) reads `fights`, so a creature with a grudge is a normal foe to that side and fights it back. `heroes_may_hit(m)` and `enemies_may_hit(m)` are the matching per-victim filters the attacks use to find who they may hit (`hurt_monster` does not call them); a wild animal may be hit by anyone. A hero's **melee fallback** (`abilities::melee_targets`: a ranged primary strikes, a ranged secondary is blocked) counts only creatures the heroes fight (a Hostile one, or one with a grudge against them), not `heroes_may_hit`: a calm animal in reach neither triggers it nor blocks the ranged ability. The client's `inMelee` matches (`meleeFoe` in `game/shotTargets.ts`). The ogre's slam counts only the creatures it `fights`.
- **Only a hit sets a grudge** (`Run::hit_gives_grudge`, called from `hurt_monster`): damage, a burn tick and a zero-damage on-hit (a sleep orb) from a hero or companion give the animal a grudge against the heroes, from an enemy against the enemies; a `Flee` animal is scared instead (see Wildlife). Effects applied without a hit give none (the user's decision): a War Cry frightens a calm boar without angering it. Lava gives none. A creature that is not wild never gets a grudge.
- Companions pick prey by `find_prey`, which takes an enemy or a creature with a grudge against the heroes, never a calm animal.
- **`Rule.skip_calm`** makes chains, jumps and the gaze skip a calm animal (one that does not fight the user), so a chain or jump never goes to a bunny; a boar with a grudge against the heroes is a normal target. Areas, swings and dashes still hit a calm animal they cover.

**Companions (`run/companion.rs`, `run/wolf.rs`, `defs/companions.rs`).** A companion is an ordinary `Monster` with `side: Friendly` and `companion: Some(Companion)`, so it reuses movement, terrain, knockback, effects, lights and snapshots, and the same Attributes, abilities, kit and equipment as every creature. `Companion` holds `leader` (the hero id it follows now), `recruiter`, `summoned`, `prey`, `following`, `reach_t` and its `escort` (the `Escort` distances). The **wolf** (`summoned`) has no script of its own: it is an Escort (`companion::regroup`/`act`, run through `behaviour::tick`) on the melee base AI with the Leap module, like the knight; `run/wolf.rs` only spawns it, handles the Ranger's button and its death; `Player.wolf` holds its id. Its dash is `Monster.dash`, its cooldowns `Monster.cds`, `Monster.last_safe` (the last safe tile, kept for every non-boss monster by `terrain_monster`), set only through `run::abilities` (`monster_use`, `monster_dash_to`). The wolf carries a `Kit` (`kit_for(KitSource::Wolf(mods), &attrs)` in `sheet.rs`: Bite and Pounce, Pounce changed by the version's `WolfMods` through `apply_mods`) and uses them through `monster_use` for the heroes' side: its abilities hit enemies only and give no keystone credit. Its numbers are `WOLF_*` in `defs/companions.rs`; its profile is a Magical Beast plus 20 % cold resistance (`wolf_profile`).
- **The hierarchy.** `companion::leader_of` is a pure function of who is alive: the recruiter while alive and in the run; else, for a recruited companion, the host (the first player), else the next living hero in join order. A summoned companion has no one else: when its summoner is gone `update_leaders` (called each step) removes it with a puff (`Boom k 9`), no message and no cooldown. Fallen heroes do not come back before #221, so a switch of leader is for good.
- **Summoned vs recruited.** Summoned (the wolf): made by an ability, dies with its recruiter, its death starts the ranger's re-summon wait. Recruited (the knight): a Neutral creature that a hero turned Friendly with E (`companion::recruit`), keeps going under the next leader when its recruiter falls, and its death costs nothing (`companion::died`: no XP, no coins, no kill, `Ev::Died` and the message "The Knight has fallen").
- **Ability.** `AbilityId::SummonWolf` (secondary, 8 s) calls `wolf::use_ability`: no living wolf means `spawn_wolf` (`summon_spot`, `Boom k 9`); a living one means `heal_monster` for 20 % of its max (also at full health). `wolf_died` (called by `kill_monster`) gives no XP and no coins, sends `Ev::Died` and the message "<name>'s wolf has fallen" and sets the ranger's `cd2` to `WOLF_DEAD_COOLDOWN` 60 s, scaled like Summon Wolf's resolved cooldown (60 × 10 / CHA without mods; #198).
- **The AI hook (`Run::step`).** After the sinking, cannot-act and fear checks, `behaviour::tick` runs every companion, the wolf too, through `companion::regroup` and `companion::act`; the rest go to their role (§5 "AI"). `regroup` reappears the companion beside its leader when lost (`lost`: beyond `Escort.reappear` or with no path, checked every `COMPANION_REACH_EVERY` 0.5 s; this covers the sealed boss hall), looks for prey (`companion::act` calls `find_prey`: enemies it sees within `guard` of the leader or `defend` of itself, and within `leash` of the leader, nearest the leader first; never dormant, hiding or flying ones; a companion with no attack, like a charmed deer, has no prey) and, with prey, runs the **ordinary enemy AI** (`ai::tick_monster`) so it fights with its own kit; without prey it `follow`s (starts beyond `Escort.follow`, stops inside `follow_stop`). The distances are an `Escort` (`WOLF_ESCORT`, `KNIGHT_ESCORT`). Not while pushed, sinking, asleep or frozen. Every escort counts its attack waits (`ai.cd`, `alt_cd`) down on the ticks it does not fight (`count_waits`: following, climbing out of lava, dashing), so it swings as soon as the wolf of old did. The Leap module (Pounce in its kit: 4 tiles, 300 px/s, 20 damage, 5 s) jumps chasms; an attack dash that would end over a chasm carries on to solid ground, and `dash_back` returns to `Monster.last_safe` after a push over a chasm (see "AI", Leap). Hits on prey go through `hurt_monster` with the leader as source for the wolf, so its kills and statistics count as the ranger's.
- **The knight.** `EnemyType::Knight` (Human, 60 HP, speed 68, Strike with the Soldier Sword; a first guess, label `balancing`). `levels/dungeon.rs` spawns one, last (so every earlier entity keeps its id), in every dungeon's start room at `knight_spot` (the free tile nearest 32 px north of the heroes' spawns, clear of them and of props; none means no knight), Neutral. E (`objects::use_object`) tries `wake_teammate` first, then `companion::recruit` (the nearest Neutral monster within `USE_REACH`): Friendly, `Companion { leader: hero, recruiter: hero, summoned: false, .. }`, a fresh `Ai`, the message "<hero> recruited the Knight" and `Boom k 9`.
- **Foes.** Enemies choose among heroes and Friendly monsters: `ai::find_visible_foe` yields `Target::Player(id)` or `Target::Monster(id)` (it replaced `Target::Ally`). Area attacks and hazards use `Run::foes_in` and `foes_touching`, `hurt_creature` and `knock_creature`, so bosses' cleaves, breath, novae, fire patches and barrel blasts hit companions; bosses and the lich's curses still choose heroes. `hurt_monster` refuses damage from a hero source on a Friendly monster, the safety net behind the per-attack skips (`dagger_target`, `melee_arc`, `dash_contact`, `advance_projectile`, `explode`); each has a test. Friendly effects and heals (Holy Light, Haste, Purify, War Cry) reach Friendly monsters through the same side checks.
- **Snapshots.** Friendly monsters are always sent to the party (`snapshot_ents`), with `extra` = the leader's id, `side` as the last field and a health bar; the client draws their numbers and bar by the hero rule (over the darkness, see Draw order). A Neutral creature is filtered by sight like any monster. `Ev::Dmg` and `Ev::Heal` on a companion have `p` set (the hero rule). The rules log tags the knight like any monster ("Knight 1"); only the wolf is named "<leader>'s wolf".
- **Client.** `FIGURES[DireWolf]` and `FIGURES[Knight]` (atlas frames `dire_wolf_*` and `npc_knight_idle_anim_f0-3`/`npc_knight_run_anim_f0-3`, trimmed to one shared box by `buildFigure` in `scripts/figure-frames.ts`), the bar by `side` (a Neutral creature's is blue-grey, a Friendly one's green like a hero's), always shown with the creature's name for every non-Hostile creature but the heroes and the wild animals (the knight "Knight", the wolf "<leader>'s wolf"; `barName` in `game/barName.ts`), `pickUseHint` (`game/useHint.ts`: "Wake" for a sleeping teammate, "Recruit" for a Neutral creature that is not a wild animal, then the bare cap for barrels and torches; nearest within `CONST.USE_REACH`, in the server's order), `shotPassesThrough` (`game/shotTargets.ts`: predicted hero shots fly through every non-Hostile creature but a wild animal), a lunge for the bite, `natureSparkles` for `Boom k 9`, `secondaryLabel` in `ui/hud.ts` ("Summon Wolf" or "Heal Wolf"), prediction of the cooldown only, and the cosmetic dagger and shot checks skip non-Hostile creatures.
- **Charm (#223 and #224, `run/charm.rs`).** One spell, `AbilityId::Charm` (41, renamed from Charm Monster), and one effect, `EffectKind::Charmed` (harmful, 8 s, saved with WIL, magical; resisted fully by Undead, half by Dragon and Demon in `defs/races.rs`; hero races resist 0; `Run::apply` refuses it only on the Neutral knight, like every effect). It works for any caster and on any creature: the orb hits the caster's foes by the ordinary side rules (`targets::may_hit`), and the charmed creature joins **the caster's side** for the duration. A hero's Charm lands on monsters (and on a hero on another side), a monster's Charm on heroes and their companions (the wolf, a recruited knight), a charmed hero's Charm on its own party. A creature already charmed toward the caster's side is not a foe and is not hit. **Decision 2 (the side):** the charm side is the caster's side (`charm::side_for`); if the caster is gone (dead, or Neutral) the target takes the opposite of its own side, and a resumed pause recomputes it the same way. A refresh by a caster of the other side moves the creature to that caster's side and never overwrites `before`. `Charm.caster` is an entity id (a hero or a monster). Below, the monster path first; a charmed monster becomes a recruited-style companion of its caster. `charm::sync` runs once per step right after `tick_effects`, masterless monsters first, then owned ones: a monster whose unpaused `Charmed` entry names a caster (`source`), or whose living master is charmed now, is switched on: `Monster.charm = Some(Charm { caster, side, on, before })` keeps its `side`, `companion`, `wild`, `grudge` and `ai.home` (taken once; a refresh or a pause never overwrites it), and it takes the charm's side with `Companion { escort: KNIGHT_ESCORT, leader/recruiter: caster, summoned: false }`, no `wild`, no grudge and a fresh `Ai`. Another caster's refresh only moves `recruiter`/`leader` (and the side, see above). A charmed monster on the enemy side (a monster caster) fights the heroes and their companions with its kit; if its caster dies it keeps the caster's side and, with no living creature of that side to escort (`drop_leaderless`: `companion::leader_of` finds none), acts as a plain Hostile creature of its kind until the charm ends. The summoner rule: a summoned companion (`Companion.summoned`) takes its summoner's side every step (`charm::follow_summoner`), so the wolf turns with its charmed ranger, and a minion with its charmed master; a companion that was not summoned (a recruited knight, a monster a Wizard charmed) stays and follows another living hero of its side (`leader_of`, as when its leader dies). When nothing charms it now (the timer ran out, or anti-magic or a Dispel paused the entry, #187: no special case), the saved fields come back where it stands, with a fresh `Ai` at the old home (no wind-up, cast, slam, dash or channel carried over); `charm` stays while a paused entry or a charmed master is left, so it is charmed again with the same `before` when the pause lifts. Minions raised by a charmed master are switched at once (`minions::raise` calls `sync_one`). Death: `kill_monster_by` and the lethal branch of `hurt_monster` call `charm::revert` first (so does the sinking kill in `terrain_monster`), so a charmed monster dies as the enemy it is (XP, coins, its minions, `Level::on_killed`), never through `companion::died`. Bosses: the step's monster loops skip a boss only while it is not charmed, and `levels/boss.rs` does not tick its `BossBehaviour` while it is charmed, so a charmed boss runs the generic Escort path with its kit (the generic AI uses `kit[0]` and `kit[1]`; the chase in `ai.rs` picks the alt ability through `ai_shapes::worth_using`, see §5 "Rules by shape", so a charmed Demon swoops: its Swoop is `Shape::Dash` with no module) and resumes its pattern afterwards. A companion with speed 0 (the Disciple) neither follows nor reappears (`companion::regroup`, `act`). Rules log: `rules_log::charm` writes `LineKind::Charm` lines ("is charmed and follows <caster>", "turns back"; the caster may be a monster). **A charmed hero** (`charm::sync_hero`, run for players before monsters): while an unpaused `Charmed` entry names a caster, `Player.charm = Some(HeroCharm { caster, caster_name, side, brain })` and `Player.side` becomes the charm's side (the field's "always Friendly" is gone). Everything that reads sides then follows without a "charmed" check: enemies ignore the hero and its area abilities help their side, the party's hits, shots and hazards hurt it (see "Sides"). `charm::is_hero_charmed(&Player)` says "charmed now" (a charm and an unpaused entry; a Dispel or anti-magic pause gives control back and comes back after the pause, as for monsters). The hero is driven by `run/hero_brain.rs` through an input adapter (see Tick); its kit is its equipped LMB and RMB, resolved as always, and it walks and fights with the monsters' machinery: nearest foe it sees (`fights`: the party and Friendly companions), else the last place it saw one, else it follows the caster at `KNIGHT_ESCORT` distances, else it stands. It stops inside 0.85 of its primary's reach, steers with `walk_line`/`astar` as `Mover::Enemy` (with a 4 px look-ahead so it never clips a chasm corner) and picks abilities through `ai_shapes::worth_using`, never by ability id. **Ruling:** its shots take ids from `AI_SHOT_BASE` (0xF000), so the client never mistakes them for its own predicted shots; it does not lead its shots. **When it ends** (timer, Dispel, anti-magic): `side` goes back to Friendly, a dash or Ground Slam in progress is cleared, position, HP and running cooldowns stay, and inputs count again from the next one. A hero refreshed by a caster of the other side gets a fresh brain. **Deaths** go through the normal hero death path (permadeath, spectating, banking). `charm::blame` credits the kill to the creature that cast the charm in the rules log, the statistics and the death text ("<hero> has fallen to <caster>'s charm"), never to the teammate who struck the blow; the same for a hero killed by a charmed hero, by an effect tick, or by terrain while charmed. Kills and damage by a charmed hero give the party no XP, coins or credit while it is on the enemy side (`credit_of` hands out a hero only while Friendly), and a hurt hero is never refused for friendliness. `Player.history` (a position ring buffer, like the monsters') lets a hit on a charmed hero be rewound. **Debug:** F8's Charmed on the own hero uses the nearest living Hostile monster as caster (none: a message and nothing applied). The wolf: `wolf::spawn_wolf` takes the ranger's side, and Summon Wolf neither heals nor re-summons a wolf an enemy has charmed (the cooldown is still spent). **Client:** `SelfState.charmed_by` (`Option<String>`, wire element 21 of `SelfW`; set by `visibility.rs` only while `is_hero_charmed`, from `HeroCharm.caster_name`: the hero's character name, "the <Kind>" for a monster, "someone" if the caster is gone; `charm::caster_name`) feeds the charmed view (§8, §9), the heart icon (`ui/effectIcons.ts`), a pink bobbing heart above the head (`game/status.ts`, beside Fear's "!"; also over charmed heroes) and the `CharmOrb` glow (`game/anim/defs.ts`).
- **Adding another companion:** see "How to add a companion" in §11.

**Solid objects in movement (`collision.rs`).** `move_box` and `step_move` take `solids: &[Solid]` (`Solid { x, y, half }`, squares). Solids are checked axis by axis like tiles; a figure that already overlaps one may still move away from it in any direction, so nobody gets trapped. `Run::solids(except)` lists the objects for which `WorldObject::blocks()` holds (solid, not gone, not a sinking barrel). The same predicate decides whether a shot stops at an object (`advance_projectile`, through `stops_shots`, which adds closed chests), which tiles A* avoids (`Run::object_tiles`) and whether a straight walk is clear (`ai::object_free_line`), so a sinking barrel stops nothing. They apply to heroes (`apply_input`), monsters (`ai::move_toward`, `separate_monsters`, `slide_monster`) and sliding barrels. The TypeScript port (`sim/collision.ts`) has the same parameter, and the predictor (`world.ts`, `Predictor.solids`) takes the barrel positions from the latest snapshot (`RunSession.onSnapshot` keeps those with `anim < 2`). The shared fixtures include runs with barrels, so `sim.test.ts` pins Rust and TypeScript to the same result. Barrels do not block sight.

**Wildlife (#227; `Role::Wildlife`, `Monster.wild`, `Monster.grudge`).** The nymph's cove holds animals: `EnemyType::Bunny`, `Deer`, `Stag` and `Boar` (race Animal; ids `KIND.Bunny` 27, `Deer` 28, `Stag` 29, `Boar` 33). An animal is a Neutral `Monster` with `wild: Some(Wild { reaction, scare, min_flee, scare_from, calm_dist })` set from `EnemyDef.wild`; the bunny, deer and stag have no abilities and no weapons, the boar has a Strike with tusks. Unlike the unrecruited knight, anyone may hit it (`heroes_may_hit`/`enemies_may_hit` accept `wild`), and it has no use key (not recruitable).
- **Calm.** `behaviour::tick` runs a Wildlife monster through the base AI like a Hunter, but with no foe it wanders near its home (`Monster.wanders`), and no one is its foe (`fights` is false for a Neutral without a grudge), so enemies ignore it and `skip_calm` keeps chains off it.
- **`Reaction::Flee` (bunny, deer, stag).** A hit sets `scare` to the attacker's id (`Wild.scare`) and `min_flee` to `WILD_MIN_FLEE` (1.5 s, `defs/enemies.rs`); `calm_dist` is max(`WILD_CALM_DIST` 96 px, the distance at the hit + 48). `behaviour::wildlife_flees` runs from the attacker (from `scare_from`, where it was last seen, while it is out of sight) for at least `min_flee` after every hit, so a second hit runs it again. After that it calms down (`scare` cleared, Idle) when the attacker is gone (dead or left), out of its sight, or beyond `calm_dist`; it then wanders back toward its home.
- **`Reaction::FightBack` (boar).** A hit gives it a grudge against the side of the attacker (`Grudge.heroes` or `enemies`); with it, `fights` makes it a foe of that side, and as a Hunter it chases, strikes, searches, returns home and wanders. It keeps the grudge for good, so an enemy the boar was hit by (a lured goblin) and the boar fight each other, and a boar that hurt nobody else stays calm to the rest.
- **Death.** A calm animal pays nothing: no XP, coins or kill credit. A boar with a grudge against the heroes killed by the heroes' side counts as a normal kill and pays its XP but no coins (`kill_monster_by`, `terrain_monster`); any animal killed otherwise pays nothing.
- **Snapshot.** A calm animal is sent as Neutral with `FLAG.WILD`, in view only (like any Neutral). A boar with a grudge against the heroes is sent Hostile (without the flag), so it is an enemy to the client's rules; the client picks the animal look from the creature's kind, never from the side. Client: `isAnimalKind`, `FIGURES` for the four kinds, `showsBar`/`barName` (`game/barName.ts`: an animal has no name, and its bar shows only once it is hurt), `shotPassesThrough` (shots hit animals), `pickUseHint` (no Recruit cap). Art: lyaseek "Forest creatures", the Outline variant.
- **The cove's population.** `run/levels/dungeon.rs` spawns, after the knight (so every earlier id stays), the boar, the stag, three deer, then ten bunnies (`cove::ANIMALS` 15) at `cove::animal_spots`: tile centres 24 px from the trunk, from the animals' own seeded RNG (salt in `cove.rs`), so the run's random stream is unchanged. A small cove with fewer spots drops the bunnies first. The fingerprint tests were re-recorded for this (a separate commit, #227).
- **Known gaps (needed before a ranged or casting animal exists).** A Neutral-side creature's projectiles hit nothing (`impact`), and a hazard ability used by a Neutral creature reaches `unreachable!` in `run/abilities.rs`. No animal has either today.

**Monster knockback and terrain deaths (`run/mod.rs`).**
- `Monster` has `knock` (px/s), `sinking`, `burn_t` and `last_hit_by`. `knock_monster(mi, from, dist, str)` pushes a monster by weight (`push_dist`) straight away; bosses, airborne and sinking monsters are never pushed. A pushed monster slides (`slide_monster`: `Mover::Dash`, so only walls and solids stop it) and its own movement is skipped meanwhile.
- `terrain_monster` runs after the AI step, like `terrain_player`. A monster over a chasm or deep water starts sinking (`FALL_TIME` / `DROWN_TIME`, `Ev::Sink` with the monster's id, wind-up cleared); when the time is up, `kill_monster` runs once, with the kill credited to `last_hit_by`. Bosses are skipped. Lava burns monsters that are not lava walkers (`Mover::Demon`) with heat damage `LAVA_DAMAGE` every `LAVA_TICK`; a lava death gives no kill credit, but a burn tick (Burning, `from_effect` damage) that finishes a monster credits `last_hit_by`. A monster standing on ground it cannot walk (lava) walks out first (`ai::escape_terrain`, ignoring heroes). The client reuses the hero fall and drown animations for monster figures.
- Known gaps (deferred): `escape_terrain` has no fallback when no walkable tile lies within 3 tiles, so such a monster stays put; a wind-up is not cleared by `knock_monster` (only by sinking), so an attack may resume after the slide.

**Lag compensation.**
- **Players are rewound too (#224).** Each `Player` keeps a position history like the monsters' (`Player.history`, pushed every step; `Player::pos_ago`), so a hero's hit on a charmed hero (an enemy-side hero) is rewound by the attacker's view lag like a hit on a monster (melee arcs, dash contact, the fast-forward of shots). A charmed hero's own attacks are the server's: no rewind.
- Melee arcs, the dagger and dash contact test monster positions rewound by the client's `view_lag` (RTT/2 + its interpolation delay), up to 31 ticks.
- Player projectiles spawn fast-forwarded by RTT/2, at most 150 ms, through the same 4 px hit-checked steps as every tick (`advance_projectile`), so the fast-forward cannot skip an enemy. Every shot ends at the first wall or figure it meets: hero shots never pass through enemies, except a bodiless one that fully resists them (#191; an explosion still hits everyone in its radius).
- **Shot hitboxes:** player projectiles hit a monster when `Monster::shot_dist` is below its radius plus the shot's. Shots are drawn `SHOT_HEIGHT` (8) px above their ground point and figures stand up from their feet, so the hitbox is a capsule from the feet north to `EntityKind::body_height` (sprite height above the feet, measured from the atlas) minus `SHOT_HEIGHT` and the radius, plus the dragon's lift while airborne. Small figures stay (nearly) round; the demon is hit up to its horns. Movement, melee and explosions still use the round footprint.

**AI (`behaviour.rs`, `ai.rs`, `modules.rs`; #227).** A creature's behaviour is three layers, each answering one question, and `Run::step` hands every monster (after the fear and cannot-act checks) to `behaviour::tick`:
- **Role** (`behaviour.rs`, `Role`, derived by `role(&Monster)` and never stored): whom it fights and what it does with no foe. `Hunter` (Hostile enemies: see heroes and Friendly creatures, chase, search, return home, wander), `Escort` (companions: `companion::regroup` then `act`, fighting with the ordinary AI), `Wildlife` (animals: calm and wandering, or fleeing; see "Wildlife") and `Waiting` (the unrecruited knight, Neutral: does nothing). The order in `role`: `wild` first, then Neutral, then `companion`, else Hunter. A dash in progress carries on whatever the role (`modules::dash_step`).
- **Base AI** (`BaseAi` in `defs/enemies.rs`, run by `ai.rs`; `Monster.base_ai` set at spawn from `EnemyDef.base_ai`): how it moves and attacks. `Melee` (close in), `Ranged { preferred }` (keep that distance in px; use a melee ability when closed on) and `Stationary` (never moves). `Monster.wanders` (also from the def; true for a monster without a def) says whether it wanders near home with no foe: the Mimic is `Melee` with `wanders: false`. `ai::tick_monster` runs it from the monster's own fields. Which ability to use is picked from the kit: kit entry 0 is the main ability and entry 1 the alt (`Kit::first`; a test pins the order for every enemy type).
- **Chase.** `ai::chase_of(run, mi)` says how it walks toward a goal and `chase_step` does one step: `Chase::Pathfind` (A*, `path_toward`) for escorts (the wolf and the recruited knight, on purpose) and for figures wider than a tile, `Chase::Straight` (`move_toward`) otherwise.
- **Modules** (`modules.rs`, `AbilityDef.module: Option<Module>`): what an ability brings with it. `Module::Leap`, `Ambush` and `Minions`. The base AI asks the kit's modules **in kit order** at fixed points (`on_sight`, `on_act`); a hook returns true when its module claimed the tick, else the base AI goes on. `on_windup_end` and `on_dash_end` read the module of the ability that just ended, and `on_danger` looks for the Leap ability directly: `on_sight` (a foe may be in sight: Ambush), `on_act` (where the base AI would choose its attack: Minions run a cast, Leap starts a leap), `on_windup_end` (an ability's wind-up ran out: Leap starts the dash), `dash_step`/`on_dash_end` (a dash ticked or ended) and `on_danger` (the creature stands on a chasm, deep water or lava). Giving a creature the ability gives it the behaviour: no creature has a script of its own any more (the wolf had one).
- **Leap** (Pounce: 4 tiles, 300 px/s, 20 damage for the wolf). Used only when Pounce's cooldown has passed (`cds.ready`), against a foe in line of sight beyond the main attack's reach (+ 8 px) and within the leap's range, whose landing ground it can stand on (`leap_start`, then a wind-up, then the dash in `on_windup_end`). A leap that hurts its prey holds the next swing back one cooldown (`on_dash_end`); an attack dash that ends on ground it cannot stand on carries on to solid ground (`overrun`). **The rescue:** on a chasm, deep water or lava (not for a lava walker) `on_danger` leaps back to `Monster.last_safe` (`dash_back`), but only if Pounce is ready and the creature can act; an asleep, petrified or frightened creature falls, drowns or burns like any other. `Monster.last_safe` is kept for every non-boss monster by `terrain_monster`.
- **Rules by shape (`run/ai_shapes.rs`, #224).** When the chase uses an ability is decided once per `Shape`, for any creature's kit (a charmed hero, a boss, any monster), never by ability id. `worth_using(run, creature, ability, foe, main_ready)` is a `match` over **every** `Shape` with no `_` arm, and so are `reach_of` and the chase's alt rule in `ai.rs`: a new shape does not build until it has an AI rule. The rules: Swing (a foe within reach), Projectile (a foe seen within range and in line of fire; Charm counts), Blast (as Projectile, not in melee), Ring (a foe inside the radius; the main ability cooling or two foes), Dash (a foe within the dash's travel plus the foe's radius, `max(range, speed x duration + radius)`, with a free line and no chasm at the landing), GroundArea (aimed at a foe in range; Consecrate: a foe within its radius of the feet), SelfArea (a foe within `SELF_AREA_NEAR`, 96 px; it helps its own side as it always does), Buff (a foe near, not already coated), Summon (`minions::room` or the companion limit allows it and it sees a foe; the wolf is summoned when there is none or it is hurt), Breath and Gaze (never: they stay with their boss patterns, the arm exists on purpose). Test: every hero ability is used by a charmed hero in a small scene (`hero_brain::every_hero_ability_is_used_by_a_charmed_hero`), so a new ability of an existing shape is covered automatically. Charmed bosses and monsters whose alt ability is one of these shapes now use it in the chase.
- **Ambush** (`ambush.rs`, see below) and **Minions** (`minions.rs`) are the other two modules; their code did not change, they are only called through `on_sight` and `on_act`.

- **Wide figures:** `path_toward` (also used by the bosses' chase since boss halls have chasms) treats figures wider than a tile (the demon, the dragon) specially: the straight-line shortcut also checks lines offset by their radius against terrain (`collision::terrain_line`; walls are fine, figures slide along them), A* (`astar_clear`) charges extra for tiles next to anything they cannot enter, a waypoint they make no progress toward is skipped, and a boss with no path walks straight at the hero. The swoop still charges in a straight line and stops at a chasm edge.
- **Movers:** each monster has a `mover`: `Mover::Demon` for imps, chorts, summoners, summoned imps and the demon boss, `Mover::Incorporeal` for anything with the Phasing trait, `Mover::Enemy` otherwise. `move_toward`, `separate_monsters`, `astar` and summon spots use the walking rule for it, and `path_toward` only walks straight when `walk_line` (not just line of sight) is clear. So enemies never enter chasms or deep water, and only demons cross lava. The debug path to the boss is planned as `Mover::Enemy`, around all terrain.
- **Phasing (`Mover::Incorporeal`).** `Map::blocks` lets it through walls, closed doors and solid objects (it is blocked only by Void and the map edge), ignores terrain (no slowdown or harm from shallow water or lava, no sinking over a chasm or deep water: `terrain_monster` skips phasers) and `astar`/`walk_line` give it straight paths. It never rests in a wall: `Map::can_stop`/`can_stop_at` (and `ai::can_rest`, which also rejects a spot inside a solid object) decide where it may stop, wait or attack; it may hover over a chasm, deep water or lava. If it ends up somewhere it may not rest, `leave_wall` walks it to the nearest spot it may. It still body-blocks other figures (`separate_monsters`) and is never knocked back (`knock_immune`). `Mover::Incorporeal` is server-only: client prediction uses movers only for heroes.
- **Bodiless and phasing by default (#191).** `Traits.bodiless` (set by the Incorporeal race): `Run::passes_through(c, split)` is true for a bodiless monster whose final capped resistance is >= 1 for every damage type in the split (players: false for now). Such an attack passes through: no damage, on-hit effect, push or reveal; projectiles fly on and do not explode on it. `targets::Rule.passes: Option<DamageSplit>` gives `Skip::Bodiless` ("it has no body" in the rules log). One central guard in `hurt_monster` (before the keystone, reveal and everything else; effect ticks excepted) makes every other source of damage pass too, such as barrel blasts and hero-side hazard pulses; the barrel also skips the knock. The same check covers explosions, monster and hero dash contact and hero melee targets, and the wolf will not pick prey its bite passes through. Mixed damage is per type: a Physical + Magical hit does only its Magical part to a ghost (the wolf's bite is 20 % Magical, so it does hurt a ghost). `Run::solids_for(mover, except)` and `object_tiles_for(mover)` are empty for `Mover::Incorporeal` and are used by monster movement, separation and dashes, so any obstacle added later is passed without extra code. `Map::can_stop` for Incorporeal is `!blocks(.., Dash) && tile != Void`: a solid tile (wall, closed door, later columns) is passed but never a resting place. Client: an incorporeal figure never wades, splashes or sprays over water or lava (`splashes(flags)` in `entityAlpha.ts`, checked in `PhaserRunView.terrainState`).
- **Targets:** `Ai.target` is `Option<Target>`, `Target::Player(id) | Object(id) | Place(Vec2)`. Perception sets `Player` every tick. The barrel an enemy aims at lives in its `Windup` (`Windup.at: Option<Target>`), because perception would overwrite `ai.target`. `Place` has no user yet.
- **Enemies shoot barrels:** before a ranged attack of a non-boss enemy (`ai::barrel_near`), it looks for an unlit, resting barrel that it can see, within its range, within the blast radius of its target hero, and far enough from itself that the blast spares it (blast radius + its own radius + 8 px). It picks the one closest to the hero and winds up toward it. When the wind-up ends, it shoots the barrel if it is still there and unlit, else the hero. Bosses and melee enemies never do. Enemy shots stop at a barrel and hurt it.
- **Pathing around barrels (beyond the plan, added because big bosses got stuck):** `path_toward` walks straight only when `object_free_line` is clear, i.e. no solid object within the body's radius plus 6 px of the line. Otherwise it runs A* (`astar_clear`) with the tiles under resting barrels blocked (`Run::object_tiles`) and an extra cost of 8 for tiles next to a barrel (the "squeeze" cost; it applies to all monsters). So a chaser switches to A*, refreshed every 0.6 s, whenever a hero stands next to a barrel; that costs a little CPU and can make chasers slightly late there. Sinking barrels do not count (`WorldObject::blocks`).
- Perception: the nearest living hero or ally (the wolf) within `sight` and with line of sight. Enemies never target players they cannot currently see.
- States: `Idle` (wander near home, if `wanders`) → `Chase` (melee approach, ranged keep a preferred distance at INT 11+, see Smart behaviour) → wind-up → attack. Losing sight leads to `Search` (A* to last known position) and then `Return` (A* home).
- Wind-ups telegraph attacks: the client flashes the body red and shakes it. The hit lands only if the target is still seen.
- **Smart behaviour (#220).** INT decides what a creature does, in tiers, and no list of creatures or bosses is involved. `defs/smarts.rs` holds two knobs: where each tier starts (`SMARTS.tier_int` = INT 6, 11, 16, 21; below 6 a creature is very dumb) and which tier each behaviour needs (`SMARTS.needs`: Dodge tier 0 = INT 6, AdvancedDodge and KeepDistance tier 1 = INT 11). `SmartsDef::can(int, Smart)` is the only question asked; the INT is the creature's current total (`Monster.attrs`), so INT effects count. Other knobs: notice distance (`notice_px`, 80 px at DEX 10, +3% per DEX point; 80 and not 60 because an orc needs about 64 px to clear a bow arrow), reaction delay (0 s), safety margin (2 px), threat share (5% of current HP).
  - **Keep distance:** the ranged back-off (`BaseAi::Ranged { preferred }`, in `ai.rs`'s Chase state) happens only at KeepDistance (INT 11+). Below it a ranged creature holds its ground and shoots or strikes.
  - **Dodging** (`run/dodge.rs`). `dodge::tick` is called from `Run::step` right before `behaviour::tick` for every monster (all roles, so the wolf and charmed creatures too) and from `BossEncounter::tick` for the boss (`BossBehaviour::busy` says whether a script action, a windup, a cast or any Dragon flight phase is running). It returns true when the creature spent the tick stepping; `ai::count_cooldowns` keeps its attack cooldowns counting on those ticks. A dodge is cleared in the fear, sleep, stone, sinking and knockback branches. A shot is a threat when: (1) the creature's INT reaches Dodge; (2) it is not busy (windup, cast, dash, slam, channel; sleeping, stone, afraid, sinking and knocked-back creatures skip the AI anyway) and can move; (3) the shot is a foe's (same side rules as hits) and has not hit it yet; (4) `course_hit`: replaying the shot's real flight (the same stop and hit rules as `advance_projectile`, through `Monster::shot_dist_at`, the capsule of tall figures) would hit it; (5) it sees the shot (`perception::sees`: a fire bolt in the dark yes, an arrow only within darkvision); (6) the shot is within the notice distance; (7) it matters: the damage after resistances is at least the threat share of current HP, or, at AdvancedDodge, the shot carries an effect that `Run::would_take_hold`; (8) stepping clear is possible: the time to clear (distance to a sure miss, margin included, at `m.speed` x `speed_mul()`, plus the reaction delay) is below the shot's time to arrive and the step is free through the normal collision. `plan` picks the side of the creature's heading, with no heading the side with more room, else the other; for several shots it prefers the spot that clears all, else the soonest. The step is at walking speed through `collision::move_box`: no cooldown, no chance roll, no dash. A dodge in progress is redirected only by an earlier-arriving shot its spot does not clear. Fireballs explode at the aimed spot, so stepping off their path does not save from the blast (#237).
- **Minion abilities (#204).** The necromancer's kit is `[ShadowBolt, Strike, RaiseDead]`, the summoner's `[FireBolt, Strike, SummonImps]`. Raise Dead raises `RaisedSkeleton` and `RaisedArcher`; Summon Imps raises `SummonedImp` (drawn as a normal imp). `Ai.cast` is the wind-up (0.7 s, shown as `Anim::Windup`; the cast pose plays when it lands): the AI starts it when it sees a foe, the ability is off cooldown and `minions::room` is above 0; the cast lands at the foe's last known position, also when the foe is out of sight by then (`monster_use` refuses an ability with no room, so a full army spends no cooldown). Crowd control (Sleep, Frozen, Petrified, Fear, a knockback, sinking) cancels the wind-up; no cooldown was spent, so the caster starts again when it next sees a foe. Numbers: necromancer and summoner 2 per cast and 4 alive, the lich 3 and 5; the cooldown is `MINION_COOLDOWN` (12 s) scaled by the caster's INT (cooldown chain), so about 7 s at INT 17, 8 s at INT 15 and 5.5 s at INT 22. `minions::raise` takes the type with fewer living first (ties go to the melee skeleton) and puts the minions on a ring of 22 px, or on the caster's spot where the ring spot is blocked. `Ev::Raise.k` tells the client which effect to draw (1 fire for imps, 2 the lich's dark purple, 0 green).
- **Ogre (mini-boss):** `EnemyType::Ogre` (`KIND.Ogre` 22), radius 10 (wide, so its chase uses `path_toward` with clearance like the bosses instead of `move_toward`), 260 HP, slow. Its kit is `Strike` (the club, reach = weapon reach + body) and `GroundSlam` (the same def as the Barbarian's: radius 34 around the user, pushes away, hits no objects), chosen when two living heroes' centres are within the slam radius, or one while the club is on cooldown. The slam winds up 0.5 s of its own plus 0.5 s of the club's = 1.0 s (`Anim::Slam` 10); the client draws the telegraph ring from `animT` and `CONST.OGRE_SLAM_RADIUS`/`OGRE_SLAM_WINDUP`. At impact the server sends `Ev::Boom{k: 5}` (dust shockwave). It is not a boss (`is_boss` false): no boss bar, no force field; the client draws a wide HP bar over it, always visible.
- **Kits and choice:** an enemy's abilities are `EnemyDef.abilities` (ids) and `weapons`; `kit_for(KitSource::Enemy(t), &attrs)` (`server/src/sheet.rs`) resolves them at spawn into `Monster.kit`; `monster_numbers` re-resolves the kit from `Monster.base` whenever an Attribute drain changes the monster's Attributes, and `Monster.cds` (`Cooldowns`) keeps one cooldown per ability and per user. The AI picks which ability from the situation (a melee one in reach, a ranged one from at least `ALT_BOLT_MIN_DIST` (40 px) and beyond the melee reach), waits 0–0.3 × the cooldown on top so it is never faster than the ability, and uses it through `monster_use`. A wind-up is the ability's own plus the weapon's (hero weapons have 0); `Windup` remembers which ability it leads to; each attack keeps the other back for `ATTACK_GAP` (0.4 s). A cooldown starts when the ability is used, after its wind-up. Movement follows the main ability.
- **Demon dungeon:** `enemies::for_boss` (through `Level::enemy_type`) swaps types when `Run::new` spawns the dungeon's enemies: with the demon as boss, skeleton archers become imps, skeleton warriors chorts and necromancers summoners. The swap itself leaves the map alone; only the terrain theme differs per boss (lava or water, see §6), which can move a few spawns off the liquid.
- Minions are never tinted: raised skeletons and summoned imps look like the ordinary ones (the user's request).

**Incorporeal ambushers (`run/ambush.rs`; the Ghost #169, the Shade #170).** Both are Undead + Incorporeal enemies with the kit *Touch* (Swing, needs a Touch weapon, keyed on CHA, applies Weakened for the Ghost and Frail for the Shade) and *Vanish* (a Buff that gives the user Invisible; base cooldown 20 s, shortened by CHA with `Scaled::per`, counted only while visible). `ai.rs` hands the tick to `ambush::tick` for every monster whose kit holds Vanish:
1. Visible with a hero in sight and Vanish ready: it vanishes (never while `ai.windup` or `slam` is set, so an attack is not lost).
2. Invisible: it picks the straggler (`straggler`: the visible living hero furthest from the heroes' centre; one hero: that one; tie: nearest to it) and phases toward it through walls. With nobody in sight it waits, unseen, until Invisible ends.
3. Within Touch reach and where it may rest (`can_rest`, never inside a wall) it appears with a warning of `AMBUSH_WARNING` (0.5 s), then touches.
4. If it has not reached its target after `AMBUSH_MAX_INVISIBLE` (`defs/abilities.rs`, 5) seconds, or a hit reveals it early, Invisible ends and the ordinary AI fights with Touch; when Vanish is ready again the cycle repeats.
The spawns are the haunts: `dungeon/generate.rs` `place_haunts` (own random stream, salt `HAUNT_SALT`, so the map and the usual spawns of a seed do not move) gives each room or hall from depth `HAUNT_MIN_DEPTH` (0.35) a haunt with chance `HAUNT_CHANCE` (1 in 4). `enemies::haunt_for(boss)` says what appears: Ghost for the lich's dungeon, Shade for the demon's, nothing for the dragon's. All numbers are first guesses (label `balancing`): stats, HP, speed, weapon damage, the 20 s cooldown, the 5 s limit, the 0.5 s warning, XP 10 (Ghost) and 12 (Shade).

**Bosses (`bosses/`).**
- Interface: `trait BossBehaviour { spawn_extras, can_be_damaged, tick }` (no enrage: the demon fights the same all fight long), created by `bosses::create(BossId)` and held by the level's `BossEncounter`. During its tick the behaviour is taken out of the encounter (`behaviour: Option<…>`), so it can borrow `Run` mutably.
- A boss script chooses **which** ability, **when** and **where** (opening delays are script-local) through one path (`monster_use`, `bosses::use_in_pose` in code); it never sets damage, radius, push, speed, wind-up or cooldown: those come from the boss's `Kit` (`kit_for(KitSource::Boss(b), &attrs)`, re-resolved by `monster_numbers` from `Monster.base` on an Attribute change). Boss pushes use the boss's own STR.
- Boss HP is its base HP; it does not depend on the number of heroes (TODO 4b, 2026-10-19; before, `base × (0.5 + 0.5 × players)`, and enemies `× (0.75 + 0.25 × players)`).

| Boss | Base HP | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Demon | 1300 | `Strike` with *Demon Claws* (cleave, arc 2.1), `Swoop` (a long dash on foot, each foe hit once), `RingOfFire` (16 fire orbs). Each attack's animation (`Melee`, `Dash`, `Cast`) starts with its wind-up and runs on through a short recovery (the strike frames play out); the timings are `DEMON_*` in `defs/bosses.rs`, shared with the client. No enrage. |
| Lich | 900 | Spawns `LICH_DISCIPLES` (6) disciples in a ring. Immune while a disciple lives. `GloomBolts` (a fan of 5, `KIND.GloomBolt` 60). `RaiseDead` (3 per cast, 5 alive, `MINION_COOLDOWN` 12 s scaled by its INT 22, about 5.5 s, `Ev::Raise{k: 2}`): raised skeletons and `RaisedArcher`s (drawn as a skeleton archer, 20 HP, 2 XP), half and half; the disciples do not count towards the limit. It is the first in the order of choice, after its opening delay `LICH_RAISE_FIRST` (8 s). `Nova` on its kit cooldown (10 s base, about 4.5 s at INT 22) plus `LICH_NOVA_EXTRA_WAIT` (a random 0–2 s wait of the script, regardless of distance): `Anim::Slam` while it winds up (purple telegraph ring), then `Ev::Boom{k: 6}`, 24 damage and a push by the lich's own STR 10 (barely anything) for every hero within the Nova's radius. Order of choice: Raise Dead, nova, gloom bolts. No drain. |
| Dragon | 2000 | `FireBreath` (channelled cone, burning patches), `Bite` (*Dragon Bite*, narrow arc straight ahead, own cooldown; chosen before the claw when ready), `Strike` (*Dragon Claws*, wide arc), `TailWhip` (*Dragon Tail*, a swing behind it, physical), `Fireball` (the Wizard's def, one ball per use, by the dragon's INT, push 20 with its STR), and `Smash` on landing. After each strike a short recovery (the strike frames play out). Flight every 10–12 s (`DRAGON_FLIGHT_EVERY`): `Anim::TakeOff` 0.5 s standing still, then 3.5 s airborne (`Monster.airborne`, `FLAG.AIRBORNE`; melee and dashes skip it; only the breath; chases with `path_toward`), then `Anim::Land` 0.6 s and the Smash (`Ev::Boom{k: 5}`); take-off and landing flight are not abilities. Attack anims (`Melee`, `Bite`, `Tail`, `Breath`) start with the wind-up; the client shows the wind-up frame for `DRAGON_*_WINDUP` s. |

## 6. Dungeon generation (`dungeon/generate.rs`)

The map is 140×140 tiles of 16 px. Tile ids: Void 0, Floor 1, Wall 2, DoorOpen 3, DoorClosed 4, ShallowWater 5, DeepWater 6, Chasm 7, Lava 8, Trunk 9, Road 10, Thicket 11, Barrier 12.

**Tile rules (`dungeon/mod.rs`, mirrored in `client/src/sim/map.ts`).** There is no single "solid" rule any more:
- `opaque`: Void, Wall, DoorClosed, Trunk and Thicket (not Floor, Road, DoorOpen, Barrier or terrain). Blocks sight (FOV, `line_of_sight`) and projectiles. Terrain is never opaque. `hard_opaque` is `opaque` without Trunk and Thicket (the soft blockers: opaque but not hard-opaque): light passes the soft ones, see §7 "Soft blockers".
- `blocks(x, y, mover)`: opaque tiles and Barrier tiles block everyone. Deep water blocks every mover but `Dash`; chasms block `Enemy` and `Demon` (heroes may step in, and fall); lava blocks only `Enemy`.
- `speed_factor(pos, mover)`: `SHALLOW_SPEED` 0.7, `LAVA_SPEED` 0.4 (1 for demons), 1 for a dash. `step_move` takes it from the tile under the hero's centre at the start of the step.
- `Trunk` (#202, a tree's trunk: the cove's big tree, the woodland's trees): opaque but not hard (#203), so sight, line of sight and shots stop at it like at a column while light passes; it is not terrain. It blocks every mover but `Incorporeal` (walking and dashing; the user's decision), and an Incorporeal mover can pass it but not stop on it (`can_stop`). It is not `safe`; in the cove its 8 neighbours are always plain floor (the cove gets no terrain), checked by `cove_trunk_cuts_nothing_off`; the woodland drops a tree whose trunk would cut off walkable ground.
- `Road` (#203): a dirt road, open ground like Floor: not opaque, `safe`, speed factor 1.
- `Thicket` (#203): a hedge of undergrowth, a wall for every mover: opaque but not hard (blocks sight and shots, passes light), not `safe`.
- `Barrier` (#259): an invisible wall, the end of the playable part of an open map (the woodland). It blocks every mover, `Dash` and `Incorporeal` too (a phaser treats it like the map's edge); it is not opaque, so sight, light and shots pass (a shot flies on until its range ends); not `safe`. The client draws it as the ground of the tile west of it (`groundOf` in `woodland/ground.ts`, else the east one), so the wall column repeats its neighbour column and no seam shows. The parity fixture (`export.rs` `fixture_map`) holds a Barrier strip.
- `safe`: Floor, DoorOpen and ShallowWater, the ground a hero can stand on unharmed. Generator reachability (`bfs`) and `last_safe` use it.

The generator is seeded (ChaCha8) and deterministic:
1. **Placement:** built in a canonical orientation. The start room (8×8) is at the bottom center and the boss hall (26×20) at the top center. Right after the start room comes **the nymph's cove** (#202): a dedicated room of the boss hall's size (26×20, `BOSS_W`×`BOSS_H`), always one per dungeon (the user's decision). Its position is drawn from a clone of the generator rng on stream 14 (`cove_rng`), so the main rng makes no extra draws for it: any x from 3, y in the ordinary-room band (below the hall's band), at least 3 tiles from the start room, up to 100 tries (generation fails and retries if none fits). The same stream then gives the cove's seed (1 to 1 000 000) for the client's plants and pixies. It is pushed as `RoomKind::Cove` before the other rooms, so they keep clear of it. Because the cove is a room, the connections in step 2 reach it like any other. Existing seeds get new maps (the user's decision); the six recorded hash tests were re-recorded once on purpose. Rooms (6–10) and halls (12–18 × 11–15) are rejection-sampled below a band that keeps them away from the hall.
2. **Connections:**
   - a minimum spanning tree plus up to 6 extra loop edges, carved as 3-wide L corridors
   - the boss hall gets a single corridor with a 3-tile entrance (DoorOpen tiles, sealed later)
   - one-tile wall stubs left by overlapping carves (a cell with floor on three sides and around both corners between them) are filled with floor; they would draw as T junctions, and filling them opens no new path
   - walls are placed on every void cell next to floor
3. **Validation, retried on failure:** every room is reachable (BFS), and the boss hall is the farthest area by path distance, with a margin of 4 tiles.
4. **Population:** enemies scale with path depth. Necromancers appear only in halls. The cove gets no enemy (it is peaceful, the user's decision; its wild animals are spawned by `run/levels/dungeon.rs`, see §5 "Wildlife"), and no chest, barrel, terrain or ogre either (steps 4, 5 and the ogre skip `RoomKind::Cove`, as do the haunts).
   **Chests:** `place_chests` gives about one in three rooms and halls a chest on its top row (enemies never spawn there), against the wall, with floor on both sides and below so it never blocks a corridor mouth. A quarter are mimics. Chests use their own random stream (a clone of the generator rng on stream 7), so adding them did not change the maps of existing seeds.
5. **Terrain:** `place_terrain` runs on its own stream (8), like the chests. The theme (`Theme::for_boss`: lava for the demon, water otherwise) is passed into `generate(seed, theme)`; `DungeonLevel::build` picks the boss before generating. In ordinary rooms (40 %) and halls (two tries at 60 %) it places:
   - **pools:** ovals a tile away from the walls. Water pools are deep wherever all 8 neighbours are pool, so a shallow rim always surrounds the deep centre; lava pools are all lava
   - **chasm strips:** wall to wall across the room, 1 to `MAX_CHASM` (3) tiles wide so the Barbarian's dash clears them, with a 2–3 tile floor bridge

   A feature only covers floor, keeps two tiles from corridor mouths (open ground outside the room) and never covers a chest. It is undone if it cuts any safe ground off from the start or makes a room as far as the boss hall. Enemy spawns it covers move to the nearest free floor tile in the room. The start room stays clear. About 4 % of the floor becomes terrain.

   **Boss hall chasms** (`place_boss_chasms`, stream 9, every theme), so knockback matters in boss fights: one or two **wall strips** (4–7 tiles long, 1–2 deep, along any wall) and one or two **pits** (2x2 to 3x2, three tiles from the walls). Nothing goes within 3 tiles of the boss's start or near the entrance (the bottom centre, 4 tiles to each side, 5 deep). Pits keep 3 tiles from other chasms (the dragon is over two tiles wide), strips 2. A feature that cuts any hall floor off from the entrance is undone.
   **Lava pool in the demon's hall** (`place_boss_lava`, after the chasms on the same stream, lava theme only, asked for by the user): one oval lava pool (as in the rooms, 4×4 to 7×6), kept off the boss's start and the entrance like the chasms, with a tile of floor between it and any chasm, and undone if it cuts hall floor off from the entrance (up to 100 tries). The demon crosses it unharmed and at full speed (`Mover::Demon`); heroes knocked in are slowed and burn.
   **The ogre** (`place_ogre`, stream 10, after the terrain): one per dungeon, in a random ordinary room or hall at depth ≥ 0.35 (any ordinary room if none fits), on a tile with safe ground in all 8 neighbours and no chest. It is pushed to `spawns`, so it rotates with them. Stream 10 is a clone, so maps, spawns and chests of existing seeds are unchanged.
   **Barrels** (`place_barrels`, stream 11, last, a clone of the generator rng, so the maps, spawns, chests, terrain and ogre of existing seeds are unchanged): about one in four ordinary rooms and halls (at least 6×6) gets a group of 2–4 barrels in a 2×2 block, away from the walls, on tiles with plain floor all around (no wall, door, water, chasm or lava next to them), and not within a tile of an enemy spawn, a chest or another group. The boss hall gets 3–5 single barrels, each with a 7×7 area of plain floor around it (so a wide boss can always pass), outside `hall_keep_clear` (the boss's start and the entrance) and at least 4 tiles from the next barrel. A barrel or group is only placed if, with its tiles counted as walls, no safe tile is cut off from the start that was reachable before. With few valid spots a hall can get fewer than three barrels (best effort). They end up in `Layout.barrels`, and `Run::new` spawns them as objects.
6. **Rotation:** a random quarter turn, so the start can be on any side and the boss hall is on the opposite side.
7. **Torches** (`place_torches`, stream 12, **after** the rotation, so a "face" is a brick face on screen; a clone of the generator rng, so the maps, spawns, chests, terrain, ogre and barrels of existing seeds are unchanged; `level_setup_is_unchanged` leaves torches and their ids out):
   - **Candidates:** a wall cell with floor directly below and no floor left or right (`Face`), or with wall above and below and floor to one side (`West`/`East`, side sconces). Doorway jambs and corners never qualify (the "no floor left or right" test on `Face` is what rules out jambs).
   - **Spacing:** each straight run of candidate cells (per room or corridor stretch, side and line) gets a torch about every `TORCH_SPACING` (6) tiles from an unbiased random offset (`gen_range(0..TORCH_SPACING.min(len))`); runs under 3 cells get none; no two torches touch.
   - **Dark pockets:** each room and each corridor stretch (a connected piece of corridor floor between rooms, found by flood fill) is left without torches with probability `DARK_SHARE` (0.2).
   - **Never dark:** the start room and the boss hall are never dark, and always get at least one torch: if the normal rules give them none, a fallback puts one in the middle of their longest candidate run, whatever its length.
   - The result is `Dungeon.torches` → `Layout.torches` as `TorchSpawn { wall, side }`, and `Run::new` turns them into torch objects.
8. **Sunlit rooms** (`place_suns`, stream 13, after the torches, its own clone of the generator rng, so nothing else generated moves; #156). A level gets 0, 1 or 2 sunlit rooms with weights 25 / 50 / 25 (`SUN_ROOM_WEIGHTS`). A candidate is any room except the boss hall and the cove (the start room is allowed) whose floor `rect` is at least `SUN_MIN_ROOM_W` x `SUN_MIN_ROOM_H` (6 x 8) and has a floor tile that `sun_tile_ok` accepts: at least `SUN_MARGIN_LEFT` (2) tiles from the left wall (the light comes from the left), `SUN_MARGIN_FAR` (1) from the right and bottom walls, and `SUN_MARGIN_TOP` (4) below the top row of the rect (five rows below the top wall). The sun tile is a random qualifying tile. With probability `SUN_SECOND_CHANCE` (1/3) the room gets a second sun on a qualifying tile 3 to 5 tiles (`SUN_SECOND_DIST`, Euclidean) from the first, if there is one. If fewer fitting rooms exist than the roll asks for, it places what fits (possibly none); in practice about a quarter of the sunlit rooms end up with two suns. The result is `Dungeon.suns` → `Layout.suns`, a flat `Vec<(i32, i32)>` (0 to 4 per level). `Run::new` calls `Run::add_sun(x, y)` for each: a static `Daylight` light on that floor tile (`add_sun` is also the piece for hand-designed rooms). **Torches in a sunlit room start out** (`TorchSpawn.lit` false for every torch whose `front()` lies in the rect of a room that holds a sun; `spawn_torch` calls `start_light_off`, so they go out in `RunStartInfo.lights` as off); they can still be lit with E or fire, and the daylight stays on.
9. **The cove** (`dungeon/cove.rs` `finish_cove`, after `place_suns` and before the torches in sunlit rooms are put out; #202). The room is dressed after the rotation (it is 20×26 when turned):
   - **Trunk:** the tile at the room's centre (`rect.x + w / 2`, `rect.y + h / 2`) becomes `Tile::Trunk`. The room is plain floor, so nothing else in it needs checking; `cove_trunk_cuts_nothing_off` checks that all 8 neighbours are safe and that no safe tile is cut off from the start.
   - **Suns:** 5 (the user's decision), appended to `Dungeon.suns`. The first is on the trunk tile (the sun margins apply, the tile type does not, as it becomes the trunk; if the margins fail, the nearest `sun_tile_ok` tile). The other four are at the quarter centres (`rect.x + w * fx / 4`, `rect.y + h * fy / 4` for fx, fy in 1 and 3), each moved to the nearest `sun_tile_ok` tile, and dropped if within `COVE_SUN_GAP` (3) tiles of an earlier sun. So the room normally has 5 suns, and torches in it start out like in any sunlit room.
   - **Record:** `Dungeon.cove` / `Layout.cove` is `Cove { rect, tree, seed }`; `Run::new` sends it at run start as `RunStartInfo.cove` (`CoveInfo { x, y, w, h, tree_x, tree_y, seed }`, tiles). Other level types have none.
