The test `guarantees_hold_over_many_seeds` checks 200 seeds (both themes), including chest placement (on floor against a wall, outside the start room and boss hall, not on an enemy), that chests and mimics actually appear, and the terrain guarantees: no safe tile cut off, spawns and chests on floor, no terrain in the start room, only chasms (and some) in the boss hall plus a lava pool in every lava-theme hall (none elsewhere), with its centre and entrance clear, only the theme's liquid, every terrain kind present, and a terrain share of 2–15 %.

### The woodland generator (`dungeon/woodland.rs`, #203)

`generate(seed)` is seeded (ChaCha8 on `seed ^ WOODLAND_SALT`) and builds a 188×46 map that starts as Thicket: the playable stretch of 140 columns (`WOODLAND_PLAY_W`) with a run-out of 24 columns (`WOODLAND_RUNOUT`) at each end (#259). In the run-out the forest and the road go on, so the party stands on a long road, not in a box; a column of `Tile::Barrier` stops it (`WALL_W` = column 26, `WALL_E` = column 161; `playable_x(x)` is true strictly between them). The game camera stops at the map's edge (`setBounds` in the `PhaserRunView` constructor, for every level), so no screen sees where the run-out ends. Every number is a first guess (`balancing`). Columns below are given within the playable stretch (add 24 for the map column).
1. **Road:** a centre line `centre(x)` = the map's middle plus two sines, clamped to rows 12 to 33, and a 3-tile-wide Road along it, from end to end of the map.
2. **Walkable band:** Floor within 11 ± 3 tiles (smooth noise) of the line, inside a 3-tile Thicket border above and below; it runs to both ends of the map.
3. **Clearings:** six ellipses (radii 4 to 6 by 3 to 5) spread along the playable stretch, 7 to 10 tiles off the line, alternating above and below, carved as Floor. The start is a 10-column rect from column 3; the end is an ellipse (7×6) at column 131 whose playable part's last 14 columns are the `end` rect (the ellipse runs on past the eastern wall). Floor the road cannot reach turns back to Thicket.
4. **Roadside trees:** candidates are open Floor tiles with open neighbours, outside the start and outside every clearing's and the end's **ellipse** (not their bounding rects). A tree is dropped when its foot is within 3 tiles of the road's centre line, and half of those within 3 to 5 tiles. Kinds: round 28 %, conifer 24 %, tall 19 %, small round 14 %, wide 10 %, bare 5 % (#279; a dead, leafless tree, only by the road, never on the edge); spacing 2.0 to 3.2 tiles; three passes. Each has a fresh seed and a scale of 80 to 120 % (small rounds 50 to 65 %).
5. **The edge:** trees and big bushes on the Thicket up to four tiles from walkable ground, denser near it. Edge plants take their (seed, scale) from `EDGE_SEEDS`, 12 fixed pairs per kind (indexed by the wire id, so it has a row for bare too, which the edge never uses), so the client draws at most 60 distinct edge looks. No Thicket tile beside the ground is left without a plant within two tiles.
6. **Bushes on the ground:** one per about ten walkable tiles, 75 to 150 % (24 to 48 px wide: `scale_pct` is the drawn width, 32 px at 100 %), never on the road line.
7. **Trunks:** each tree's foot tile becomes `Tile::Trunk`; a tree whose trunk would cut off walkable ground is dropped, until none does. Then every `safe` tile of the two wall columns becomes `Tile::Barrier` (last, so the reachability passes before it see one band from end to end and keep the run-out's forest).
8. **Spots:** the party spawns on the road at the start. Nothing lives in the run-out (animals and their group members only on playable columns; the clearings are all inside). Up to four of the six clearings get a group of 2 to 4 enemies (goblin archers, orc warriors, orc archers), 21 tiles or more from the spawns. The animals are seven groups on the walkable ground, 6 tiles apart: three of 3 to 5 bunnies, two of 2 to 3 deer (one with a stag), two single boars. No spot is within 1.5 tiles of a trunk. The result is `Woodland { plants, start, end, clearings, animals, seed, sun }`; its `seed` (1 to 1 000 000) is for the client's grass, flowers and critters. `Layout.daylight` is true; there are no chests, barrels, torches or suns.
9. **The run's sun (#277):** `Sun { side, elevation_deg }`: `side` is -1 (left) or 1 (right), `elevation_deg` is rolled uniformly in `SUN_ELEVATION` 35..=70 (first guess, `balancing`). It rolls from its own `ChaCha8Rng` on `seed ^ SUN_SALT`, after everything else, so the map, plants and spawns of a seed do not move (`level_setup_is_unchanged` stays green). It travels as `WoodlandInfo.sun: SunInfo { side, elevation }` and only drives the client's light and shadows; the simulation never reads it.

The tests in `woodland.rs` run over many seeds: the same seed gives the same forest and the same sun, the sun is in range, a pinned fingerprint of the tiles, plants and spawns of five seeds (the sun is not in the hash, so a change that lets the sun move the map breaks it), the road reaches every playable floor tile and nothing beyond the walls, the border above and below is Thicket, the road runs on past the walls (road at both map ends, a barrier where the road crosses each wall column, no open tile left in it, start, end and every creature inside, a forest in the run-out), trunks are the roadside trees, nothing stands on the road, the counts are in range, the start is clear and creatures keep their distance from trunks. `bare_trees_grow_by_the_road_not_on_the_edge` and `plant_kind_ids_match_the_client` (the `PlantKind` ids against the client's `PLANT_KINDS` order) came with #279, which re-pinned the map fingerprint (the bare roll moved the plants).

## 7. Field of vision and light

`fov.rs` is recursive shadowcasting with the viewer's vision in tiles (9 for every hero today); walls (`Map::opaque`) block sight but are themselves visible. Terrain does not block sight. Sight alone shows nothing any more: **lighting replaced the fog**. Heroes have no light of their own, so a tile is only seen when it is in sight **and lit** (see "Light sources", §5). Unlit tiles are fully black. Sight and light are used in three places:
- **The hero's view (`vispoly.rs`, client `sim/view.ts`, the same rule, pinned by the shared fixture):** what a hero sees is its `View` (since 2026-10-16), computed from the viewed hero's **pixel position**. Open tiles count when any of 16 sample points (a 4×4 grid, 4 px apart) lies inside the visibility polygon (see "The vision mask" below); walls count when the tile FOV sees them. So, from the same position, the server sends monsters on the floor the client shows, with no black wedges where the two drift apart behind a corner. `View::compute` runs the FOV and the polygon (one per player per snapshot) and tests only the open tiles in the polygon's bounding box. The point-in-polygon test uses a binary search over the ray angles (the polygon is star-shaped around the viewer). `Player.view` replaced `Player.fov`.
- **Server snapshots (`visibility.rs`):** `seen(pos)` means on a tile of the viewer's view **and** (on a lit tile (static or moving layer, `Lights::lit`, the same rule as `is_lit`) **or** within `dark_reach`, the viewer's vision × darkvision, measured from the viewer). It uses the viewed hero's sight, so a spectator gets the teammate's view and darkvision; large figures count as seen if any of their corners is. Monsters, enemy projectiles, hazards, world objects and positional events are only sent when seen. Teammates are always sent, and so are the party's own projectiles, the `Dmg` of their hits and the `Boom` of their explosions (sent without a position), so a shot into the dark flies on and shows its hit instead of vanishing at the edge of the view. The lich drain beams keep their "boss fight on" rule.
  - **Torches** (world objects that own a light, `owns_light`) need sight only (`object_sent`): a torch in the viewer's FOV is sent even if it is out or its tile is dark, one outside the FOV is not. The client's light state does not depend on the torch entities (it comes from `RunStartInfo.lights` and the light events), and a torch sprite outside the FOV sits under full black anyway.
  - **Hero events (`on_hero`):** `Run::event_on_hero` marks an event (hurt and heal numbers on a hero) by its index in the tick's event list. Those are sent where the hero is in sight, lit or not; every other positional event needs sight and light. The client draws numbers on heroes (`Dmg.p`, `Heal.p`) over the darkness (`numberDepth`, `DEPTH.numbers`), so they show even when the hero is unlit.
  - **The moving-light list (`Snapshot.lights`):** every lit moving light within the viewer's FOV radius plus one tile plus its own reach (`lights_for`: a distance test with no line-of-sight check), as `MovingLight(x, y, kind, colour?)`. It can include glows from behind walls (§13). The monster that carries a glow is only sent when it stands in seen space.
- **Client light pass (`LightField`, `LightView`):** replaces the fog canvas, see below.
- **Enemy perception:** `perception::sees` (above). **Behaviour change (2026-10-15):** humans (necromancer, summoner, lich disciples) do not see heroes in the dark; monsters (goblin and orc archers, orc warriors, mimic, ogre) only within a third of their vision (about 2.5 to 3 tiles); undead, demons and bosses see fully in the dark. A hero in light is seen as before. `simulation_is_unchanged_after_waking_the_boss` was re-recorded for this: in the lich hall the disciples stay idle in the dark and one orc loses the hero.

**Soft blockers and the soft shadow (#203).** Two rules, one per question.
- **Sight, line of sight and shots** use `Map::opaque`: walls, closed doors, trunks and thickets all stop them. `View::compute`, `fov.rs`, `vispoly.rs` and their client ports take a `Blocker`: `All` (every opaque tile) for what the hero sees and the server sends, `Hard` (`hard_opaque`) for light.
- **Light** uses `hard_opaque`: the static light's shadowcast (`run/light.rs`, `LightField`) passes trunks and thickets, so a tile behind a trunk stays lit, and the dungeon air (`Mood`, `Wind`) flows over trunks. The sun reaches ground a hero cannot see.
- **The soft shadow (`game/shademask.ts`, `ShadeMask`; its geometry `shadeCovers` is shared with the haze's soft level).** The client holds two views: `LightView.view` (hard: walls only; it drives darkness, tint, `seenLit` and the vision mask) and `LightView.sight` (all: what the server lets the hero see). Ground inside the hard polygon and outside the full one is dimmed with black at `SHADE` (0.3), never blacked out: the scenery (canopies, bushes, flowers) is drawn above it, while a creature standing there is hidden because the server sends none (so the shade can show ground with no creature on it; that is by design). On dark maps only (the cove; on daylight maps the vision mask's soft level replaces it, #278) the layer is an `Image` at `DEPTH.shade` (over the ground, under figures and canopies), drawn from a small canvas over the camera window (`RES` px per tile), redrawn only when either view or the window changes, and free when no soft tile is in the window. `PhaserRunView.frame` keeps it off until the scenery has settled (`WoodlandView.settled`, `woodland/settle.ts`): the ground is painted and no plant is pending in the camera window plus 2 tiles; once everything is drawn it stays on, before that a walk into undrawn trees switches it off again. Without the wait, wedges behind trunks that are not drawn yet would show as dark patches over bare ground.
- **Tests:** `hard_opaque_is_the_old_rule`, `shots_and_line_of_sight_stop_at_trunks`, `a_trunk_hides_a_monster_behind_it`, `a_creature_inside_a_trunk_tile_is_seen` (server); `sim.test.ts`, `view.test.ts`, `shademask.test.ts`, `visionmask.test.ts` (levels and `maskSteps` of the haze mode, `fillPolygon`, `blurField`, `fieldToRgba`, `canvasRes`), `settle.test.ts` (`plantTile`: every demo tree maps onto a trunk or thicket tile), `unseenfx.test.ts` (`maskTransform`, `hazeUniforms`) (client). The fingerprint tests passed unchanged (their scenarios never meet a trunk).
- **Tests of the woodland's depth (#277):** `the_sun_is_in_range_and_pinned_to_the_seed` and `the_map_is_unchanged_by_the_sun` (a pinned FNV-1a fingerprint of tiles, plants and spawns for seeds 1, 2, 3, 7 and 42) in `woodland.rs` (server); `shadows.test.ts` (direction and length of the shadow, solid raster without gaps at a low sun, contact ellipse, the foot, mirroring), `shadowlayer.test.ts` (`fits`), `vegetation.test.ts` (the shape does not change with the light, the cove tree's FNV pin guards against drift; it was re-recorded on purpose in #279), `demoMath.test.ts` (sun, shadow and haze steps) (client).

**The light pass (`game/light.ts`, `game/lightview.ts`, `game/gpulight.ts`, `game/lightshader.ts`).**
- **`LightField`** holds the static lights (from `RunStartInfo.lights` and the light events; `RunSession.onEvent` calls `set`, `add`, `remove` and, on `Ev::Tile`, `mapChanged`) and builds the same static light map as the server with the `sim/fov.ts` port. The shared fixture (`export.rs` → `fixtures.json`, checked by `light.test.ts`) pins the TypeScript map to the Rust one, so a tile the server counts as lit is never drawn fully black, and no light is drawn on a tile the server counts as dark. Colours mix additively: each source adds `colour × intensity × falloff(distance / radius)` per tile and channel, capped at 255 (torch plus lava gives orange, blue plus red purple). The falloff is a smoothstep from 1 at the centre down to `EDGE` (0.1) at the radius edge, never brighter further out, so a light fades out **inside** the tiles it lights. Moving lights from the snapshot are added each frame with the same falloff (`addMoving`): a radius-r shadowcast from the tile of the sent position (`Math.floor(px / TILE)`, the server's `light_tile`), the same tiles the server's moving layer marks; the shared fixture also pins these (moving lights in the open, beside a corner, against a wall and exactly on a tile corner).
- **The light pass is a fragment shader (#293; before it, two canvases filled on the CPU).** `LightView` works out the light per tile on the CPU every frame, as before (`accumulate`, `warmTint`, `tileDark`, `dvMask`); `GpuLight` (`game/gpulight.ts`) takes the result per tile and smooths it up to **2 px per tile** (`PIC_SUB`) on the GPU. The look is the same as the canvases had (the user approved it side by side). Its parts:
  - **Two data textures**, one texel per tile (`addUint8Array`, NEAREST): `lightdata` with `R,G,B` = the tint (`warmTint`) and `A` = the darkness (`tileDark × 255`), and `dvdata` with the darkvision strength in `R` (`A` = 255). `GpuLight.put` writes a texel (`packTile`, `packDv` in `game/lightshader.ts`); `lightdata` starts all dark. **`pma` is set to false** on both (`tex.source[0].glTexture.pma = false`, right after creating them): Phaser premultiplies a data texture's RGB by `A` on every upload, and our `A` is the darkness, so a bright lit tile (`A` near 0) would lose its tint and come out black. `WindowUpload` and a context restore both honour the wrapper's `pma`.
  - the **darkness** (alpha of `lightdata`): 1 on every tile not in the viewer's FOV or not lit; on lit tiles in the FOV it is `1 − max(brightness, MIN_LIT)`. `MIN_LIT` (0.08, about the falloff's edge) keeps a lit tile from ever being fully black while its rim really fades.
  - the **tint** (`R,G,B` of `lightdata`; the warm light, #148): each tile's summed light colour becomes a multiplier (`warmTint` in `lightview.ts`): per channel `255 * (1 - s * (1 - c / max))` with `s = WARM (0.45) * min(1, max / 255)`, `c` the channel of the summed colour and `max` its brightest channel. Near a torch, stone and figures keep their own colours and turn warmer, never paler (the old additive tint washed them out). Tiles that are not seen and lit get white (no change); a dark tile is black in the picture anyway (the blackening below). `LightView.light` (a getter: the per-tile summed light colour as a `Float32Array`, 3 values per tile) and `LightView.seenLit` (a `readonly Uint8Array`, 1 per tile, 1 when the tile is seen and lit) give `Mood` its read-only view of the light.
  - **Step 1, the picture:** a Phaser `Shader` game object (source `lightFrag(dv)`, the pure string in `lightshader.ts`) renders into a render texture (`setRenderToTexture('lightpic')`), `w·2 × h·2` px (the dungeon map is 140×140 tiles, so 280×280 px; the woodland 188×46, so 376×92 px). Per picture pixel it reads 4×4 taps of the data texture with the **Catmull-Rom** weights, the same polynomials as `catmullRom` in `lightview.ts` (a unit test, `lightshader.test.ts`, compares the GLSL with them), clamps the sum to 0..1 and outputs `tint × (1 − darkness)`: one pixel per tile with LINEAR filtering is plain bilinear, which showed creases along the tile grid in the long fade (screenshots, 2026-10-15). A tile whose own darkness is 255 (out of sight or unlit) is **blackened** in the shader: output black, so the fade stays on the lit side of the boundary (with the cubic taps alone the centre of a dark tile next to a bright one got 10 to 13 % light, more than the dimmest lit rim, and read as lit floor where no monster is sent). The shader compares `darkness ≥ 254.5/255`, so on the GPU a darkness of 254.5 or more counts as dark (the byte rounds up to 255); this can only make a tile darker, never lighter. A second Shader, `dvpic`, does the same for `dvdata` (only the red channel, no blackening) and is the mask that `DarkvisionFX` samples (`LightView.mask`).
  - **Step 2, the Image:** `lightpic` is drawn as one `Image`, scaled ×8 (`TILE / PIC_SUB`) with LINEAR filtering (`setFilter` after `setRenderToTexture`: a render target is NEAREST under `pixelArt`), MULTIPLY blend, at `DEPTH.fog` (7000). Because the picture already holds `tint × (1 − darkness)`, one multiply does what the old tint canvas (just below the darkness) and the old darkness canvas did. It is an `Image` and not the `Shader` itself because a `Shader` has no blend mode; and it sits in the normal depth order, not in a camera post pipeline, so what is drawn above `DEPTH.fog` (sound text, hero bars, numbers, the vision mask) stays unlit.
  - **The Shaders are drawn by hand, not from the display list.** `Shader.flush` ends with `renderer.setFramebuffer(null, false)`: it binds the screen, not the framebuffer a camera post pipeline has pushed. With `UnseenFX` (every woodland run) or `DarkvisionFX` (every hero with darkvision) attached, a Shader in the display list would make everything drawn after it in that frame bypass the effect's target and be painted over. So `GpuLight` creates each Shader, calls `setRenderToTexture` and `removeFromDisplayList()`, and draws it in `LightView.update` (`GpuLight.flush`; the scene's update runs when no framebuffer is pushed) with the same steps `setRenderToTexture` uses once (`pipelines.clear()`, `load()`, `flush()`, `pipelines.rebind()`) plus one guard, a scissor the size of the picture pushed around it (the current one is canvas-sized, so a picture larger than the canvas would be cut off). No blend guard is needed: `pipelines.clear()` already forces the NORMAL blend mode. The constructor draws once more after `setRenderToTexture` because Phaser's own first draw has no scissor guard and can fail outright (the `drawArrays` warning below: on a woodland-demo restart `UnseenFX.destroy` has left a vertex attribute enabled); the `pipelines.rebind()` after it repairs the state and our draw then fills the picture. Outside the display list the Shaders are not destroyed with the scene, so `GpuLight` destroys both on `shutdown` (that frees the program, the framebuffer and the picture); and because `addUint8Array` and `addGLTexture` return nothing when the key exists, the constructor first removes any `lightdata`, `dvdata`, `lightpic` or `dvpic` left by an earlier scene (a second run in one tab, a demo's `scene.restart`).
  - **WebGL 1 notes.** RGBA8 only, no `texelFetch`, so the taps read `texture2D` at texel centres. The data textures are not powers of two, so `setChannel0` must be given `{ minFilter: 'nearest', magFilter: 'nearest', wrapS: 'clamp_to_edge', wrapT: 'clamp_to_edge' }` (its default is `repeat`, which is incomplete on such a texture in WebGL 1 and samples black). `uTiles` (the map size in tiles) is a custom uniform and must be declared on the `BaseShader` (`setUniform` cannot add one). The GLSL writes map row r of the picture into texture row r (`flipY` of `setRenderToTexture` is unused in 3.90), so the `Image` and `DarkvisionFX` read the picture the way they read the old canvases. After a lost and restored WebGL context the light pass comes back (checked in `?cove`).
  - Known console noise, harmless, not worth another look: `INVALID_ENUM getFramebufferAttachmentParameter` from Phaser's own framebuffer destroy when a scene ends (WebGL 1); and one `INVALID_OPERATION drawArrays` on a woodland-demo restart, because `UnseenFX.destroy` leaves a vertex attribute enabled when Phaser makes its first draw in `setRenderToTexture` (see the constructor's extra draw above); our own draw follows and works.
- **The vision mask (`game/visionmask.ts`, `sim/vispoly.ts`):** the edge of sight is drawn as straight shadow lines, not along the tile grid (the user's request, 2026-10-16).
  - `visibilityPolygon` (pure, `vispoly.test.ts`) builds the area seen from the viewer's **pixel** position. Wall edges that face open tiles are merged into segments, and rays go to every segment end (± 1e-4 rad, so a ray grazing a corner continues behind it) and at 96 even angles. Each ray stops at the nearest segment or at the sight radius `sightRadius(vision)` = `(vision + 0.5) × TILE` px, so the outer edge is a circle covering the tiles the shadowcast reaches. It costs about 0.05 ms per polygon (measured in Node on the fixture map, vision 9). A ray tests only the segments of its *angular bucket* (#280, `bucketSegments`: 256 buckets around the viewer; a segment goes into every bucket its angle span touches plus one on each side, and into all of them if it passes within 1 px of the viewer), so the result is exactly that of testing every segment (pinned by a brute-force comparison in `vispoly.test.ts` and by the shared fixture). Without the buckets the cost grows with rays × segments: the woodland's full view (trunks and thickets block it) has about 200 segments and over 1000 rays and took about 2.2 ms per compute while walking; with them about 0.8 ms. The server's `vispoly.rs` still tests every segment (same result).
  - `LightView` keeps the viewer's `View` (`LightView.view`, the hero's view above). It recomputes it when the viewer moves (to 0.1 px), the rounded vision changes or the map changes (`mapChanged`, on `Ev::Tile`). Darkness, tint and darkvision use `seenForLight`: the view plus open tiles next to an open tile in view, so a tile the polygon only grazes (no sample point inside) stays lit and the darkness does not fade the edge into tile-shaped notches; the extra tiles lie under the mask apart from slivers under 2 px. The mask's edge therefore cuts through lit floor. Walls outside the FOV stay black. (`wide` off, E in `?lights`: the tile FOV only, the old edges.)
  - `VisionMask` draws the view's polygon in white into a canvas over the camera window (plus 2 tiles a side) at **8 px per tile** (haze mode: 4, `canvasRes`, #280), plus every visible wall tile whole (the polygon stops at a wall's face, so it would cut off brick faces and rims). On dark maps a Phaser `Shader` game object at `DEPTH.fog + 0.5` draws black with alpha `1 − mask`. Outside the polygon the canvas is grey, not black, by `VisionMask.outside` (the strength beyond sight: 1 on dark maps; on daylight maps it is `DEFAULT_HAZE.outside` and no shader object is made, `UnseenFX` reads the canvas instead, #278). On dark maps the edge is crisp (only the canvas's antialiasing): a blurred fade was tried and made the shadow behind a pillar look too wide. On daylight maps it fades (the feather, below). A game object and not a camera post effect, so the drain beams and the numbers above the darkness stay visible. The canvas is redrawn and uploaded when the window origin or the view's `version` changes, so while the hero walks that is nearly every frame (a canvas of about 360×224 px; on dark maps not measured live, the haze-mode cost is measured below). The window only grows.
  - Out of sight is pure black on dark maps (the dungeon, the cove). A dim map memory and a fog look were demoed and rejected (2026-10-16).
  - **The unseen by map kind (#278, decided 2026-11-08).** What the hero cannot see looks different on the two kinds of map, so that dark always means shadow and pale always means "something could be hiding here". **Dark maps** (dungeons, the cove): unchanged. Beyond sight is black (`VisionMask.outside` 1), and behind the cove tree's trunk the `ShadeMask` dims with black at `SHADE`. **Daylight maps** (`RunStartInfo.daylight`, the woodland): the unseen is **haze**, never darker: the scenery is still drawn (#258), washed toward a pale grey-blue, desaturated and slightly blurred. No haze in dungeons: the fog look for lit tiles behind walls was rejected on 2026-10-16 and would reveal the layout.
  - **Haze mode of the mask (`VisionMask.haze`).** The canvas holds three levels as the unseen strength `u` (white 0, black 1; the shader reads `1 - red`): seen 0, soft (`VisionMask.soft`, behind a trunk or thicket: inside the hard polygon, outside the full one) and outside (`VisionMask.outside`, beyond sight). `levels(outside, soft)` turns strengths into canvas greys; `maskSteps(haze, levels)` lists the drawing steps over the outside grey, in order: the hard polygon in white (`sight`), the visible hard walls whole (`walls`), the blur (`feather`), the wedge and the seen plants. In haze mode the first three are built in memory, not on the canvas (#290): `sight` is rasterised by `fillPolygon` into a float grey field the mask owns (a scanline fill with 4 sub-scanlines per row and exact horizontal coverage, so an edge pixel goes toward white by the share of it the polygon covers, like a canvas fill), `walls` fills whole tile cells of it, and `feather` blurs it (`blurField`), turns it into RGBA (`fieldToRgba`) and writes it with `putImageData`; the wedge and the seen plants are then drawn on the canvas as before. Nothing is read back from the canvas. The *wedge* (inside the hard polygon, outside the full one) is drawn after the blur, so its sides are crisp: the feather softens the edge of sight only (user, 2026-11-08: "the trunk vision block should be at feather 0"). It is a *ramp* (#278, Task 11; user feedback 3: the haze looked detached from the trunk and ended in a hard edge at the rim): the haze grows out of the trunk, from nothing at its near face to `soft` at `VisionMask.ramp` px behind it (`DEFAULT_HAZE.ramp` 24, about one tile, approved by the user), in `RAMP_LAYERS` = 4 steps. `rampLayers(softGrey, ramp, 4)` gives the layers, lightest first: layer k is everything at least `k * ramp / 4` px behind the full polygon at the grey `soft * (k + 1) / 4` of the way from white; with `ramp` 0 one layer at the full soft grey from depth 0 (the old crisp wedge). Each layer is drawn with `darken` (it never lightens) over the complement of the full polygon grown by its depth (`extendPoints`: every point moved outward along its ray from the hero; the canvas rectangle plus that polygon, even-odd), all inside a clip: the hard polygon with its rim points (the rays that reached the radius, `extendRim`, within 1 px of `View.r`) pushed out by `max(TILE, 3 * feather)` px, so the blur's whole transition lies inside it. Inside sight the wedge is therefore crisp-sided and fades in from the trunk; past the rim the pixel is `min(blurred fog, soft grey)`, which follows the fog down to the outside grey, so there is no step at the radius arc (the earlier `hard ⊕ full` wedge ended exactly on the arc where the fog is about half-way, a visible edge). `View.r` (the sight radius in world px of the last `compute`) is new for this; `isVisible` and the polygon are unchanged (server parity). soft 0 is white, so no haze behind blockers. The earlier *blockers* step (every trunk and thicket tile the full view touches painted whole in white) is gone: it made the wedge start at the tile's far edge at full strength, detached from the tree; the ramp leaves the ground at the trunk's foot nearly clear by itself and the seen-plant silhouettes keep the tree's picture clear. *Touched* (`touches` in `sim/view.ts`, now used by the seen plants only) = `View.isVisible` (the tile shadowcast) OR any of 12 points 0.5 px outside the tile's border (at 2, 6, 10 and 14 px along each edge, corners left out so a diagonal neighbour's open ground cannot count) lies inside the full polygon. Why both: the tile shadowcast starts at the hero's tile centre and the polygon at the exact pixel, so where a nearer trunk hides part of a tile, or at a grazing angle, the hero sees ground right against a trunk that the tile test calls hidden, which hazed the roots and the tree's picture although the hero saw them (the user's screenshot). So *touched* means "the hero sees ground against a side of the tile", not "sees its face": a trunk partly behind another one can count as seen when the hero stands near the edge of their row, and its whole tree is then unhazed (the canopy reaches over ground the hero sees). `isVisible` itself is unchanged (server parity); `touches` is a client look only. It uses the full view, not the hard one: a trunk exactly behind another (in line, or walled in) is in the hard view but is not touched, and the hard view would punch an unhazed square into the wedge. In haze mode the mask creates no black `Shader` game object; it exposes `texture` and `rect` (the canvas's world rectangle) for the effect instead.
  - **A soft edge and seen plants (#278, user feedback 2026-11-08).** Two fixes in the haze mode. (1) *Feather:* after the polygon fills the mask is blurred (`blurField` in `visionmask.ts`: three separable running-sum box passes over the in-memory grey field, radius in canvas px = `round(feather * RES / TILE)`, at least 1; no `ctx.filter`, which not every phone browser supports), so the edge of sight fades over about three times the radius. `VisionMask.feather` (world px, 0 = hard) is read from `HazeLook.feather` (`DEFAULT_HAZE.feather` 24, picked by the user) and is part of the redraw key. (2) *A seen plant is never hazed:* `UnseenFX` is a camera effect and the mask is in ground coordinates, so a tree's picture (drawn upward from its foot) was hazed wherever it reaches over hazed ground behind it, and the haze appeared to start at the trunk instead of behind it. The old `ShadeMask` was a game object under the canopies, so it never had this. `VisionMask.seen` (a hook set by the owner: `version()` and `paint(ctx, ox, oy, s, full)`) is called after the blur, so the silhouettes stay crisp, and its `version()` is in the redraw key. `WoodlandView.seenPainter` implements it: every shown plant whose image box meets the canvas window and whose own ground tile (`plantTile`: the tile its foot lies in, the server's `trunk_tiles`; for every tree of the demo seeds that is a trunk or thicket tile, so a foot on a tile boundary is not an issue) is *touched* by the full view (`touches(full, x, y)` in `sim/view.ts`, the predicate that decides whether a tile counts as seen, so a tile and its tree's picture always agree) is painted white (unhazed) at its world position as a cached white silhouette (the plant's page region drawn with `source-in` white; mirrored plants are flipped). Its version is a counter that changes when a plant is shown and when the plants are cleared. The mask redraws almost every frame while the hero moves, so the haze-mode canvas is made with a `willReadFrequently` 2D context (our own element, `textures.addCanvas`: CPU-backed, so `putImageData` is a plain copy, and the plant silhouettes, CPU-backed too, draw into it without a GPU round trip) and the field, the blur's second buffer, the `ImageData` and the raster's crossing lists are kept with the mask (sized with the canvas), so a redraw allocates nothing; `WoodlandView` keeps each shown plant's box so the paint loop culls before anything else. *Cost (#280):* the woodland dropped to about 50 fps while walking (34 at worst) on the user's desktop. Measured in `?woodland` (headless Brave, software rendering, 1600×900, walking, about 60 redraws per run), one `VisionMask.update` including the full view it reads (`LightView.sight`, computed lazily on the first read): 5.0 ms before; 3.8 ms with the polygon's angular buckets (the full view 2.2 → 0.8 ms, see above); **2.2 ms** (p95 about 3 ms) with the haze canvas at **4 px per tile** (`canvasRes(true)`, half the dark maps' 8: a quarter of the pixels for the fill, the blur, `putImageData` and the upload; the blur radius in canvas px halves with it, so the feather is the same in world px). The fog edge is blurred anyway; the wedge's sides and the seen-plant silhouettes are drawn at the coarser grid, smoothed by the LINEAR upload. *No readback (#290):* the blur used to read the canvas back with `getImageData` every redraw, and that waits for the GPU process: right after a woodland start, while the plant pages are uploaded each frame, one call took about 140 ms in the same headless measurement (the whole frame; 32 calls, 2.0 s of a 6 s walk), which is the likely cause of the low fps right after a woodland map loads. Building the field in memory removed it (the same walk: no mask readback; the remaining `getImageData` calls are one-off, 17 ms in all) with the same look (the before/after stills differ only in the moving clouds and insects). A GPU blur (in `UnseenFX`, with a second unblurred channel for the crisp wedge and silhouettes) stays the next step if the CPU redraw itself becomes too slow. Dark maps never set `feather` or `seen` and are unchanged.
  - **`UnseenFX` (`game/unseenfx.ts`).** A camera post pipeline (registered in `main.ts`, modelled on `DarkvisionFX`) attached to the main camera on daylight maps only. Per pixel, with `u` from the mask: blur by averaging taps within `u * blurPx`, desaturate by `u * desat`, mix toward `colour` by `u * mix`. `HazeLook` { outside, soft, colour, mix, desat, blurPx, feather, ramp } and `DEFAULT_HAZE` (outside 0.8 and feather 24, picked by the user on the phone 2026-11-08; soft 0.7, colour 0xb8c4cc, mix 0.45, desat 0.6, blurPx 1.5, ramp 24 (world px over which the haze behind a trunk grows from nothing to `soft`; 0 is crisp); all approved by the user in `?woodland` on the phone, 2026-11-08). `blurPx` is in world pixels (`hazeUniforms` scales it by the zoom). The mask's UV is an affine function of the screen coordinate prepared on the CPU in doubles (`maskTransform`): absolute world pixels in a mediump shader would step by 2 px at 2400+ px and stair-step the mask's edge. `syncUnseenFX(camera, on, texture, rect, look)` attaches, updates or removes the pipeline; a destroyed mask texture skips the effect for that frame. Each attach compiles a pipeline, so `PhaserRunView.frame` keeps it attached for the whole run on daylight maps.
  - **Wiring (`PhaserRunView`).** On a daylight map `visionmask.haze` is on and `UnseenFX` is always attached. Only the soft level waits for the scenery to settle (`WoodlandView.settled`): `visionMask.soft` is 0 until then and `DEFAULT_HAZE.soft` after, so wedges behind trunks that are not drawn yet do not haze bare ground, while the beyond-sight haze is there from the start. The pipeline itself is not toggled with `settled`: that would rebuild it and pop the haze. The `ShadeMask` is created in every run but is on only on dark maps (`!daylight && settled`). `DAYLIGHT_OUTSIDE` is gone. Cleanup removes `UnseenFX` and `DarkvisionFX`. `UnseenFX` and `DarkvisionFX` can both sit on the camera (debug darkvision in a woodland run); `UnseenFX` is attached in `create`, so a darkvision pipeline attached later runs after it.
  - **Daylight maps (#258, the woodland):** beyond sight the scenery (ground, trees, bushes, flowers) is still drawn, hazy (see above). `LightView` leaves the darkness off there (`seenForDark(visible, daylight)`: every tile counts as seen for the darkness on a daylight map); `seenLit` still follows the real sight, so bees, butterflies and sunbeams stay hidden beyond it. Creatures stay hidden because the server sends none from beyond sight (`run/visibility.rs`); sight distances are unchanged. On daylight maps the trunk shade (`ShadeMask`) is off: the mask's soft level does its job (the same geometry, `shadeCovers`), so it never stacks with the outside haze.
- **Filtering:** the data textures are NEAREST (one texel is one tile); the pictures `lightpic` and `dvpic` are set to LINEAR once, after `setRenderToTexture` (Phaser makes a render target NEAREST under `pixelArt`). Nothing resets them, because no canvas `refresh()` happens any more. Phaser keeps the filter on the texture's wrapper, so a restored WebGL context keeps LINEAR too.
- **Uploads: only the camera window (#292).** `GpuLight` sends only the window `LightView` recomputed (the camera window plus `MARGIN`, in tiles, so in texels) from its `Uint8Array` buffers to the GPU: `WindowUpload` in `game/texupload.ts` copies that rectangle into a window-sized `Uint8Array` (`copyWindow`) and calls `texSubImage2D` on the texture's `WebGLTexture`. Phaser has no API for a partial upload, so it sets the same pixel-store flags Phaser uses for the texture (the wrapper's `pma`, false for the data textures, and `flipY`) and restores the TEXTURE0 binding afterwards. Outside the window the GPU texture keeps stale values, which the camera never shows. That is about 11 KB per frame (66×40 texels × 4 bytes); with darkvision the second texture adds as much. For comparison, before #292 the three whole canvases were uploaded every frame: 3 × 280 × 280 × 4 bytes, about 919 KiB. The darkvision data (`dvdata`) is neither written, uploaded nor drawn while darkvision is 0, because `syncDarkvisionFX` has removed its effect then (the frame that turns it off still clears the tiles that left its reach). Toggling darkvision creates no Shader or texture: both exist from the constructor.
- **What the light pass costs (#293; F3's `light` is the time of `LightView.update` per frame).** The user's readings on a desktop GPU, with the shader path: woodland about 0.50 ms standing still and above 1.00 ms while walking and fighting; dungeon about 0.50 ms standing and about 0.80 ms and more while busy. With the old canvas path the woodland stood above 0.80 ms. Where the time goes (headless browser, software rendering, a two-player run, a hero with darkvision, ms per frame): dungeon standing 0.73 = the per-tile loop 0.43 + `accumulate` 0.06 + the output step 0.24 (upload 0.03, draw 0.20); woodland standing 0.88 = per-tile loop 0.52 + `accumulate` 0.03 + output step 0.34 (upload 0.07, draw 0.26). While the viewer moves, the visibility computation (`View.compute`) adds 0.46 ms (dungeon) and 0.70 ms (woodland). One hand draw of a Shader costs about 0.11 to 0.14 ms whatever the tile count (Phaser's state changes and its uniform and attribute look-ups); with darkvision there are two. The design's goal, well under 0.5 ms, was not reached: the output step roughly halved (woodland demo 0.31 to 0.17 ms, `?lights` 0.20 to 0.12 ms; the upsampling is off the CPU), but the per-tile loop and the visibility computation are the same as before and make up most of the rest.
- **Flicker:** each light's brightness wobbles by its kind's `flicker` and a per-light phase (`flicker(kind, id, t)`, two sine waves).
- **Redraws:** `LightView.update` runs every frame, but redraws only the camera's tile window plus 3 tiles (`MARGIN`: the shader's Catmull-Rom taps read two tiles around each one; about 66×40 tiles), so flicker and moving lights cost little. A torch's radius 7 means up to 225 tiles per torch, from a cached shadowcast. The viewer's FOV is recomputed when the viewer's tile changes or the map does.
- **Sound text (the rule, 2026-10-16):** text that stands for a sound is drawn **above** the darkness at `DEPTH.sound` (7500), so it shows even where nothing else does: the wolf's "grrrr" and the sleeper's "Z". It uses `SOUND_TEXT` (`game/effects.ts`): a dark fill `#1a1020` with a light outline `#d8d0e0` (a pale fill vanished inside the pale outline), set per call as `fx.text(..., SOUND_TEXT.fill, false, DEPTH.sound, SOUND_TEXT.stroke)`. Damage and heal numbers are not sounds and keep their rule below. A sound only shows for an entity the client has: heroes and the wolf are always sent, an enemy in the dark is not (no enemy sound exists yet).
- **Draw order:** every figure and shot is drawn **under** the darkness, heroes, teammates, their name tags (children of the figure's container) and the party's shots (also the own predicted ones) included, so a hero on an unlit tile vanishes like a monster (the user's decision, 2026-10-15). Glowing shots still show because they carry their own light. Numbers (damage, heal, immune) and HP bars on heroes and allies are above the darkness (`DEPTH.heroBars` 7999, `DEPTH.numbers` 8000), so they show even when the hero is unlit. Monster numbers and HP bars are under it (`DEPTH.enemyNumbers` 6501, `DEPTH.enemyBars` 6500), chosen by `numberDepth(p)` (from `Dmg.p` or `Heal.p`) (the user's decision, 2026-10-23). Above the darkness: the vision mask (`DEPTH.fog + 0.5`), then the HUD, the E hint, the lich drain beams and the debug paths.
- **Daylight (#156).** The lit area of a `Daylight` light is the server's: a radius-7 shadowcast like any static light, and the client adds no lit tile. `LightField.accumulate` takes a `dim` callback that dims a light's **centre only** (`cloudy(fall, dim)` leaves the rim at its full falloff, so the lit area never changes size): `LightView` passes `cloudAt(id, t, lookFor(id, sunLook))` for Daylight lights, 1 for the others. A cloud is a dip in the light's brightness (`sunLook.clouds`, up to 0.45 per sun, over 3 to 6 s every `gap` (8 to 25) seconds, the schedule is hashed from the light id, but each client times it on its own clock, like torch flicker, so clouds are not in sync between players). **`Sunbeams`** (`sunbeams.ts`, geometry and looks in the pure `sunmath.ts`) is created after `Mood` in the `PhaserRunView` constructor, updated right after `mood.update` in `PhaserRunView.frame` and destroyed in `PhaserRunView.destroy`; it scans the static lights every frame, so doors and light events need no hook. For each `Daylight` light that is on, within the view plus a margin (`MARGIN` 1 + `HEIGHT_RANGE` max 7 + the slant, in tiles) and whose own tile is seen and lit (`LightView.seenLit`), it draws a **beam** (a `Graphics` of gradient trapezoids with vertex alpha, ADD blend, `DEPTH.sunBeam` 6489: over figures and effects, under the air and the darkness) from a point above the sun tile (height 5 to 7 tiles, a slant of 2 tiles from the left) down to a **floor patch** (a generated soft ellipse texture `sun-patch`, ADD blend, `DEPTH.sunPatch` 1.5: over the decals, under figures) and a few **dust motes** (the `dot` sprite at `DEPTH.airDust`, shown only on seen lit tiles). Everything sits under `DEPTH.fog`, so it shows only on tiles the viewer sees lit and lights nothing itself. Every sun has its own look from `sunVariant(id)` (a hash of the light id, the same for everyone): widening or straight beam, patch width 1.5 to 3 tiles, height, 2 or 3 inner rays laid out as a `fan` or as `parallel` shafts, a faint wide main ray under them, brightness (`glow`) and cloud depth. Objects are pooled per light. No art files; the shapes are runtime geometry.
- **Open-air daylight (#203, the woodland).** `Layout.daylight` becomes `Lights.daylight` on the server (`run/light.rs`): `lit(x, y)` is true for every tile in the map, whatever the light sources say, so what a hero sees depends on the field of vision alone and enemies notice a hero as they do in the light. `RunStartInfo.daylight` sets `LightField.daylight` on the client (`RunSession` constructor): `accumulate` fills every tile with white (`DAYLIGHT`, no tint). The dungeon's air is off in daylight (`PhaserRunView` makes no `Mood`). Beyond the sight edge the scenery is dimmed, not blacked out (#258, see the vision mask above).
- The renderer knows nothing about torches, lava or projectiles: it draws whatever the registry sends. Lava keeps its pulsing glow sprites (`terrain.ts`), which sit under the darkness and only show where lit.

## 8. Netcode and responsiveness

**Input (`RunSession.fixedStep`, #302).**
- The run session (`run/session.ts`, §9 "The run session and the view interface") owns the prediction (the `Predictor`, the own shots, `predictAbility`) and the fixed step; the host only hands it one input record per step. `GameScene.readInput` builds that `StepInput` (`run/input.ts`): the movement `mx`, `my`, `primary`, `secondary` (held, or clicked since the last step: the click latches are consumed by every step, also while dead), `act` (one per E press) and `held`, true while any movement key is down, also when two cancel out (A and D), so the own figure still plays the walk animation. The own walk or idle animation reads `held` from the last fixed step, not the keys at frame time, so above 60 Hz it can switch up to one step (16.7 ms) later than the keys; that is when the prediction starts or stops moving.
- Inputs are sent at a fixed 60 Hz. Each step carries movement, the aim, and a shot id when an ability fires; the client fires when the button is held or was clicked and its local cooldown is ready. The aim comes from the only question the session asks the view, `RunView.pointerWorld()`; without an answer it follows the movement, else it stays.
- WebSocket `TCP_NODELAY` is enabled on the server listener.
- **Cache headers** (`cache_headers` in `main.rs`): hashed Vite bundles (`/assets/index-*`) are `immutable`; everything else (`index.html`, the atlas) is `no-cache`. Without this, a browser could keep running an old client against a new server, and the tile rules and prediction would disagree.

**Prediction (`world.ts` `Predictor`).**
- Local movement uses the TS collision port. It is bit-identical to Rust, which `sim.test.ts` checks against the Rust-written fixtures.
- On each snapshot the client replays unacknowledged inputs from the server state.
- Errors under 24 px are blended out over about 100 ms; larger errors snap.
- Terrain is part of the port: the speed factor and the walking rules.
- **Solid objects are part of the port** (`Predictor.solids`): the squares of resting barrels from the latest snapshot go to `stepMove`, like the server's `Run::solids`. A barrel moves only after a push or sinks, so prediction next to barrels should need no correction; that is part of the open live check.
- **Knockback is part of the port** (`stepMove`, `knocked()`). `me` carries `knock_vx/knock_vy`, and the replay starts from them, so after the snapshot that reports a push the prediction slides exactly like the server. The push itself is the enemy's action, so the client learns of it about RTT/2 late (a correction, blended or snapped as usual). While predicted knocked, `RunSession.fixedStep` fires nothing, and the replay drops dashes sent during a push the client learned of late, like the server. The predictor also applies the server's sinking rule (`sinking()` in `sim/collision.ts`): a hero not dashing whose centre is over a chasm or deep water stops moving and attacking, in prediction and in the replay, so a fall needs no round trip and causes no correction. The pending list keeps each input as it was sent, not as predicted: if the server sees no fall (or a debug climb-out already happened there), it applies the move, and the replay must too.
- **Prediction stops while charmed (#224).** `Predictor.charmed` comes from `me.charmed_by` (set only while the charm is on; paused: not charmed). While it is set, `reconcile` copies position, state and both cooldowns from the server's `me`, clears the pending inputs and blends nothing, and `step` numbers an empty input and moves and ticks nothing. `RunSession.fixedStep` keeps sending empty inputs (mx = my = 0, no ability, aim unchanged) only to keep the ack/seq clock going and, if the charm ends before the client knows, so the server sees what it expects (no flag on the wire); cooldowns come from `me`, not from these inputs. The own figure, the camera and the air mover are drawn from the interpolated own entity (`Predictor.charmedPos`). **Blending:** at the start the figure begins at its last drawn place and decays into the interpolated hero (offset times e^(-dt/0.1), like `renderPos`); when control returns the first reconcile sets `offX/offY` to drawn minus predicted, so the existing 0.1 s blend runs and there is no jump. The AI's shots (ids from `AI_SHOT_BASE`, 0xF000, owner = you) are never taken for predicted ones: `ownPredictedShot` is false for them, so they are drawn from the server and never hidden. The use hint is off while charmed; chat, the menu, the log and the stats overlay still work. Charmed hero entities: `meleeFoe` and `shotPassesThrough` (`game/shotTargets.ts`) treat a Hostile hero as a foe, and its bar is the enemy's red one. Its over-head name tag stays white on purpose (enemies have no name label to copy); the red bar, the heart and the pink party row mark it. While charmed the HUD root gets the class `charmed` (`CharmView.update`), which moves the messages below the CHARMED title block.
- Local cooldowns are corrected when they differ from the server by more than 0.15 s. The server value is first carried through the inputs it has not acknowledged yet, including the abilities they use (`Pending.used` for cooldowns; like the movement replay). Without that, a snapshot from before the server saw a shot showed cooldown 0, the client reset its own, and a held button fired a second, client-only shot (seen as two arrows when dragging the mouse while clicking).

**Own attacks.**
- `RunSession.predictAbility` starts the animation, cooldown or dash locally (the ranger's `SummonWolf` only the cooldown: the wolf itself is not predicted) and spawns cosmetic projectiles in `Shots` (`run/shots.ts`: their flight, wall and enemy hits, piercing and the hidden server copies are state in the session; the view only draws `RunFrame.shots` and reacts to `shotEnded`).
- The matching server projectile (same `shot_id`) is hidden, also for 2 s after the cosmetic one has ended (`endedShots`). The cosmetic one ends when the server one disappears, at a wall, or at the first enemy it touches (`sim/hitbox.ts`, the port of `Monster::shot_dist` with the exported `HITBOX` table; sampled every 4 px; `ResolvedAbility.projectile` is an `EntityKind`, sent as its id). Without the enemy check a shot that hit within the first ticks never appeared in a snapshot, and its cosmetic copy flew on through every enemy behind.

**Remote entities (`Interpolator`).**
- Snapshots are placed on a server-tick timeline using an estimated clock offset (a low envelope of arrival offsets).
- Remote entities are rendered behind that timeline by an adaptive delay of 1.5 snapshot intervals + 2.5× jitter, clamped to 50–300 ms.
- Brief extrapolation of at most 100 ms covers a missing snapshot.

**Measuring.**
- F3 overlay (§10 "The F3 panel"): FPS; the whole frame, its render part and `GameScene.update` (which is `RunSession.frame`); the update split per client system; display-list size, draw calls and the canvas size; then ping, jitter, interpolation delay, snapshot bytes, prediction correction, server ms per tick, entities, darkvision.
- F4 (debug runs only): cycles your own hero's darkvision 0 -> 50% -> 100% -> 0 via `DebugDarkvision` (the server honours it only in a debug run). The client ignores F4 unless the run is a debug run and your hero is alive. While darkvision > 0 the main camera carries the `DarkvisionFX` post pipeline (`client/src/game/darkvisionfx.ts`), which greys the scene where LightView's darkvision mask (`LightView.mask`, the `dvpic` texture) is set; it is removed at 0. In darkvision reach a tile is never darker than `DV_DARK` (0.45), lit or not, and the mask fades out as a light gets brighter than darkvision (`dvMask`): without this, the dim rim of every light was darker than the darkvision around it.
- F9 (debug runs only, alive): toggles immortality via `DebugImmortal` (the server echoes it in `DebugPath.immortal`; the badge shows MORTAL).
- Shift+F7 (debug runs only, alive): switches your own hero's anti-magic on and off via `DebugAntiMagic` (§5 "Magic and non-magic effects"): its magic effects, worn magic items and Radiant are paused while it is on. F7 alone is the incorporeal key.
- Shift+F8 (debug runs only, alive): a Dispel on your own hero via `DebugDispel` (`Want::Positive`, 3 picks, 5 s). Together with F8, which now applies its effect as magic, this shows the greyed icons and the grey chips.
- F8 (debug runs only, alive): cycles `DebugEffect` through every `EffectKind` (every kind, including Weakened, Clumsy and Frail) on your own hero, with the default duration (Charmed takes the nearest living enemy as its caster; at any distance; "No caster for Charmed" means no living enemy at all, and nothing happens); the server shows "name: Kind" as a message. Use it to see the HUD icons, the lying pose and the stone look without a lich.
- `?lag=&jitter=&loss=` simulates conditions in both directions, keeping TCP ordering (`DelayLine`: one FIFO queue and one timer, because browsers round timer delays down to whole milliseconds; a timer per message delivered inputs out of order, which the server then applied out of order, and that caused most of the corrections once seen in busy runs under lag).
- In a busy debug run under `?lag=150&jitter=40&loss=2` (bot walking to the demon and fighting it for 90 s), about 36 of 2,760 snapshots still correct the prediction: almost all are pushes, which the client learns of about RTT/2 late, plus the force field closing during a dash.

**Typical numbers (local):**
- server tick about 0.12–0.14 ms
- snapshot about 127 B with 2 players (the knock is `[vx, vy]` only while pushed, nil otherwise) (248 B before the compact form); a fight with 14 entities in view about 350 B (about 570 B before). Each extra entity costs about 19 B.
- correction 0.00 px

## 9. Rendering (client)

**Art source.**
- Art comes from the 0x72 pack; other CC0 tilesets that fit its style may be added (the user allowed this for terrain and enemies). Terrain tiles (water, lava, chasm) are generated at atlas build time by `client/scripts/terrain-tiles.ts` from pack colours and the pack's `floor_1`, because no CC0 set fit (the user allowed self-made art for terrain tiles only): seamless animated base textures (`terrain_<set>_f<frame>_q<piece>`) plus edge overlays (`terrain_<edge>_e<code>`, a 4-digit quarter code from the 8 neighbours, see `terrain-codes.ts`). `build-atlas.ts` turns `tile_list_v1.7` into a Phaser JSON-hash atlas, so frame names are the pack's own (`knight_m_run_anim_f2`, `weapon_axe`, `floor_1`, …).
- **Dragon frames** (`scripts/dragon-frames.ts`, poses and layout in `src/game/anim/dragonRig.ts`, shared with the client): AI-generated art made for this game at the user's request (2026-10-09 to 10-12), in `client/assets-src/dragon/`.
  - Sources: `dragon_base.png` (the standing dragon, 49×43, 9 colours) and one aligned strip per action (`dragon_walk`, `dragon_claw`, `dragon_bite`, `dragon_tail`, `dragon_hover`.png). `DRAGON_SHEETS` gives each strip's cell size, `shift` into base coordinates and the per-frame head offset.
  - Walk, claw, bite, tail whip and hover frames are copied from the strips. Idle, breath, take-off and landing are generated from the base: it is cut into parts (head, jaw, wings, tail, body; polygon masks), the parts are moved per pose, the outline is redrawn and joint cracks filled. On the hover frames only the jaw opens (fire from the air); that works because their head sits where the base head is.
  - The mouth and nostril points for the breath come from the same poses (`dragonPoints()`), so they follow the art.
  - All frames are 86×54 with the base at (19, 7); the feet are on row 50 (`footY`, the figure origin); legs hanging in flight reach below.
  - How the strips were imported (one-off scripts, not in the repo): the user's JPGs on a magenta background were converted with PowerShell `System.Drawing`; a rectangular pixel grid was fitted per strip (they were stretched unevenly), cell centres sampled (median) and snapped to the base palette; frames separated as connected shapes (specks dropped) and stray light-red pixels on the silhouette removed; then each frame placed in base coordinates, on the base head (walk, claw, hover) or on the planted hind claws (bite, tail), with the head offset found by matching the base head. The walk strip was drawn ~15% smaller and was resampled to base size. Redo the same for new strips.
- **Barrel frames** (`scripts/barrel-frames.ts`): AI-generated art made for this game with ChatGPT at the user's request (2026-10-15), source in `client/assets-src/barrel/barrel_source.png` (a 20×27 pixel grid at about 25 screen px per pixel). That grid was read once (cell medians, colours snapped to pack colours), then redrawn by hand at 16×22, about the crate's size, because 20×27 stands taller than a hero. The script holds the grid: `barrel` (plain wood, for blocking barrels that do not explode; not used yet) and `barrel_explosive` (the same with a red wood lid and a red band, the user's choice from three variants). `game/barrel.ts` draws `barrel_explosive`; `?gallery` shows both plus the fuse, fall and sink animations.
- **Torch frames** (`scripts/torch-frames.ts`): "Simple Torch Animation 16x16" by Natural_Privateer (OpenGameArt, CC0, "credit is appreciated"), the sheet and a licence note in `client/assets-src/torch/`. The sheet is a 3×2 grid of 16×16 frames, the last one empty; `buildTorches` copies the filled cells into the atlas as `torch_f0`… (the flame animation, 8 fps in `game/torch.ts`) and makes `torch_out`, frame 0 without its flame pixels (red ≥ 180 and green > blue), a bare stick, for an unlit torch. The side sconces reuse the same frames: `TorchView` mirrors them and moves them to the side wall's inner edge facing into the room. `?lights` shows lit and burnt-out torches. Credit: README Art.
- **Cove look** (#202; `game/covemath.ts`, `game/cove.ts`, `game/pixies.ts`, `game/silhouette.ts`, `scripts/cove-frames.ts`; try it in `?cove`, and it is in the game now, in every dungeon). The user approved the design on 2026-11-01.
  - Tree: drawn for this game by code (`coveTree()` in `game/vegetation.ts`: the general round tree with the `old` option (#279: thicker, more bent trunk, four longer roots, a lower fork), seed 11, at `COVE_TREE_SCALE` 0.97 (the largest that fits), 96x100, trunk foot at (48, 80) = `COVE_TREE_FOOT`), pack-style palette lit from the top left (`DEFAULT_LIGHT`): a lobed crown with hanging vines, the crown's shadow on the trunk. The cove's surroundings (`ROOT_FRINGE`, `TRUNK_CLEAR`, the painted canopy shadow, `CANOPY_UP`) still fit the older, thinner tree; the user left them for #299 (a willow replaces the cove tree). It sits at `DEPTH.entityBase + trunk foot y`. In the game only the trunk tile blocks movement (`Tile::Trunk`, §6); roots and canopy don't.
  - Grass is only a look, no tile: `paintCove` draws one rounded carpet 22 px in from the floor edge with a wobbling edge, base `3e8a43`, faint patches `3a8340`, edge shade `31743c`, the canopy shadow under the tree, and pointed 2-4 px blades along the border; the stone under the bottom edge is darkened. `coveGrass` says where grass is (flowers and plants only go there). Flowers are single pixels or 5-pixel crosses in five colours, denser towards the tree.
  - Plants (`placeCovePlants`, 30 in all, seeded; seed 1 uses the approved prototype's seeds; the demo room is a different size, so it is not pixel-identical): bushes, tufts and ferns as atlas frames, kept apart (24 px for bushes, 15 px otherwise) and off the trunk and roots (`TRUNK_CLEAR`). Then 10 tufts and ferns among the roots (`ROOT_FRINGE`, own rng stream so the others keep their spots): around the root tips and in front of the roots, 9 px apart, never on the trunk base, so the roots blend into the grass. Depth-sorted by their feet like figures; heroes walk through them.
  - In the game (`game/cove.ts`): `coveLayout(CoveInfo)` turns the tiles into px: the floor rect's top-left is `x*16, y*16` and the trunk foot is the trunk tile's bottom edge and horizontal centre, `(tree_x*16 + 8, tree_y*16 + 16)`, which gives the `covemath` spec (floor size, tree relative to the floor, `seed`). `CoveView` is created in the `PhaserRunView` constructor after the sunbeams from `RunStartInfo.cove`, updated each frame after the entity views have moved, and destroyed in `PhaserRunView.destroy`. It draws the grass at `DEPTH.decal`, the tree and plants at `DEPTH.entityBase` plus their foot y, the pixies, and the silhouette. All of it is under the darkness, like figures, so an unseen cove stays black. The silhouette is for the own hero while it plays (not spectating or dead): `EntityView.bodyLook()` gives its `BodyLook` (`bodyLookFrom`; world-space sprite position, origin, scale, flip and `footY`, the depth-sort foot) and `Silhouette.update` takes it. The plant cap scales with the floor area, `Math.round(30 * w * h / (320 * 256))`, so the 26×20 room gets about 49 plants where the demo's room gets 30. `autotile.ts` draws a trunk tile as floor (the tree image covers it).
  - Pixies: tiny additive glow balls with a 1-2 px core and one pale wing pixel on each side that flips every frame; they drift on an ellipse around the canopy. Client-only, no light, no game effect.
  - Silhouette (own hero only, WebGL only): while the hero is behind the tree, a copy of the body sprite (no weapon) filled with `cae6f5` at 40 % alpha is drawn above the tree and cut to the tree's shape with a `BitmapMask`. "Behind" is judged by the sprite's y (its foot), as depth sorting does (`behindOccluder`, `heroBox`). Teammates and enemies get none.
  - Light: several Daylight lights so the whole room is lit, with `Sunbeams` and `Mood`.
- **Woodland look** (#203; `game/vegetation.ts`, `game/woodland/*`, `game/unseenfx.ts`, `game/visionmask.ts`, `PhaserRunView`; try it in `?woodland`). The user approved the look on 2026-11-07 (conifers, forest, clouds, road).
  - Plants: `vegetation.ts` draws every tree, conifer, bare tree and bush at run time from shapes (`drawTree`, `drawBush`, `drawPlant(kind, seed, scalePct, light = DEFAULT_LIGHT)`; `PLANT_KINDS` round, wide, tall, conifer, bush, bare = the wire ids of `PlantKind`), in the pack-style palette; pure, no Phaser, so the atlas script imports it too (the cove tree). A bush's `width` is its drawn width (32 px at 100 %). The drawing code is in `vegetation/*`. The user approved the look of #279 on 2026-11-10:
    - Leafy trees (`leafy.ts`, round/wide/tall): a bent, tapering trunk (`trunkPath`) with a foot flare, two or three limbs from the fork, two to four roots, wavy bark lines, root moss; a crown of overlapping lobes (`crown.ts`) with dark seams between them, light dabs, a few dark pockets with a twig and hanging vines, and the crown's shadow on the trunk. `TreeOpts.old` makes an older tree (trunk ×1.4, bend ×1.9, four roots ×1.25 long, fork 3 units lower); only the cove tree uses it so far.
    - Conifers (`conifer.ts`): a straight trunk, 1 to 24 design units of it bare below the lowest skirt, under stacked skirts with zigzag hems in the blue-green ramp (palette 5–9), narrowing to a spire.
    - Bare trees (`bare.ts`, kind 5): the leafy trunk and roots under recursive leafless limbs (depth 4, 3 below 70 %) ending in 1-px twigs; no crown shadow. Their bark is mapped to the dead-wood ramp e..h (greyer and darker than a..d) as the last pass, so every bark pass before it sees a..d.
    - Bushes (`bush.ts`): three to five lobes (by drawn width) on one thin stem in the middle (1 px, 2 px from 40 px wide) that forks low into two or three branches under the crown; a dark underside. `BUSH_SPREAD` 1.1 against the layout's extent sets the drawn width.
    - Light contract (#277, kept by #279): the light changes only colours, never which pixels are opaque, the size or the foot. Every random draw is made whatever the light; positions that depend on the sun only recolour; every write gives the picture's colour and the default light's reference colour (`Canvas.ref`), and colour decisions read the reference. A sweep test checks it (6 kinds × 12 seeds × 3 sizes × 3 suns). `Canvas.id` is a lobe-id map for seams and shadows between lobes and skirts; whole-picture passes loop over the tracked box, and `finish` crops to it.
    - Draw cost (#279, ms per plant at 60/100/120 %, before → after): round 2.47/4.13/5.80 → 0.58/1.06/1.49, wide 2.53/4.83/6.44 → 0.80/1.26/1.70, tall 1.91/2.98/3.79 → 0.45/0.79/1.02, conifer 4.38/5.76/6.61 → 0.30/0.40/0.45, bare 0.42/0.68/0.83 (budget: tall's), bush at 100/150 % 0.61/1.19 → 0.24/0.25; all the distinct looks of demo map seed 1: 1266 → 217 ms. `npx tsx scripts/plant-sheet.ts --bench` measures it; `npx tsx scripts/plant-sheet.ts <dir>` writes the judging sheets (every kind at 1× and 3×, small sizes, a right sun, the cove tree).
  - Light and sun (#277): the light is a parameter of every plant. `lightVector(sun)` gives a unit vector toward the sun in screen space (`DEFAULT_LIGHT`, a sun on the left at about 51°, is the one fixed light of the cove tree and the atlas script). The random stream is independent of the light (each random draw is made where the default light would make it), so a seed's plant shape never changes with the sun; only the shading does. Within a run the light is one constant, so the `PlantPages` frame key stays (kind, seed, size).
  - Cast shadows (#277; `woodland/shadows.ts`, pure, no Phaser; `woodland/shadowlayer.ts`): every tree, conifer and bush casts a ground shadow from the run's sun. `shadowVector(sun)` is the ground offset per pixel of height: away from the sun and toward the viewer, `sx = -side * L * SHADOW_X`, `sy = L * SHADOW_Y`, `L = cot(elevation)`, `SHADOW_X` 0.9, `SHADOW_Y` 0.45 (the 3/4 view compresses the depth axis). `shadowOf(img, foot, vec, mirrored)` projects every opaque pixel above the foot as a solid raster without pinholes (also at a low sun) and adds a contact ellipse at the foot (radii `CONTACT_RX` 0.3 and `CONTACT_RY` 0.12 of the width), so a high sun still grounds the plant. A mirrored plant is projected from its mirrored frame, so the direction does not flip with the plant; the shadow frame is keyed by (kind, seed, size, mirrored). The output is solid (alpha 255 or 0): softness comes from the layer, never from the raster.
  - The one-layer rule: `ShadowLayer` is one `RenderTexture` the size of the map (3008 × 736 px for 188 × 46 tiles; `fits` checks it against the renderer's maximum texture size, and shadows are skipped with a console warning if it does not fit) at `DEPTH.plantShadow` 1.55 (over the ground strips, under the trunk shade 1.6, figures and canopies), shown at `SHADOW_ALPHA` (0.28, a first guess) as a whole. Each shadow frame is stamped into it fully opaque, so two overlapping shadows are exactly as dark as one, never darker. A shadow is rasterised in the same `DrawQueue` job as its plant, packed into the `PlantPages` next to the plant frame (key `shadow:...`) and stamped at the plant's foot (`ShadowLayer.stamp`) when the plant shows, so it appears with its plant and needs no change to the settle logic. The layer keeps every stamp and draws them again after a restored WebGL context (#298, §9 "Board").
  - Shadows lie under figures by decision: a hero under a tree stands on dark ground and is not darkened. Cloud shadows stay over everything (a cloud is above everything). Figures keep the soft ground shadow of `EntityView`; the cove tree keeps its painted canopy shadow (`covemath.ts`).
  - Ground (`woodland/ground.ts`): the whole map is painted once into RGBA, Floor and Trunk tiles as the cove's grass with flowers (about one per 40 px²), Road as packed dirt, Thicket as dark undergrowth; where a stronger ground meets a weaker one (road over thicket over grass) it creeps 3 px into it, dithered, with a faint darker rim where the road meets grass, so no tile edge shows. `GroundPainter` paints in row bands, the bands nearest the spawn first, and `WoodlandView` uploads the result as 64 px strips (one per frame, nearest the camera first). Until a strip is up a flat placeholder shows.
  - Plant drawing (`woodland/plantpages.ts`, `WoodlandView`): each distinct plant (kind, seed, size) is drawn once and packed into a `PlantPages` page, a 1024×1024 canvas texture; equal plants share a frame (the server's `EDGE_SEEDS` keep the count down). A `DrawQueue` runs the jobs, the ground bands before any plant, within an 8 ms budget per frame that counts the page uploads too (a job starts only if the slowest job seen so far still fits; the last upload's cost is taken off the next frame's budget), plants nearest to the camera first, sorted again when the camera has moved 320 px. A plant shows as soon as its frame exists. Trees and bushes sort with figures by their foot (`DEPTH.entityBase + y`), so a canopy covers a creature behind the trunk. The plant-draw timing is logged to the console only in the `?woodland` demo.
  - Own hero: `Silhouette` as in the cove, one copy per tree the hero stands behind (#262): `occludersOf` (`silhouette.ts`, pure) lists every tree whose box holds the hero, front-most first, at most `MAX_OCCLUDERS` (4), looked up in a grid of 4×4-tile buckets; `WoodlandView` keeps a pool of copies, each drawn just above its own tree and masked by it. Where a front tree covers a back tree's copy it covers it like everything else, so nothing doubles; with one copy only, a hero covered by the back one of two overlapping trees (whose front tree's wide box but not its pixels overlap the hero) showed nothing.
  - Settling and the soft level: `WoodlandView.settled` (`woodland/settle.ts`, `PendingGrid`, `sceneSettled`) says the scenery is complete around the camera; the haze's soft level (§7) waits for it (on dark maps, the `ShadeMask`).
  - Cloud shadows (`woodland/clouds.ts`): one tileable noise texture (periodic lattice, 512² at a quarter of the screen's resolution, so it repeats every 2048 px) baked once and rebuilt only when `size` or `cover` change; a `TileSprite` at `DEPTH.cloud` scrolls it (speed along +x with a slight +y drift), with black at alpha `dark`; every client starts at its own offset. The defaults are the user's tuned `DEFAULT_CLOUDS` (size 360, speed 38, dark 0.46, cover 0.35). Mind: a `TileSprite` copies its frame into its own `fillPattern`, uploaded NEAREST under `pixelArt`, and re-copies only on `setFrame`/`setTexture`; `Clouds.smooth` sets LINEAR on that pattern after every copy.
  - Critters (`woodland/critters.ts`): bees (3 per flower patch) loop round flower patches (radius 4 to 9 px) and butterflies wander (one per 120 walkable tiles, white, yellow, light blue or orange, flapping, now and then landing); every position is a pure function of time and seed. They sort by ground y like figures (so canopies and trunks cover them), a bee by its own position in its loop (`beeDepth`, #263; not its flower's, or it vanished behind a bush it flew in front of), and show only where the viewer sees (`seenLit`).
  - Daylight: no darkness or tint, no `Mood` (§7). The map's wall and void tiles are drawn as in the dungeon; the ground tiles are not (`GROUND_TILES`, Barrier included). Sight is the vision mask's circle with the scenery hazy beyond it and behind trunks and thickets (#258, #278; §7).
- **Ghost and Shade looks** (#169, #170; `game/anim/entityAlpha.ts`, `game/shadeHaze.ts`, `game/anim/EntityView.ts`, `game/effects.ts`; try them in `?spectres`).
  - Ghost: `ghostPulse` replaces `INCORPOREAL_ALPHA`, a sine between 0.55 and 0.85 once per 1.5 s, out of step per entity (`ghostPhase(id)`). During `ANIM.Ambush` it blinks between 0 and the pulse every 60 ms (`GHOST_FLICKER_SECS`, from `animT`). When a figure reappears into `ANIM.Ambush`, `PhaserRunView.drawFigure` skips the 0.3 s fade-in: the flicker is the entrance. Death: `fx.disintegrate`, about 24 pale sparks rising over 0.6 s, no skull.
  - Shade: drawn a little more solid than other incorporeal figures (`FigureDef.incorporealAlpha` 0.75 instead of `INCORPOREAL_ALPHA` 0.6), wrapped in a veil of pure black strands (#200, `ShadeVeil`; maths in `VeilStrands`, `veilPixels`, pure and tested). Ten strands are rooted inside the body (`STRANDS`, built by `around(deg, …)`, facing right, mirrored), so they grow out of it: eight go round it in order, two wrap across the lower body in front (drawn over the figure at 80 %, below the eyes). Each strand has two rest directions: standing, straight out from the body (together a cloud round it, segments 1.6 px, a slow billowing wave); moving, behind it (segments 3 px, a faster ripple). The blend follows the body's own speed (full stream at 25 px/s, eased over 0.25 s). Each point eases toward where it would hang off the one before it, slower toward the tip, and a segment never stretches past 1.3 times its length, so the strands stay attached when it moves. The veil follows the body, not the entity position, so it moves with the attack lunge. Drawn as whole black pixels: strands about 3 px wide at the root, 1 at the tip, solid then 60 % then a dithered 30 % (`veilAlpha`); between neighbouring back strands, round the whole ring, a cloth fill at 55 / 35 / dithered 20 % (`sheetAlpha`). Inside the ring of roots a solid black core, so the veil has no hole where the body is (it would show when the body fades at death). The veil fades in with the figure, rises with the ambush silhouette and is cleared while the Shade is dead or invisible. In `ANIM.Ambush` the figure is drawn as a flat dark silhouette (`SILHOUETTE_FILL`, tint fill, no eyes) whose alpha rises from 0.2 to 0.9 over `AMBUSH_WARNING` (`silhouetteAlpha`; the constant is `CONST.AMBUSH_WARNING`, exported from the server). Death (no skull): `fx.shadeDeath` takes the veil as it was last drawn (`EntityView.veilRemains`, kept by `ShadeVeil.remains` even after the dead flag cleared it; a settled `restingVeil` if the view is gone) and the body's last frame. The body fades in 0.3 s; the veil, with nothing holding it together, spreads out from the body's centre to 1.7 times its size (ease-out), rises 4 px and fades to nothing over `VEIL_DEATH_LIFE` 1 s (`dissolveVeil`).
  - Frames (`scripts/spectre-frames.ts`, #200): 16x16 like the pack monsters, so they draw at ×1 with the hem on the bottom row. The design is an AI-generated ghost (ChatGPT, supplied by the user), redrawn by hand on a 16x16 grid with Claude Code; the Shade is the same body, all black but its glowing red eyes (the user's choice after the phone review; the veil gives it its shape). The script holds ASCII grids (head and body, two idle hems, four run hems) and the two palettes, checks them and draws `ghost_idle_anim_f0..3`, `ghost_run_anim_f0..3` and the same for the shade. Idle bobs 1 px up on f1 and 1 px down on f3 (the last wisp row is cut off there, as approved); run leans 1 px forward with a trailing wisp. The figure is 12 rows tall and 12 wide; with the bob that is the `body_height` 13 the server uses for shots, and `handY` 6 puts the touch at mid-body. The first version (#169, #170) cut 24x24 frames from two 1536x1024 AI sheets; they looked too big and chunky next to the pack and were dropped (#200). To change the art, edit the grids and run `npm run atlas`.
- **Demon frames** (`scripts/demon-frames.ts`, layout in `src/game/anim/demonFrames.ts`, shared with the client): AI-generated art made for this game at the user's request (2026-10-13), in `client/assets-src/demon/`.
  - The user supplied a front-view pixel demon (`images/demon.jpg`); an image generation AI first turned it to a side view facing right, then drew one sheet per action from that (prompts: one strip per action, flat magenta background, same size as the reference). The source images were deleted from the untracked `images/` inbox after the import (the user's request, 2026-10-13); redoing a strip needs a new sheet.
  - Strips: `demon_idle` (4), `demon_walk` (6: the sheet's images 1, 2, 3, 8 and back through 3, 2, the order the user chose, played at 9 fps; the legs cross in the middle of each stride, seen from the side; the strip holds them in play order; from the user's third walk sheet, `images/demon_walk_improved_cross_walk.png`: the first one, 4 frames, was no clean cycle and looked like a limp, and the second, 8 frames, kept the legs apart), `demon_cleave` (5: wind-up, swing, impact, follow-through, recovery; the user's sheet had 7, the two upright resting poses were dropped), `demon_charge` (4: bull crouch, two charging frames, the stop) and `demon_cast` (4: gathering power, rising, the burst, recovery). Every cell is 82×63 with the hips at x 33 (`hipX`, the body's horizontal origin, mirrored when facing left) and the feet on row 62 (`footY`). Drawn at ×1 (about 62 px tall with the horns).
  - How the sheets were imported (one-off scripts, not in the repo; redo the same for new sheets): the JPGs converted to PNG with PowerShell `System.Drawing`; figures found as connected shapes on the magenta; a pixel grid fitted per figure (pitch and phase from the colour edges; 8.6 px for idle, walk and cast, 5.7 px for the charge); the cleave sheet had been resampled by the AI and has no clean grid, so it was sampled at a fixed 5.45 px that matches the others' size; cell centres sampled (median of the middle half, empty where mostly magenta); colours snapped to one 16-colour palette (k-means over all frames, magenta fringe excluded and dropped on the silhouette); only the figure and pieces touching it kept; each frame placed with the loincloth's centre on `hipX` and its lowest pixel on `footY`. The 8-frame walk sheets (resampled by the AI too) was sampled at a fixed 6.25 px (its figures then match the idle height), snapped to the existing 16 colours, and placed on the average of the loincloth's centre and the eyes (minus their mean distance, 10.1 px for the cross walk), so the head stays within 2 px across the cycle; its tail needed 4 more pixels on the left, so every strip's cells were widened from 78 to 82 px (4 empty columns on the left).
  - `build-atlas.ts` checks each strip's size and copies the frames below the lich's.
- **Lich frames** (`scripts/lich-frames.ts`): pixel art drawn for this game with Claude Code at the user's request (2026-10-13), after two reference images the user supplied (a high-resolution crowned lich, a pixel-art lich). The user approved the look in `?gallery`.
  - Pixel grids (one character per pixel) in pack colours: a gold spiked crown, a skull with glowing eyes in a crimson hood, gold crescent pauldrons, a crimson mantle with a jewel clasp, a lean robe with a gold trim strip, and a ragged hem it floats on (no feet).
  - `lich_idle_anim_f0-3` (float bob, hem sway, one eye flicker) and `lich_run_anim_f0-3` (the hem trails behind), 16×28 like the heroes, so it draws at ×1; plus `weapon_lich_staff` (8×30, a bone staff with a purple orb), held 7 px out (`handX`) so it does not cover the right pauldron.
  - The build checks every grid's size and colours; to change the art, edit the grids and run `npm run atlas`.
