- **Title logo** (`scripts/build-logo.ts`, run by `npm run atlas`): made for this game with Google's image generation AI (2026-10-12), in `client/assets-src/logo/logo.png` (1408×768; the generator's watermark in the bottom-right corner was painted over once with wall from its left). The script writes three files into `public/assets/`:
  - `logo.png`: the whole image. The login screen shows it large (`.lobby.title`, `h1.logo.splash` from `ui/logo.ts`); every other `.lobby` screen uses it as a darkened background, darkest in the middle so the emblem behind the panels disappears and only the walls show (on portrait screens the left wall).
  - `logo-emblem.png`: the shield with the title (crop x 370, y 78, 680×626), the small header of the character select, lobby and error page.
  - `favicon.png`: the treasure chest on top, area-averaged to 64×64. The admin page has no favicon.
  - The `<h1>` around the images keeps the name for screen readers (`alt`); the edges fade out with `mask-image` (header from 70% to 85% of the radius, so the full width of ADVENTURERS stays opaque).
- **Recolors:** `RECOLORS` in `build-atlas.ts` swaps whole pack colors for other pack colors and writes the result as new frames in a strip below the sheet (32 px per entry). Today: `summoner_anim_f0-3`, the necromancer with its purple robe (`5f2d56`, trim `9f294e`) turned maroon and red (`62232f`, `da4e38`, the imp's and chort's reds). `scripts/png.ts` is a small PNG codec (8-bit RGB/RGBA, no interlace), so no image library is needed.
- No other self-drawn sprites: only the terrain tiles, the lich and the Ghost and Shade redraw, all at the user's request. See CLAUDE.md "Art".
- Runtime-generated textures are limited to simple effect shapes (`dot`, `spark`, `glow`) and the ground shadow ellipses under figures.

**The dungeon air (`game/mood.ts`, #148; the user approved the look on 2026-10-24).** `Mood` is created after the `LightView` in the `PhaserRunView` constructor, updated each frame right after `lightView.update` in `PhaserRunView.frame` (same `dt` in seconds as `Effects.update`) with the lit and unlit torch entities (`x`, `y`, `extra` side, `anim === 1` lit) and the snapshot's moving lights, told of terrain changes in `mapChanged` (`PhaserRunView.tileChanged`, from the `Tile` event; it collects the lava and water tiles again) and destroyed in `PhaserRunView.destroy`. There is no setting to switch it off. No server or wire change.
- **Layers and depths** (`DEPTH` in `effects.ts`): the air draws over figures, shots, spells and effects and under enemy HP bars and damage numbers, the tint and the darkness: `airHaze` 6490 (a thin cloudy layer over the view: two tiling sprites of a generated 128 px cloud texture, tint `0x8a7c6c`, alpha 0.10 and 0.07, drifting; each is sized to the camera window plus a margin, not the map, because a Phaser TileSprite allocates its full size, and is moved with the view while `tilePosition` keeps the pattern anchored to the world), `airDust` 6491 (faint motes in loose clouds, shown only on a seen and lit tile, brighter where the light is, coloured by the light's hue blended halfway to white, ADD), `airSmoke` 6492 (lava smoke), `airDrips` 6493 (a drop falls into a water tile and leaves two flattened ripple rings and a splash), `airEmbers` 6494 (orange sparks over lava, a trail behind Fire moving lights, one burst when a Blast light first shows). Because they lie under the darkness, the darkness and the vision mask hide them exactly like figures.
- **The exception, torch smoke** (`DEPTH.torchSmoke` 7000.6, just over the darkness and the vision mask (both at 7000.5), under the lich beams at 7001): a torch's smoke rises at once into the dark wall top above it, where the darkness would swallow it. It is drawn above the darkness, but only for a lit torch whose floor tile (`floor(y / TILE) * w + floor(x / TILE)`; the server puts a torch's pixel position inside the floor tile in front of it, see `TorchSpawn::pos`) is seen and lit; the puffs fade out within about 0.25 s when that tile leaves the view. The torch is already visible there, so this reveals nothing. Puffs start at the flame's tip (`flameTip` in `torch.ts`).
- **Density and caps:** dust clouds scale with the camera's world area (7 per 240 tiles, 4 to 24, `cloudCount`), lava smoke and embers spawn per lava tile in view (on average every 4.2 s and 1.08 s), drips per water tile in view (every 7 s), torch smoke per torch in view; effects spawn only for tiles and torches in the camera window plus one tile, and fire embers only for moving lights in it. The server sends moving lights without line of sight, so air also needs sight: a Fire or Blast light makes embers only when its own tile is seen (`seenLit`), and lava smoke and embers come only from lava tiles that are seen. Hard caps: 24 clouds, 200 smoke puffs, 160 embers, 12 drips (`moodmath.ts`). Motes are one world pixel.
- **Stirred air (#158; the user judged it in the `?mood` demo on 2026-10-24).** Client-only and cosmetic, no wire change. `Wind` (`wind.ts`) is a coarse grid of 8 px cells over the camera window plus a 48 px margin (velocities in px/s, capped at `WIND_MAX` 160). Each frame the grid flows first (advect, blur, decay with `WIND_DECAY`; a gust lives about a second), then the queued pushes, wakes and blasts of that frame are applied. **Pockets:** a moving figure (`AirMover`, `shot: false`) pushes a soft pocket of air along its direction and a little outward, reaching about 2.7 radii (at least 16 px) and also ahead of it; walls stop the air. **Projectile wakes:** a shot (`shot: true`) cuts a narrow wake of half-width `r` with alternating eddies behind it, and a warm one (`heat`) lifts the air behind it. `r` and `heat` come from `projectileAir(kind)` in `airshots.ts`, derived from the `PROJECTILES` entry (`r = clamp(12 * scale, 6, 28)`, `heat` 1 for a warm glow), so a new projectile needs no air code; an optional `air: { r?, heat? }` on the entry overrides either, and kinds without an entry get `{ r: 6, heat: 0 }`. **Blasts:** a `LIGHT_KIND.Blast` light pushes a ring outward. **Draft:** a faint, slowly changing current from low-frequency noise (at most `DRAFT_MAX` 3 px/s), added at the sample point, never stored in the grid, never strong enough to stir a cloud. **Who samples:** dust motes, smoke puffs and embers add the wind at their position. **Stirring and respawn:** when the wind at a dust cloud exceeds `STIR_SPEED` (8 px/s) its motes are released and the cloud fades (1 to 2 s); a stirred cloud respawns at once (`stirDelay` 0, the user's verdict; the demo's Respawn button toggles 3 s) anywhere bright, but never inside stirred air. **The seen rule:** movers on tiles that are not currently seen and lit push nothing (`seenAt`, from `seenLit`), so the air reveals nothing; the game still passes only what the client has. **`MoverTracker`** (`airmovers.ts`): `begin(dt)` per frame, `track(id, x, y, r, shot, heat)` gives an `AirMover` whose velocity is the change since the last frame, or null on an id's first frame, when it stood still, or when it moved more than 48 px (a teleport). Speeds are clamped to 400 px/s; ids not tracked in a frame are forgotten. `PhaserRunView.collectMovers` feeds it: figures from `ents` (not torches, hazards, chests, barrels, projectiles, the dead or the own hero; mimics count), the own hero once from prediction (id -1), server projectiles not owned by the player, and the own cosmetic shots (id `-(100000 + shot)`), so each shot counts once. **Heights:** a shot is drawn 8 px above its ground position, so `PhaserRunView` sets `AirMover.airY = y - 8` and the air is injected there (`seenAt` and the view test stay on `y`; if `airY` lies in a wall cell, `y` is used). **Wall-face torches:** their smoke starts inside the opaque wall tile, where the wind is 0, so a torch puff (`src >= 0`) samples the wind at `max(y, top of its floor tile + 1)` while it is drawn at its real position. `projectileAir` is memoized per kind (the returned `ShotAir` is read-only) and `MoverTracker` reuses its position records, to keep the per-frame allocations down.
- **`?mood`** (`MoodDemoScene`): the air on a small room with a knight who walks a loop round the pillar (dashing every 6 s), an arrow, a fireball, a water pool and lava. Buttons: dust, smoke, embers, haze, drips, Stir, Draft and Respawn (instant / 3 s). URL: `?stir=0`, `?draft=0`, `?respawn=<seconds>`, `?wind=1` (draws the wind field as short lines), `?dust=0` etc.

**Board (`PhaserRunView.drawBoard` + `autotile.ts`).**
- The tiles are baked once into a RenderTexture (`mapRt`, `paintBoard`), with a slight per-tile brightness variation and faint grid lines (board-game look). A wooden table frame surrounds the map.
- **A restored WebGL context (#298):** a render texture lives only in graphics memory; when the browser loses the context and restores it (driver crash or update, a GPU switch, reclaimed graphics memory), Phaser recreates it empty and draws nothing into it, while image and canvas textures are re-uploaded. Until #298 the dungeon's floor and walls vanished for the rest of the run. `onContextRestored(scene, redraw)` (`game/contextrestore.ts`) subscribes to the renderer's `restorewebgl` event (`Phaser.Renderer.Events.RESTORE_WEBGL`, emitted after the textures and framebuffers are recreated) until the scene shuts down or the returned function is called; it imports Phaser for types only, so modules using it load in unit tests. Users: `PhaserRunView` clears `mapRt` and runs `paintBoard` again from the current map (tile changes included); `ShadowLayer` keeps its stamps and replays them; the demo scenes (`?lights`, `?cove`, `?sunlight`, `?mood`) build their board with `demoBoard(scene, map)`, which does the same. Anything new that is drawn once into a render texture must register a redraw the same way; render textures drawn every frame (the light pass's `lightpic` and `dvpic`) need nothing. Check: `WEBGL_lose_context` in the console (`gl.getExtension('WEBGL_lose_context').loseContext()`, then `restoreContext()` a second later); checked in a dungeon run, a woodland run, `?lights`, `?cove` and `?woodland` (2026-11-10, headless).
- **Per-frame costs (#294):** a few Phaser calls cost more than they look, so per-frame code avoids them. `Text.setColor` (any `TextStyle` setter) redraws the text canvas and re-uploads it: `drawBarsAndBeams` sets a bar label's colour and depth only when they change (a charm flips the side) and moves it every frame. Every `setDepth` queues a `StableSort` of the whole display list, even with the same value (0.1–0.2 ms per frame at 770–1,090 children, measured 2026-11-09); nothing else skips it yet, but more figures (#282) or scenery grow it; skipping unchanged values or keeping static scenery in a `Layer` are the remedies. A PreFX controller (`preFX.addColorMatrix()` etc.) sends the object through a render-to-texture pass for as long as the FX pipeline is set, even with the controller's `active = false` (Phaser 3.90: `FX.enable` sets `FX_PIPELINE`, only `disable` resets it); so a temporary PreFX is removed with `preFX.disable(true)` when it ends (petrified figures, `EntityView`). The HUD's wolf flag is a plain loop (`hasOwnWolf`), not an array spread. Measured cost per figure is low (~0.01 ms per `EntityView.update` with ~100 figures in `?gallery`).
- **Grid lines (#289):** `gridLines` builds a Graphics (not on the display list) with a 1 px outline (`strokeRect` at +0.5, black, alpha 0.12) per floor tile (not walls, void, terrain, or the woodland's own ground); `drawBoard` draws it into `mapRt` once and destroys it. Until #289 the grid was a live Graphics on the display list, which Phaser re-tessellates every frame: about 127,000 commands on a 140×140 dungeon, ~9 ms of render per frame (Phaser's step 12–15 ms → 4–5.5 ms in a headless smoke run). Each outline also covers the first pixel column and row of the right and lower neighbour, so shared edges are drawn twice (slightly darker). `redrawTile` (a tile change, e.g. the boss hall's force-field entrance) refills the 3×3 area, then draws the grid of tiles x−2…x+1, y−2…y+1 into a throwaway RenderTexture the size of the area, which clips the lines to it, and stamps that onto `mapRt`; the lines just outside the area are therefore not drawn a second time. A redraw of unchanged tiles leaves the area pixel-identical (checked on 120 tiles, 2026-11-09). The wall pieces drawn with an upward offset (`dy`) that reach outside the area are stacked again by a redraw, as before #289.
- Autotiling follows the pack's 3/4 style:
  - walls with floor to the south show bricks (`wall_mid`, occasional holes and banners), with a rim (`wall_top_mid`) on the cell above
  - side walls show a light strip on the edge facing the floor (`wall_edge_mid_left/right`); south walls show the rim (`wall_top_mid`) at the top of their cell, next to the floor. Both hug the floor, so every junction reads as a corner, never a T
  - corners: a side wall meeting a brick face's rim uses `wall_edge_bottom_*`; a room's top corners use a stub (`wall_outer_top_*`) above the strip; bottom corners use the stub lifted to the top of the cell
  - `TileDraw.dy` lifts rim and stub sprites (their art sits in the bottom 4 rows) to the top of a cell; it only moves transparent rows outside the cell, so redrawing single cells stays correct
  - the cells behind a wall are usually `Void`, not `Wall` (the server only walls cells next to floor), so neighbour checks must not require `isWall` there
- The boss hall entrance has no door: open and sealed entrance tiles both draw as floor. When it is sealed, `forcefield.ts` draws a shimmering blue force field over it (a runtime effect; the pack has no such sprite): a pulsing translucent wall with drifting light bands and glitter sparks, a curtain on horizontal entrances and an edge-on band on vertical ones. `PhaserRunView.syncForceField` builds it from the map, so players who join or spectate later see it too. Both orientations are in the gallery (`?gallery`).

**Terrain (`terrain.ts`).**
- `TerrainLayer` draws every terrain cell of the map once (the floor under it comes from the board): animated base textures plus edge overlays, a red glow over lava and rising embers. `autotile.ts` treats terrain as floor-like, so walls next to it get their faces and rims; the grid lines skip terrain.
- `PhaserRunView.terrainState` handles each figure: `sink` (wading: feet cut off in shallow water and lava, not while dashing), `WadeFx` (splashes on entering, ripples while wading, embers on a hero in lava), and the fall / drown animation (`fall`, `drown`, `bubbleFx`) from `Ev::Sink`, or at once for the own hero when the predictor sees it sinking. The demos use the same helpers.

**Figures (`anim/defs.ts` → `EntityView`).**
- `FigureDef` describes:
  - idle/run/hit frame prefixes (4-frame pack animations)
  - the weapon frame and how it is held (`blade` / `bow` / `staff`)
  - the hand height, an accent colour (`base`, used by spell particles and dash afterimages) and a footprint radius (`baseR`, the ground shadow size)
  - an optional scale or tint
  - an optional `swing` (reach and arc of the melee swoosh, matching the server's damage reach)
  - optional `mouth` / `nostrils` frame points
  - for figures with their own action frames (the dragon, the demon): `frames` (prefix, count, fps per action), `windups` (attack anim → wind-up seconds), `lift` (hover height), `footY` (the feet row when the frames leave room below), `hipX` (the hips' column when the figure is not centred in its frames), `bite`; `pickFrame.ts` chooses their frame
- Pack stand-ins:
  - the demon is not a pack figure: it has its own AI-generated frames (see Demon frames above), drawn at ×1
  - the lich is not a pack figure: it has its own frames (see Lich frames above), drawn at ×1
  - the summoner is the pack necromancer with a red robe (an atlas recolor, see above)
  - the dragon is not a pack figure: it has its own AI-generated frames (see Dragon frames above), drawn at ×1.2
- Weapons are drawn at 0.6× (`WEAPON_SCALE`).
- The pack has no attack frames, so attacks are approximated:

| Attack | How it looks |
|---|---|
| Melee (blade) | Weapon fades out at the swing start angle; a crescent swoosh sweeps the damage arc at the damage reach; the weapon fades in at the end angle. Small lunge and tilt of the body. |
| Bow / crossbow | Recoil and spark. The assassin's dagger is a short thrust. |
| Staff / cast | Staff thrust and sparkles. |
| Wind-up | Body flashes red, shakes, weapon drawn back. |
| Dash | Stretched body and afterimages. |
| Dragon breath | Particles from the mouth (per-frame point from `dragonPoints()`, follows bob, flip, lift and scale) into the ground cone. During the wind-up, smoke comes from the nostrils. |
| Dragon flight | The body rises by `lift` over its ground shadow (which shrinks); the landing draws the slam ring at `DRAGON_SMASH_RADIUS` over `DRAGON_LAND`. |

- Facing is left/right only. The `Dir` type and a `TODO(4-dir)` mark where 4-direction sheets would plug in.

**Figures stand on a soft ground shadow** (`ensureShadowTexture`), not on a miniature base; the bases were removed at the user's request.

**Chests and mimics (`chest.ts`, `mimic.ts`).** The pack has three 3-frame chest animations (`chest_full_open`, `chest_empty_open`, `chest_mimic_open`); frame 0 is the same closed chest in all three, so a mimic cannot be told apart by its look.
- `ChestView`: closed, then the lid opens over the gold (0.3 s), then the empty chest.
- `MimicView`: there are no walk or idle frames, so everything is the three opening frames plus squash and stretch. Hiding: closed chest that rattles now and then when the local hero is within 40 px. Reveal (the `HIDING` flag drops while in view): jump with the lid flying open, gold burst and a red "!". Hop (Move): crouch (lid ajar, squashed), flight (jaws open, stretched, a parabolic arc 7 px high with the shadow left on the floor), landing (squash, dust). Bite (Melee): lunge with the jaws snapping. Wind-up: crouched.
- Coins: `Effects.coins` throws pack `coin_anim` frames out with gravity; a gold "+N coins" floats up.

**Other visuals.**
- Projectiles: `weapon_arrow` for arrows and bolts, additive glows for magic.
- Deaths: a `skull` decal.
- Lich protection beams (a visual only; the lich no longer drains or heals): animated red beams from channelling disciples to the lich. Drawn from `Snapshot.drain` (the lich first, then each channelling disciple; on the wire a flat scaled array, the 8th `SnapW` field), which the server sends during the boss fight, or while the lich or a disciple is in view, regardless of the field of view; the beams are drawn above the darkness (`DEPTH.beam`), so they show even when an end is out of sight.
- The lich's magic is dark purple gloom (`GLOOM` in `effects.ts`): gloom bolts flicker and trail dark smoke and pale sparks (`PROJECTILES[...].gloom`, `Effects.gloomWisp`); the nova telegraph is a purple ring with wisps drawn in (`FigureDef.slam.gloom`), the lich flashes pale purple and holds its staff high during the wind-up, and the burst is `Effects.nova`; its raised skeletons appear in a dark purple puff.
- Debug paths: marching dashed lines above the darkness, yellow to the boss, blue to the ogre, green to the nymph's cove.

**Canvas and zoom (UI polish).**
- The Phaser canvas renders at **screen resolution**: `viewLayout()` in `game/viewLayout.ts` (called by `viewSize()` in `main.ts`) picks an integer zoom (the window divided by 600 x 340, rounded, at least 1) and the view in world pixels (window / zoom); the canvas is view x zoom, and Phaser's own scale zoom is 1. The zoom sits on each scene's main camera (`cam.setZoom`), applied by `applyView` on the scene's `CREATE` event (before its first frame) and again on a resize. Every scene but the game has origin (0, 0) so the world's top left stays the screen's top left; `GameScene` keeps the centred camera. `LightsDemoScene` fits its own zoom.
- Why: text in the world is Inter and needs real pixels. The old canvas was the view in world pixels (640 x 360 at 1280 x 720) scaled up by CSS, which turned 6.5 px text into a smear. World sizes are still in world pixels; the sprites stay crisp because the game keeps `pixelArt` (NEAREST).
- Cost: the canvas has zoom squared times more pixels (4x at 1280 x 720). The smoke test's frame rate in headless swiftshader fell from about 27 to about 15. Measured on the user's PC with `?res=world` (below, #295), woodland start, capped at 60: screen resolution 58 FPS, frame 4.07 ms; world resolution 60 FPS, frame 1.63 ms (uncapped readings were inconclusive). **Decision (the user, 2026-11-09): the canvas stays at screen resolution; no lower render resolution, because world text becomes unreadable** (#62 closed). Frame-time work goes elsewhere.
- **`?res=world`** (#295, debug accounts only): `viewLayout(..., 'world')` makes the canvas the view in world pixels and puts the integer zoom on Phaser's `scale.zoom` (a CSS upscale, the layout from before the screen-resolution canvas); the cameras get zoom 1, so the same world area shows. `requestedResolution(location.search, Welcome.debug)` decides it on `Welcome`, and `relayout` in `startGame` (also the resize handler) applies it with `scale.setZoom`, `scale.resize` and `applyView`; the URL alone does nothing on a normal account. World text is blurry in this mode, as expected. The F3 panel's `canvas` line shows which mode runs. The demo pages (no account) always use screen resolution.

**World texts (`game/worldText.ts`, `game/keycap.ts`, `Effects.text`).**
- All text drawn in the world uses Inter (`FONT_TEXT`). `WORLD_TEXT` holds the sizes and styles in world pixels (tag 6.5 px at 600, number 7 px at 800, crit 9 px at 800 in gold `#ffd24a`, mark 7 px for the sleep Z and the "!", sound 6 px italic, small 6 px for "+N coins" and "IMMUNE"); at zoom 2 that is about 13 screen pixels for a name tag. `TEXT_RESOLUTION` (4) is the text texture resolution.
- `smoothText(t)` sets LINEAR filtering on a Text's texture and again after every `updateText`, because `pixelArt` makes text NEAREST, which aliases when a 4x texture is drawn smaller.
- `Effects.text(x, y, s, color, opts)` takes `{ big, depth, stroke, kind }` (`kind` is a `WORLD_TEXT` key). A crit is its number alone in gold at the crit size, with a short pop (`popScale`: 1.4 easing to 1 in 0.12 s); the old "CRIT" word is gone.
- `KeyCap` (`game/keycap.ts`) is the use hint: a gold key cap with a dark "E" and an optional white label ("Wake") to its right. `set(label | null)` shows it (null: the bare cap, at a chest or door), `hide()` hides it.
- Two text resolution policies exist: world texts use the fixed `TEXT_RESOLUTION`, the rules-log tags use zoom x camera zoom x devicePixelRatio (TODO).

**Fonts.** Self-hosted, no CDN: `client/public/fonts/` holds Inter (variable, weights 100 to 900, normal and italic, latin subset) and IM Fell English SC (400, headings only), both from the Fontsource npm packages (5.3.0) with their OFL licence texts. `client/src/fonts.css` has the `@font-face` rules and is imported by `style.css` and `admin/admin.css`. Family strings: text `'Inter', system-ui, sans-serif` (`--font-text`, `FONT_TEXT`), heading `'IM Fell English SC', Georgia, serif` (`--font-head`, `FONT_HEAD`). `loadFonts()` in `fonts.ts` loads every face Phaser draws with and is awaited before `new Phaser.Game`, so canvas text never renders in a fallback; it never rejects and gives up after 3 s. `fonts.test.ts` fails if the CSS, HTML or font code mentions `googleapis`, `gstatic`, Press Start or JetBrains. Sizes: body 15 px, small 13 px, smallest 11.5 px, HUD 13 px, nothing in the HTML UI below 11 px; digits that change in place use `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`.

**UI.** DOM overlay (`ui/`):
- Roster, character creation and the character hub with its four tabs (§11a "The character screens"), then the lobby with the run list and the waiting room.
- HUD (`ui/hud.ts`): HP, two ability slots, party list, boss bar with the IMMUNE tag, messages, spectate banner, Esc menu with Settings, run coin counter (pack coin icon via CSS from the atlas), end screen with stats, XP and coins, F3 debug panel, debug badge.
  - **Cooldown ring** (`ui/cooldown.ts`): the slot's gold rim fills clockwise from the top as the cooldown runs down, with the seconds left (`toFixed(1)`) in the slot. `slotView(cd, max)` gives fill, ready and label; `CooldownTracker` learns the slot's maximum from the values it sees (a new use, the Berserker speed-up) and reports `flash` on the update where the slot becomes ready (a short flash).
  - **Effect symbols** (`ui/effectIcons.ts`): one 16 x 16 SVG body per effect, a fill or a line style and a "weak" variant (outline or thinner line) for the lesser tier (Singed, Chilled, Tainted). `EFFECT_ICON` is a `Record` typed by the effect ids, so `tsc` fails on a missing symbol. `effectIconSvg(id, px)` feeds the HUD (own hero with the whole seconds left, party list without), `effectIconDataUri` feeds the board textures (`iconTextureKey`, loaded in `BootScene`). The letters and `effectGlyph` are gone.
  - **Name tags** (`game/tagEffects.ts`, `TagEffects`): a pooled row of symbol images to the right of a hero's name tag (5.5 world px each, the first 2 px from the tag and 1 px between symbols); other heroes only, the own hero has no tag. No effect words are drawn on the board.
  - **Messages:** `message(text, 'game' | 'notice')`. The text is set with `textContent` (server messages carry player names). `game` is the heading face in the centre (force field, and everything that comes from the server as `Ev::Msg`, such as "{name} left the dungeon" and the debug effect line of F8); `notice` is the small client line (debug on or off, "You have fallen").
  - **Asleep panel:** `setAsleep(on)` shows a panel with a moon above the HP bar (`RunSession.frame` calls it from the own effects), the successor of the old board hint. The "E Wake" over a sleeping teammate is the board's `KeyCap` with the label "Wake".
  - **Help line and menu:** key caps (`helpHtml`, `<kbd>`), the Esc menu titled "Paused" with a hint.
  - **Settings** (`ui/settings.ts`): the Esc menu's Settings view, one checkbox row per `Toggle { label, key, get, set }` and a Back button. `RulesLog.settings()` supplies Rules log (L) and Calculation lines (Shift+L), `GameScene.create` passes them to `Hud.setToggles`, and the Hud adds Network stats (F3). The keys and the checkboxes stay in sync because the view re-reads the toggles (`sync`) when it opens and on each frame while open. This adds one line to the rules-log boundary (now 7 lines outside `src/rulesLog/`).

### The run session and the view interface (#302)

A run's logic and its pictures are apart. `RunSession` (`client/src/run/session.ts`) holds everything that decides what is true in a run and imports no Phaser; a view draws what the session hands it. Three pieces: the session, a view behind the `RunView` interface (`run/view.ts`), and a host that owns the frame loop and the raw keys and creates both. The 2D game is `GameScene` (host: keys, click latches, `readInput`, the `FrameTimer`, the Esc, Q, E and F keys as calls on the session) plus `PhaserRunView` (view).

**What the session owns.** The `TileMap` and the `LightField`; the `Predictor` and the `Interpolator`; the entity map; the last snapshot; alive, spectating and own flags; the disabled slots; the debug data (`DebugDraw`). The network subscription and its messages (`onMessage`: `Snap`, `HeroChanged`, `RunEnded`, `DebugPath`, `DebugDodge`); the events that change state (`onEvent`: `Tile` changes the map and the light field, then tells the view; `Light`, `LightAdd`, `LightRemove`, `Msg`, the HUD part of `Coins`); the fixed step and ability prediction; the own predicted shots (`Shots`, `run/shots.ts`); and every call into the HUD, so any view has the whole HUD. The debug keys (F4, F7, F8, F9) are methods on the session (`debugDarkvision` and so on), which ignores them outside a debug run and while dead.

**One frame.** The host calls `RunSession.frame(deltaMs)` once per display frame. It runs the fixed steps (at most five), samples the interpolator, works out the own hero as drawn (`OwnHero`: predicted, or the interpolated hero while charmed) and the camera target (the own hero, or the watched teammate), advances the shots, picks the use hint, then calls `RunView.frame(RunFrame)` and passes the `ViewStats` it returns to `Hud.frame` with its own numbers.

**The interface.**
- `RunView.frame(f)` draws one frame and returns the numbers for the F3 panel (`ViewStats`; `NO_STATS` for a view that has none). `RunFrame` carries `dt` and `now`, the interpolated entities, the own hero, the spectated teammate, the camera target, the last snapshot (lights, looks, drain, `me.vision`, `me.darkvision`), the predicted shots and which server shots to hide, the use hint and the debug lines.
- `event(ev, own)` takes the events that only have a picture (`PictureEv`: `Dmg`, `Heal`, `Boom`, `Zap`, `Died`, `Sink`, `Raise`, `Immune`, `Effect`, `Coins`); camera shake is the view's reaction to them. `tileChanged(x, y, v)` comes after the session has updated the map and the light field. `shotEnded(shot, how)` ends a predicted shot's picture. `destroy()` frees the view.
- A view is made from `RunViewInit` (`session.viewInit()`: the run's start info, the session's map and light field, shared and read only for the view, and the own kind) and given to the session with `setView`; with no view the session still runs and the HUD still gets its frame.

**Rules.**
- The session asks the view one question only: `pointerWorld()`, the simulation px under the pointer (or `null`), for the aim. Everything else flows from the session to the view.
- Picture-only state stays in the view: entity views, wading and sinking, the vanish and reappear marks, skull decals, bar labels, the force field's age, the rules log's panel and tags. The session does not keep what only a picture needs.
- The interface grows by fields and events, never by back doors. A view that needs one more datum gets a field on `RunFrame` or `RunViewInit`, or a new event; it does not reach into the session, and the session does not reach into a view.
- A `RunFrame` and the objects in it (the entity map, `own`, `shots`, the debug arrays) are valid only during the call: the session reuses them, so a view copies what it keeps.
- Lights and sight reach a view as data it already has: the shared `LightField` and the snapshot's lights and vision numbers. The view makes its own picture of sight (the Phaser view with `LightView` and `VisionMask`).

**How the 3D view plugs in** (#302, "The 3D run" below). Its host builds a `RunSession` with its own `input` function (a `StepInput` per fixed step, from its keys; `dir8` in `run/input.ts` rounds a direction to the eight the wire carries), creates its `RunView`, calls `session.setView(view)` and then `session.frame(deltaMs)` each frame, and calls `session.destroy()` and `view.destroy()` at the end. A view without a pointer (the 3D view until #311) returns `null` from `pointerWorld()`; the aim then follows the movement direction. `RunSession.receive(m)` hands the session a message by hand, through the same handler as the net's (the 3D run replays the messages that arrived while its code loaded). `run/` and `render3d/` import no Phaser, which `imports.test.ts` checks (§12); the session's HUD is DOM, so a run in the 3D view has the whole HUD without extra code. `run/testing.ts` has the fakes for a view's own tests.

### The 3D view (#301, #302)

Work packages 1 and 2 of the 3D renderer (#300). Two pages: `?r3d=demo` is #301's dev scene (the reference demo's hall, plus a corridor 3 tiles wide seen across, one seen along, and a second room's near wall), with no server; `?r3d` is a real run drawn in 3D, for debug accounts only (#302, "The 3D run" below). The Phaser view stays the game's view until #310. Everything is in `client/src/render3d/`.

**Library and boundaries.**
- `three` is pinned to exactly `0.187.0` (`@types/three` the same), with no caret. The code imports it only as `three/webgpu` and `three/tsl`; a bare `three` import would load a second build.
- The folder imports no Phaser, directly or through another module. Its imports from outside are Phaser-free shared modules: `../ui/atlas` (the atlas loader), `../game/frameTimer`, `../run/` (the session), `../sim/map` (`TileMap`), `../game/terrain-layers` and `../game/terrain-codes` (terrain frames), `../game/woodland/ground` (the ground painter), `../game/anim/` data (the class figures). `imports.test.ts` enforces this by reading the files: it follows every non-type import of each file in `render3d/` and `run/` transitively and fails if a chain reaches `phaser` (§12), so an import inside `ui/atlas.ts` or `game/frameTimer.ts` that pulls Phaser in is caught too; the folder's bare specifiers are only `three/webgpu` and `three/tsl`.
- `main.ts` reaches the folder only through `import('./render3d/boot')`: for `?r3d=demo` at once, before anything of Phaser is created; for `?r3d` on `Welcome` of a debug account (preloaded) and on `RunStarted`. three.js is its own chunk and normal play never downloads it (`imports.test.ts` checks that `main.ts` has no static import of it). Without `r3d` in the URL `main.ts` creates the `Phaser.Game` at once, exactly as before; with `r3d` it creates it on `Welcome` only when the 3D run is not wanted (`ensurePhaser`), so a `?r3d` run of a debug account never creates Phaser.
- `WebGPURenderer` uses WebGPU where the browser offers it and falls back to WebGL2 by itself; `backend=webgl` in the URL forces the fallback. The panel's readout names the backend in use.

**Structure.** Everything that can be a plain function is one and has a vitest test: `camera.ts`, `quality.ts`, `params.ts`, `heights.ts`, `demoMap.ts`, `worldMesh.ts`, `terrainMesh.ts`, `fieldMesh.ts`, `groundJob.ts`, `scene.ts`, `cards.ts`, `light.ts`, `recover.ts`, `stats.ts`; `urlWriter.ts`; `panel.ts` is plain DOM with a jsdom test. Three files use three.js: `view.ts` (the renderer, the chunk meshes, the render target; rebuilt whole after a lost device) and the TSL in `materials.ts` and `lightNodes.ts`, whose nodes and uniforms are made anew for every view.

**Host and stages** (`host.ts`). `startHost(el, search, mode, makeStage)` owns the canvas, the one frame loop, the held keys, the panel, the F3 readout, the parameters and the recovery; a **stage** (`Stage`) owns what is drawn: its `world` (a `WorldInput`: the `TileMap`, whether the ground is painted, the clear colour), `built(view)` after every (re)build, `frame(ctx)` per frame, `key(e)` for keys the host does not handle (when it returns true the host calls `preventDefault`), `stats()` for F3 and `dispose()`. `demoStage.ts` is #301's scene, `runStage.ts` the run. The stage's state and the parameters live outside the view and survive a rebuild.
- `Host.stop()` can be called at any point, also while the host is still starting (then `startHost` returns `null`): it cancels the frame, removes its key and blur listeners, cancels a pending address-bar write, disposes the view, removes the canvas, the panel, the readout and its error boxes, calls `stage.dispose()` and deletes `window.__r3d` if that is still its own. A second host on the same element therefore starts clean (a second run in one page). `startHost` checks for a stop after each await; a throwing `makeStage` or first `built` stops the host before the error goes on. An error box a failed host leaves up stays for the player to read until the next host starts on the element or the run's `stop()` (`clearErrors`).
- Keys typed into an `input`, `textarea` or `select` are not the stage's. F3 asks `stage.stats()` first: the run's stage opens the HUD's panel instead of the host's readout.

**Coordinates and camera** (`camera.ts`).
- World units are tiles: `x` east, `z` south, `y` up; 16 art px are 1 tile. Gameplay stays 2D on the plane `y = 0`.
- The camera is orthographic. Pitch is the angle between the view direction and the ground; yaw turns it around `y`. With `c = cos(yaw)`, `s = sin(yaw)` and a point measured from the camera's target: `xr = dx*c + dz*s`, `zr = dz*c - dx*s`, screen `x' = xr`, `y' = -zr*sin(pitch) + y*cos(pitch)`. `project` is tested against a three.js `OrthographicCamera` built the way `view.ts` builds it (on the view ray behind the target, up `(0, 1, 0)`), and its inverse on the ground, `groundAt`, against `project`. The camera's distance is `50 + halfH / tan(pitch)` tiles, so the near plane never cuts the bottom of the screen at a low pitch; an orthographic picture does not depend on the distance.
- Zoom is a whole number of screen px per art px, picked from the window (`pickZoom`, 2 to 8) or forced with `zoom=N`.
- `keysToWorld` turns WASD into world directions by the yaw; `facesLeft` decides a figure's facing on screen with a small dead zone, so it does not flip while walking straight away from the camera.

**Pixel handling.**
- **Screen-space snap** (`snapGround`): the camera's target and every card's foot are moved so that their screen position, measured from the world origin, is a whole number of art px at the current pitch and yaw. (Rounding to 1/16 tile in the world, as the reference demo does, is a whole pixel only without tilt.)
- **Cards** (figures, crates): a quad standing on its foot, turned to the camera's yaw. The vertex stage stretches its height by `1 / cos(pitch)`, so it shows its full sprite height on screen; its light uses the unstretched position. Torch flames lie in their wall's plane and are not stretched. A glow is an additive quad that faces the camera, so it is round on screen. `buildDynamic` moves its centre toward the camera along the view ray by its own size plus 0.05 tiles (`GLOW_LIFT`), which in an orthographic view changes only its depth: the whole disc then lies in front of the torch's wall, the wall top and the floor. (Left at the torch, the quad leans into the wall and loses half its halo; an upright quad stretched by the pitch dips below the floor instead.) The cost: a glow is added over a figure standing within about its size in front of a torch, and with the camera looking along a wall the part of a torch's disc that lies over the wall is still hidden. In the same way, and as in the reference demo, cards are moved 0.03 tiles and flames 0.05 tiles toward the camera (`materials.ts`), so they win against the surface they stand in front of.
- **Textures** are nearest-filtered with no mipmaps, `flipY = false` and `premultiplyAlpha = false`; uv coordinates are in atlas px with the frame's top row at the top (`uv / atlasSize`, no flip).
- **Smoothed ground** (a switch, on by default; the user's pick after the hardware check): floors read a second, linear-filtered copy of the atlas at a position sharpened to the texel borders (`seam + clamp((p - seam) / fwidth(p), -0.5, 0.5)`), clamped half a texel inside the frame's rect because the atlas has no padding. Apart from the off-screen target's texture when it is copied at a ratio that is not a whole number, this is the one texture that is not nearest. The switch is a uniform, so toggling it compiles nothing; walls, wall tops and cards are never smoothed.
- The wall top is generated in the shader: dark stone with a pale rim where the top borders something lower (from the height texture). The user kept this look for #302 (2026-11-11); it is tuned once the whole 3D view stands.

**Quality levels** (`quality.ts`). `effZoom` gives the render px per art px: High the screen's zoom; Fast 2; Balanced the largest divisor of the zoom up to half of it, at least 2 (else half the zoom, rounded up). `renderPlan` turns that into one frame's sizes: at the screen's zoom the scene is drawn straight to the canvas; below it, into an off-screen target that is copied to the canvas with one quad, nearest when the ratio is a whole number and linear otherwise. The copy is centred and is up to `zoom - 1` px larger than the canvas, so it may start up to 4 px outside it. The viewport is passed through unclamped: WebGL2 and Chromium's WebGPU crop it; other WebGPU implementations are not verified (if one drops the copy, offset the uv in the copy's node instead). There is no pixel grid mode (dropped by the user, 2026-11-11).

**Colour.** The reference demo's numbers are raw values, so the renderer converts nothing: `outputColorSpace = LinearSRGBColorSpace`, `NoToneMapping`, and every texture has `colorSpace = NoColorSpace`. A later package may revisit this when effects need linear light.

**Light** (`light.ts`, `lightNodes.ts`): the proof that TSL carries this renderer.
- `lightAt(P, N, wrap)` is ambient plus up to 8 point lights (two `uniformArray`s of 8 `Vector4`, updated in place every frame): a smooth falloff to the radius, the surface angle softened by `wrap` (floor 0.35, wall face 0.5, wall top 0.15, cards 1 with no normal), and a soft shoulder on the result.
- `wallVis(P, L)` is the wall shadow: a grid walk from the lit point to the light over a one-channel texture of wall heights (one texel per tile, read with `textureLoad`), at most 40 steps, started 0.03 tiles along the normal. It is a TSL `Loop(40)` with `Break()`, ported line by line from the demo's GLSL, and it compiles unchanged to GLSL (WebGL2) and WGSL (WebGPU).
- The scene has six wall torches with the demo's flicker and a moving orb; switches turn the wall shadows and the orb off.

**Lost device or context** (`recover.ts`, `view.ts`, `host.ts`). three.js 0.187 reports a loss (`renderer.onDeviceLost`) and then stops; it restores nothing. So the view is rebuilt whole on a new canvas: renderer, textures, target and meshes.
- `createRecovery` runs one rebuild at a time. A loss reported while one runs leads to one more rebuild after it; a rebuild that fails is logged (`console.warn`) and tried again after 1 s.
- Each view reports its loss through the generation it was made in, so a late report from a replaced view is ignored. A loss before boot has finished only sets a flag and starts the recovery at boot's end.
- `Renderer.dispose()` is async in three.js: the lost renderer's disposal is fire-and-forget with its rejection caught, and a `createView` that fails disposes its own renderer.
- While a rebuild runs, the scene keeps stepping, drawing is skipped and the readout says "Graphics device lost, restoring…". The frame loop asks for its next frame first and catches an exception from the frame's body (logged once per message), so one throw, for example from a context that is lost but not yet reported, does not stop the page. The panel's `Lose device (debug)` button forces a loss (WebGPU: `device.destroy()`, reported by hand because three ignores the reason `destroyed`; WebGL2: `WEBGL_lose_context`).

**Parameters and the address bar** (`params.ts`, `urlWriter.ts`, `host.ts`). `parseParams` gives the default or the clamped value for anything unreadable, so no NaN reaches the camera. `toSearch(p, current)` writes this page's keys and keeps every other key of the address as it is (`?lag=150`, `?res=…`, a key without a value). Every change, from the panel, F3 or a stage's `host.setParams`, goes through one `apply` in `host.ts`, which keeps the parameters, the panel's copy, the F3 readout and the timer in step. The address bar is written by `trailingWriter` 200 ms after the last change, because browsers throttle `history.replaceState` and would drop the final value of a slider drag. A renderer that cannot be created or initialised throws `RendererInitError`, and only that shows the "needs WebGPU or WebGL2" message; any other start-up error shows its own text.

**Draw calls.** three's `info.render.drawCalls` adds up over a frame and is reset by three's own animation loop. `view.ts` switches `info.autoReset` off, resets the counter at the start of `draw` and reads it once after the last render, so the number is this frame's (the scene's calls plus one for the copy).

**What a headless run can show.** The headless WebGL2 path (SwiftShader) draws the scene, and `tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs` uses it. A WebGPU canvas cannot be captured headless on the development machine: with the SwiftShader adapter it reads back blank, with the real graphics card scrambled, and a raw WebGPU triangle without three.js does the same. A WebGPU run there shows only that the shaders compile and frames run without errors; the WebGPU picture is checked on a real screen. A headed Playwright window on the real graphics card (`headless: false`) draws WebGL2 correctly at the real frame rate, but its WebGPU capture is black too.

### World geometry and the 3D run (#302)

The 3D view draws any map the server generates, inside a real run. The design and the plan are on issue #302.

**One rule per tile kind** (`heights.ts`, `worldMesh.ts`). Every cell has a height and a surface; nothing depends on the level.
- Height (`cellHeight`, read only through `heightAt`): `Wall` and `Void` are `WALL_H` (2 tiles), every other tile 0; outside the map counts as `Void`. `Void` is solid rock, as it looks in 2D: no wall shows a face toward the outside, and between `Wall` and `Void` there is no face. The height per cell is the hook for #167 (heights in levels) and #38 (pillars).
- A cell of height 2 gets a top quad (kind `top`) and, on each side whose neighbour is lower, one face quad per tile of height (kind `face`). Face frames: `wall_mid`; on a straight run (the cells beside it along the wall have a full face on the same side) the lower row now and then `wall_hole_1`, `wall_hole_2` or `wall_goo`, the upper row now and then a banner, by a hash of cell and side (`faceFrame`; shares 2.5 %, 2 %, 1.5 %, banners 1.5 %), on all four sides.
- A cell of height 0: nothing for terrain (`isTerrain`: it is drawn by `terrainMesh.ts`); in a woodland run a ground quad for `GROUND_TILES` (`Floor`, `Road`, `Thicket`, `Trunk`, `Barrier`; kind `ground`, uv in the painted image's px); else a floor quad, `floor_1` mostly and `floor_2`–`floor_8` by hash (`floorFrame`, 7 %). No per-tile tint, grid lines or table frame.
- The wall-height texture (`heightBytes`, one byte per tile, `round(height * 100)`) feeds the top's rim and `wallVis`; a tile change rewrites its texel.

**Chunks.** The map is cut into 16 by 16 tiles (`CHUNK`; edge chunks are narrower, a map smaller than one chunk is one chunk). `buildChunk(map, painted, cx, cz, frames)` is a pure function returning the arrays of floor, ground, faces and tops; `view.ts` makes one mesh per chunk with a bounding box and three's frustum culling (`chunks.drawn` counts what three drew last frame; on a dungeon about 6 of 90 to 110). A tile change (`View.tileChanged`) rebuilds the chunks of the cell and its eight neighbours that lie in the grid (`chunksOfTile`, up to nine, because a face's straight-run test and the terrain edges read diagonal cells), the height texel and the force field. Today the only tile changes are the boss hall's entrance.

**Terrain** (`terrainMesh.ts`). A terrain cell's layers are `terrainLayers` of the 2D view (`game/terrain-layers.ts`, Phaser-free), stacked flat on the ground 0.002 tiles apart (`LAYER_STEP`): the base frames (water; deep is water plus the deep overlay; lava; chasm), then the edge overlays from the eight neighbours. Each chunk with terrain has its own mesh (same material, kind `floor`, lit like the floor). The animated base quads are listed per chunk; `stepTerrainUv` rewrites their uv and rect when a set's frame steps (`TERRAIN_FPS`: water and deep 4 per second, lava 5), only for chunks drawn in the last frame. If the stacked layers shimmer at a low pitch on real hardware, the fallback is draw order.

**The force field placeholder** (`fieldMesh.ts`). Over every `DoorClosed` cell a translucent blue box of wall height (colour `(0.3, 0.55, 1.0)`, alpha 0.35, unlit, depth-tested but not written, `renderOrder` 2), with sides only toward cells lower than a wall and not themselves sealed, plus its top. Rebuilt on every tile change. The message "A force field seals the hall behind you!" comes from the session, so both views show it. #308 replaces the box.

**The woodland's ground** (`groundJob.ts`). The run paints the ground with `GroundPainter` (`game/woodland/ground.ts`) and the run's seed, in row bands nearest the spawn first (`bandOrder`), then blades, then flowers, at most `GROUND_BUDGET_MS` (4 ms) per frame and at least one unit per step. Until it is done the ground shows a flat grass colour (`GRASS_FLAT`, uniform `groundReady` 0); then `View.setGround` uploads the whole image once (`map.w * 16` by `map.h * 16` px, nearest plus a linear copy for the smoothed lookup; the two texture nodes are `mergeable = false`, or three merges them into one binding and the smoothing switch does nothing). An image larger than the device's texture limit throws `GroundTooLargeError`, shown in the host's error box; the run keeps the flat grass. After a lost device the kept pixels are uploaded again, not painted again.

**Clear colour.** Dungeon: `stoneClear(ambient)`, the colour a wall top renders without point lights, so beyond the map's edge the rock goes on; woodland: `WOODLAND_CLEAR`, a dark green. three's clear does not reach the canvas where nothing is drawn (WebGL2 on the real graphics card; SwiftShader does not show it), so the canvas also gets the same colour as its CSS background (`cssColour`), written when it changes.

**Light in this package.** Dungeon light is #304. The run has two lights, switched in the panel (`light` in the URL): the **work light** (default, and always in the woodland): ambient `WORK_AMBIENT` 0.9, no point lights, so the whole map is visible; the **lantern**: #301's dungeon ambient and one point light at the camera target (height 1, radius 9, colour (1.0, 0.82, 0.55)) with wall shadows by the panel's switch. The ambient is a uniform (`Lighting.setAmbient`).

**The 3D run.** `?r3d` is honoured only for debug accounts (`wants3d(search, Welcome.debug)`, like `?res=world`); on any other account it is ignored and the run opens in 2D. Debug runs start immortal and debug accounts play only with each other.
- `main.ts` preloads `render3d/boot` on `Welcome`. On `RunStarted` it hides the lobby, stops an old 3D run, buffers every server message from that moment, awaits the module, and calls `startRun3d(gameEl, search, { info, net, hud, myClass, early })`. `startRun3d` creates the `RunSession` and replays the buffered messages into it at once (`session.receive`), so nothing that arrives while three.js loads is lost (a sealed entrance, the debug message, `RunEnded`). A second `RunStarted` while the import is pending drops the stale one (a counter). A failed import returns to the lobby with an error. `backToLobby` stops the 3D run when there is one, else the Phaser scene.
- Until the host's first view exists, a 16 ms interval steps the session, so prediction and the HUD run from the start; the host's loop takes over at its first frame. If the host cannot start (no WebGPU or WebGL2), its error box stays and the session and HUD keep running; the player leaves through the Esc menu.
- `runStage.ts`: the stage's `world` is the session's live `TileMap` (`painted` and `clear` by `info.woodland`), so a rebuild after a lost device draws the current map, a sealed entrance included. Keys: W A S D and the arrows move (`moveInput`: the screen direction turned by the yaw, rounded to the eight wire directions by `dir8`); Esc the menu; Q and E watch teammates after death; F toggles the free camera; F3 the HUD's panel (`session.toggleStats`). No aiming, abilities, act or debug keys (#311). `StepInput.held` is true while any movement key is down; under the free camera the hero gets no movement.
- `runView3d.ts` (`RunView3d`, the run's `RunView`): the **follow camera** eases toward the session's camera target (own hero, or the watched teammate) with `1 - exp(-dt * 6)`, clamped by `followClamp` so the screen's footprint on the ground stays inside the map at any turn (margins `|cos yaw| * halfW + |sin yaw| * hd` and `|sin yaw| * halfW + |cos yaw| * hd` with `hd = halfH / sin(pitch)`; centred on an axis the view exceeds; at turn 0 the 2D rule). The **free camera** pans with the movement keys in screen terms, 12 tiles per second (36 with Shift), kept inside the map (`freeClamp`); switching back eases from where it was. **Figures and shots** are `Figures3d` (`figures.ts`, #303): per entity one `PoseMemory`, `FigureStates.stateOf` for the drawn state and `figurePose` (`game/anim/pose.ts`, shared with the 2D `EntityView`) for the look, turned into cards (`cards.ts`): a body card, a weapon card (`WEAPON_SCALE`, pushed `±WEAPON_BIAS` toward or away from the camera when the weapon is in front of or behind the body), a floor shadow, a lying body in the floor decals when asleep, translucent figures (`pose.alpha < 1`, dash afterimages, the fading image of a figure that went invisible) in the sorted ghost pass, a skull decal after a death. The pose's screen aim is `screenAim(yaw, aim) = aim - yaw` (the camera's right vector is `(cos yaw, sin yaw)`), so a figure faces the side it looks to on screen, with 2D's dead zone. `pose.sink` crops the body and lowers it, `pose.drop` lowers it; the aim step is in world tiles, `body` px in the card's plane (the 2D body's constant 1 px below the shadow becomes a move of the foot 1/16 tile toward the camera on the ground: a card lowered into the floor would lose its foot row; `aimStep`'s y is halved for the melee step only, so it is used as it is). Ground shadows are ellipses along screen right and ground-down (`pushShadow` takes the camera); skulls lie flat and upright on screen (`pushFlat`), 2/16 tile up the screen from the death spot; the floor decal pass is translucent (a sleeping incorporeal figure keeps its alpha). Shots: frame shots are cards 0.5 tiles up turned by the screen aim, glow shots are glows of `scale * 7 / 16` tiles; server shots in `hiddenShots` are skipped, the own `f.shots` drawn. `atlasBounds.ts` reads the opaque bounds and the size of a frame from the atlas image (the lying layout needs them). `framePoint(id, points)` is a frame pixel (the dragon's mouth) as a world point. `window.__r3d.figures` is the number of figures drawn. **Objects** come from `game/objectPose.ts` (also used by the 2D views): torches are wall cards (`pushWall`) with a flickering glow, chests and mimics cards with the 2D shadow (13 x 5 px, strength 0.4), barrels cards with `barrelPose`, a mimic's wake, hop and bite from `mimicPose` (`MimicPose.flip` turns it); the coins of a chest or a fallen enemy fly as cards with 2D's launch numbers. Hazards, props and plants are not drawn yet. `pointerWorld()` is `null`; `shotEnded` does nothing yet.
- **Passes** (`view.ts`, by `renderOrder`): opaque cards (body, weapon, chest, barrel, mimic) with depth, then shadows (1), floor decals (2: lying bodies, skulls, translucent), ghosts (3: translucent figures and afterimages, sorted back to front by `sortBackToFront`), glows (4), and the emissive cards (torch flames). `materials.ts` has the card modes tint, fill and grey, a translucent variant and a flat one.
- **Decisions** (#303): the facing uses the screen aim; shadows follow the camera's yaw (depth 0.45 times the radius); skulls lie flat and upright on screen; the standing foot sits 1/16 tile toward the camera (2D's 1 px baseline); wading or lava does not stop a figure opening walls, falling or drowning does. The Shade's veil is left to #308.
- **The see-through** (`openers.ts`, `seeThrough.ts`, the mask pass in `view.ts`, `worldMaterial`): walls open softly around what stands behind them. `opensWalls` says which things open: heroes, Hostile and Friendly creatures (a hiding mimic too, a revealed one as an enemy), chests; Neutral creatures, barrels, torches, hazards, shots, the dead and the sinking do not. `seenOpeners` keeps only those on tiles the hero's sight shows (`runView3d.ts` builds a sight `View`); the light half of "only what may be seen" relies on the server hiding unlit monsters (`run/visibility.rs`) until #309 adds the client light filter, which must apply to the openers too. Each opener draws a camera-facing disc (`discQuads`) of `radius` tiles into a quarter-size HalfFloat mask, MAX-blended: red is the strength (`discStrength`: 1 inside `(1 - edge) * radius`, a smoothstep to 0 at the radius), green is `SEE_DEPTH_BIAS (48) - near` with `near` the depth of the foot measured horizontally along the ground (`nearOf`, clamped to +-`SEE_NEAR_MAX` 40, so the height of a wall behind never counts), alpha the coverage. The wall shader (`wallOpens`) opens a pixel where the strength is above 0 and the wall is more than `SEE_DEPTH_MARGIN` (0.05) tiles nearer than the farthest thing under the disc. Modes: `soft` (the wall's alpha falls to `keep`; `wallKeep(k) = 1 - k * (1 - keep)`), `lattice` (a Bayer 4x4 dither, `bayer4`, at scene-pixel size, coarser at Fast quality) and `off`. Parameters in `SEE_DEFAULTS` (first guesses until the user picks on real hardware): radius 1.3 tiles, keep 0.15, edge 0.4. The lattice goes when the user has chosen (here or in #310).
- **`?r3d=gallery`** (`galleryStage.ts`, `galleryMap.ts`): every figure kind cycling through its states, the status-effect looks, the board objects and shots, and a corridor behind a full-height wall where things walk, on the real path (a synthetic `RunFrame` through `Figures3d`, its openers through `seenOpeners`, into `FrameInput.see`). No server, no login.
- `window.__r3d` in a run also has `hero` and `cam` (tiles) and `chunks` (`total`, `drawn`), for `r3d-run.mjs`.

**The panel in a run** (`panel.ts`, mode `run`). It sits at the bottom left so it does not cover the HUD. Rows: pitch, turn, quality, zoom (Auto, 1 to 4; the URL takes 1 to 8, Auto picks 2 to 8), and the switches Wall shadows, Smoothed ground, Lantern and Free camera. At zoom 1 every quality draws at the screen's zoom. The demo's panel has the zoom row too, and Moving light instead of Lantern and Free camera.

**What a `?r3d` run cannot do yet,** and which package adds it: hazards, plants and the hit sparks and trails of shots (#303, #306; figures, shots and the board objects are drawn); bars, numbers, name tags, hints, the rules log's tags, aiming, abilities and turning controls (#311); dungeon light, torches, lava glow and darkness (#304); sight and haze (#305); the woodland's light, wind and the cove's grass and plants (#306); atmosphere (#307); effects and the real force field (#308).

## 10. Debug and dev features

| URL | Effect |
|---|---|
| Debug accounts (no URL) | The admin marks a game account in `/admin`; `Welcome.debug` turns the debug UI on (hub free buys and Grant, the waiting room's debug pickers). In a debug run (`RunStartInfo.debug`) every hero starts immortal and the server sends `DebugPath` every 0.5 s (A* to the boss, string-pulled with line of sight, plus `ogre`: the same to the living ogre, empty once it is dead, and `cove`: the same to the walkable cove tile nearest the room's centre, empty while the hero stands in the cove (#232)). Client: yellow (boss), blue (ogre) and green (cove) dashed lines plus a badge at the right edge above the help line (clear of the rules log at the bottom left): a title that says immortal or MORTAL (red), then one row per debug key (`DEBUG_KEYS` in `ui/hud.ts`: F4, F7, F8, F9) and per path colour. There is no in-game toggle for debug mode itself and the game reads no `?debug` or `&boss=` parameter; F9 only switches immortality inside a debug run (`DebugImmortal`). F7 (`DebugIncorporeal`) makes the nearest enemy incorporeal so the rules can be tried on any enemy: it vanishes at once, a hit reveals it, it drifts through walls. Smart behaviour (#220): `DebugPath` also carries `notice`, a thin white circle of the notice distance around every living creature smart enough to dodge (drawn at its interpolated position), and each planned dodge sends `DebugDodge` (from, to; only to debug heroes of a debug run) which the client draws as a magenta line fading over 0.6 s. The host picks the boss in the waiting room. |
| F3 (no URL, every account) | The stats panel; see "The F3 panel" below. |
| `?res=world` (debug accounts only) | Renders the canvas at world resolution and upscales it by CSS instead of a screen-resolution canvas, to compare frame and render times in F3 on real hardware (#295; §9). Ignored on other accounts. |
| `?gallery[&state=melee&slow=10]` | Every figure cycling its animation states, no server needed. |

| `?knockback` `[&slow=3]` | Every push in the game, side by side next to chasms and deep water, with the game's distances and decay: orc warrior, chort, imp (no push), a barbarian who cannot dash out, the demon's cleave and swoop, the dragon's tail and front claw. The user approved the distances here. |

Dummy heroes are pushed with the game's distances. Keys: Space pause, S slow motion, 1–8 jump to an action. The user approved the frames here. |

| `?lich` `[&slow=3]` | The lich's magic: the gloom bolt volley and one Raise Dead cast of 3 (two skeletons, an archer) (left), and the nova: the purple telegraph ring at the real radius, the burst, three heroes pushed 64 px (one into the chasm) and one spared just outside. |
| `?ogre` `[&slow=3]` | The ogre's club next to the orc warrior's push (only the club's knocks a hero into the chasm), and the ground slam: the telegraph ring at the real radius, the shockwave, three heroes pushed away (one into a chasm) and one spared just outside. The user approved it. |
| `?daemons[&slow=3]` | Imp, chort and a pack of both fighting a walking knight, and a summoner summoning imps. Uses the real figure definitions; the attack logic is a local imitation of the server AI. No server needed. |
| `?spectres` `[&slow=3]` | The Ghost (alpha pulse, flicker warning, disintegration) and the Shade (veil of strands, trail, silhouette warning, the veil drifting apart) on a scripted board: idle, move, Vanish and the fade, the ambush warning and the touch, death. No server needed. |

Big buttons: Charmed (8 s countdown, loops), Paused (view off, grey effect icon, the Barbarian stands: Dispel or anti-magic), Ends (the view fades out, the heart goes, he walks back). `game/CharmDemoScene.ts`; `node tools/e2e/charm-shots.mjs` takes phone-size screenshots; `tools/e2e/charm-run.mjs` runs one debug account through F8 to Charmed in a real run and prints the view and F3 correction timeline. |

It loops every 4 s, `&slow=N` slows time; `tools/e2e/lights-shot.mjs` takes a screenshot every 0.5 s (a third argument, e.g. `0.5`, sets `&dv=`). V cycles the viewer's darkvision 0 / 50% / 100% and `&dv=0.5` starts with it: the doorway tiles of the dark room it sees only by darkvision turn dim grey (at 50% nothing unlit is in reach, so it looks as at 0). The user approved the look on 2026-10-15 and asked for the changes made then. |
| `?sunlight` | Daylight on a scripted tall room, no server needed (#156): a Daylight light with its beam, floor patch and dust, one knight in the patch and one beside it, a dark corner, an unlit torch and a lit torch in an alcove for comparison, `Mood` on. Each sun gets a random look (Vary); buttons or URL parameters (`vary`, `second`, `shape`, `patch`, `slant`, `from`, `clouds`, `often`, `height`, `style`, `rays`, `glow`, `beam`, `dust`) change the look and add a second crack. See the header of `SunlightDemoScene.ts`. |
| `?cove` | The nymph's cove on a scripted 20x16-tile room, no server needed (#202), zoomed to fit a phone: the big tree, grass carpet with flowers, bushes, tufts and ferns, several suns with beams, pixies around the canopy, and a knight walking a loop behind the tree (tap the room to send it elsewhere; this stops the loop) with its silhouette showing through the leaves. Buttons: Walk, Silhouette, Pixies, Suns 1 / 3 / 5, Beams, Reroll; URL parameters `seed`, `suns`, `pixies`, `silhouette`, `walk`, `beam`. See the header of `CoveDemoScene.ts`. |
| `?woodland` | The forest road on three server-made woodlands, no server needed (#203), zoomed so 32 tiles fit the screen's width (the zoom row steps 16 to 48): ground, trees and bushes, cloud shadows, bees, butterflies, the soft shade and the knight's silhouette behind canopies; tap the map to walk the knight there (trunks and thickets stop him). Buttons (or URL parameters) switch Clouds, Bees, Butterflies, Silhouette, Shade, Shadows and Haze, step to the next seed, change the zoom and change the cloud Size, Speed, Dark and Cover with - and + (the values are shown). The sun knobs: Sun L/R (side) and elev - / + (5° steps, 20 to 85); the shadow knob: shadow - / + (alpha, 0.04 steps); the haze knobs: outside - / + (the unseen strength beyond sight), soft, blur (0.5 px steps), desat and mix - / + and feather (the mask's blur radius in world px, 8 px steps, 0 to 64; 0 is the old hard edge) and ramp (the distance behind a trunk over which the haze grows from nothing to soft, world px, 8 px steps, 0 to 64; 0 is crisp). The demo uses the real `WoodlandView`, `VisionMask` and `UnseenFX`, so what is tuned there is what the game gets; a sun change or the next seed clears the plant pages, the frames and the shadow layer and queues every plant again. `&kind=round|wide|tall|conifer|bare` turns every tree of the map into that kind (bushes stay; `forceKind` in `woodland/demoMath.ts`), to inspect one kind in place. URL: `?woodland&seed=2&clouds=0&bees=0&butterflies=0&silhouette=0&shade=0&shadows=0&haze=0&side=L&elev=45&shadow=0.28&outside=0.8&soft=0.7&blur=1.5&desat=0.6&mix=0.45&feather=24&ramp=24`. The old "beyond sight" dim knob is gone (replaced by the haze). |
| `?mood` | The dungeon air on a scripted room, no server needed: warm torch light, dust in the lit parts, torch and lava smoke, embers, a fireball trailing and bursting into embers, a water pool with drips, and a thin haze. Buttons switch dust, smoke, embers, haze, drips, Stir, Draft and Respawn; a knight loop with a dash every 6 s, an arrow and a fireball stir the air (`?wind=1` draws the wind field, `?stir=0`, `?draft=0`, `?respawn=<s>`). |
| `?r3d=demo` | The 3D view's dev scene (#301, §9 "The 3D view"), no server and no Phaser: a torch-lit hall with corridors, figures on cards and a knight that walks with WASD or the arrow keys (turned by the camera's yaw). A compact, see-through panel at the bottom with a Hide button: camera pitch (slider 30° to 80°, buttons 30°, 45°, 48.6°), camera turn (slider, 45° step buttons, North), quality (High, Balanced, Fast), zoom (Auto, 1 to 4), switches (Wall shadows, Moving light, Smoothed ground), `Lose device (debug)`, and a readout of the backend (WebGPU or WebGL2 fallback), the zoom and the render size. F3 opens the frame times. The address bar follows every change; URL parameters: `pitch`, `yaw` (degrees), `quality=high|balanced|fast`, `zoom=1..8`, `backend=webgl` (force the fallback), `shadows=0`, `orb=0`, `smooth=0`, `stats=1`. The defaults are pitch 48.6° (the user's pick: a tile is 12 whole screen rows), turn 0°, Balanced, everything on except the frame times. Other keys of the address are kept. `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose]` loads it headless, prints the backend and any browser errors and saves `r3d.png`; `mode` is `webgl` (default) or `webgpu`, and `lose` forces a device loss and saves `r3d-after-loss.png`. |
| `?r3d` (debug accounts only) | A run in the 3D view (#302, §9 "World geometry and the 3D run"): login, roster, hub and lobby as always; on `RunStarted` the 3D host starts instead of the Phaser scene, and no `Phaser.Game` is created. Ignored on other accounts (the run opens in 2D). The generated map in 3D, every figure and shot as cards, the full HUD; the hero can only walk (W A S D, arrows), Esc the menu, Q / E watch teammates after death, F the free camera (movement keys pan it, Shift faster), F3 the HUD's panel. The panel at the bottom left adds a zoom row and the switches Lantern and Free camera (URL `light=lantern`, `free=1`, `see=soft|lattice|off`, `seeR`, `seeKeep`, `seeEdge` for the See through row; other keys of the address, such as `lag`, are kept). No debug keys and no debug paths in the 3D view yet. `node tools/e2e/r3d-run.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [level]` (level `dungeon` or `woodland`) registers an `e2e_*` debug account, starts a run with `?r3d&backend=webgl`, walks, toggles the free camera, checks culling on the dungeon, prints the number of figures drawn (it must be at least 1 once the run has started; `window.__r3d.figures`), saves `r3d-run-<level>.png` and `-free.png`, deletes the account and prints "no browser errors". |
| `?r3d=gallery` | The 3D twin of `?gallery` (#303, §9 "The 3D view"), no server and no login: every figure kind cycling through its states, the status-effect looks, chests, barrels, mimics, torches and shots, and a corridor behind a full-height wall for the see-through. The 3D panel applies (pitch, turn, quality, zoom, See through with Radius, Keep and Edge). URL: `state=<name>` pins one state (idle, move, melee, shoot, cast, windup, dash, channel, breath, tail, bite, takeoff, land), `slow=<n>` slows the clock, `focus=corridor\|effects\|objects` starts the camera there, `t=<s>` starts the clock there, `zoom` (default 2), and the 3D keys `pitch`, `yaw`, `quality`, `backend=webgl`, `see=soft\|lattice\|off`, `seeR` (0.5 to 3), `seeKeep` (0 to 1), `seeEdge` (0 to 1). `node tools/e2e/r3d-shot.mjs [baseUrl] [outDir] [extraQuery] [mode] [lose] gallery` shoots it. |
| `?lag=150&jitter=40&loss=2` | Network simulator. |

**The F3 panel** (#291). F3, or Network stats in the Esc menu's Settings, opens it for every account. The frame lines come from `FrameTimer` (`game/frameTimer.ts`), which runs only while the panel is open: closed, its `lap` returns at once and nothing is counted. Every time is an exponential average (0.9 old, 0.1 new; the first frame after opening seeds it) of main-thread CPU time; the GPU works asynchronously, so its own cost shows only where the CPU waits for it (a pixel readback, a texture upload).
- `frame`: the whole Phaser step, `game.events` `prestep` to `postrender` (input, scene update, render).
- `render`: `prerender` to `postrender`: display-list sort, culling, Graphics tessellation, batching and the WebGL calls of every camera, post pipelines included.
- `update`: `GameScene.update` alone, which is `RunSession.frame` (the old `frame` line).
- The indented lines split `update` by laps (`timer.lap(name)` books the time since the previous lap): `sim` (`RunSession.frame` up to the view: fixed steps, interpolation, prediction, the own shots' flight, the use hint's pick), `light` (the camera centring and `LightView.update`), `mood` (`Mood` and `Sunbeams`), `mask` (`VisionMask`, `ShadeMask`, unseen and darkvision FX sync), `figures` (the entity loop and pruning of views), `woodland` (`CoveView` and `WoodlandView`), `effects` (the use hint's key cap, the shots' images, bars, rules log, gaze, debug path, force field, terrain, `Effects`), `hud` (`Hud.frame`). The session books `sim` and `hud`, `PhaserRunView.frame` the rest. Since #302 the predicted shots' flight and the use hint's pick count under `sim` instead of `effects`, and the camera centring under `light`; the totals did not change, so a rise in `sim` and a fall in `effects` of that size is not a regression. A new system gets a name in `SYSTEMS` and a lap after its code.
- `objects`: `scene.children.length`, the display list's size.
- `draw calls`: WebGL `drawArrays` plus `drawElements` calls of the last frame. Phaser 3.90 has no counter, so while the panel is open `FrameTimer` wraps the two methods of the renderer's context (restored when it closes and at scene shutdown).
- `canvas`: the canvas's pixel size, plus `x<N> css` when Phaser's scale zoom upscales it (only with `?res=world`, §9).

`FrameTimer` takes its system names in the constructor (`new FrameTimer(SYSTEMS)` in `GameScene.init`; the session and `PhaserRunView` share that one timer), so the 3D view's page uses the same class with its own names (#301): `sim` (scene step and camera), `cards` (the per-frame card, flame and glow quads), `lights` (the light list), `upload` (the render plan, the readout line, and the buffer and uniform updates before the scene pass), `scene` (the scene pass alone) and `present` (the copy to the canvas; 0 on High). There `renderEnd(drawCalls)` is given three's count, because no WebGL context is wrapped; F3 on `?r3d=demo` shows FPS, `frame`, `render`, `update` (the first three laps), the six laps, the draw calls, the canvas and render sizes, the zoom and the backend (`render3d/stats.ts`, `statsText3d`). In a `?r3d` run F3 opens the HUD's panel instead, fed by the session (`sim`, `hud`) and the 3D view's numbers (draw calls, scene objects, canvas size).

## 11. Extending

- **New class:**
  - server: add a `ClassId` variant (`protocol.rs`), a `classes.rs` entry and an `EntityKind`; reuse or add `AbilityId`s with an executor in `run/abilities.rs`
  - client: add to the `FIGURES` entry, `ui/text.ts`, and `FIG`/`CLASSES` in `ui/classes.ts`
  - database: add a migration that widens the `characters.class` CHECK constraint
  - then run `cargo test`
- **New ability:** see "How to add an ability" in §11a.
- **New enemy:** add an `EnemyType` + `def()`, its `EntityKind`, population in `generate.rs::populate`, and a `FIGURES` entry. (The mimic is the exception: it is placed by `place_chests` and drawn by its own `MimicView`, not a `FIGURES` entry.)
- **New boss:**
  - server: add a `BossId` variant, a `defs/bosses.rs` entry and a new `run/bosses/<name>.rs` implementing `BossBehaviour`, plus a `create()` arm
  - client: add the `EntityKind` and a `FIGURES` entry, `BOSS_TEXT` in `ui/text.ts`, and `BOSSES`/`BOSS_FIG` in `ui/lobby.ts`

**How to add a companion** (see "Companions" in §5). The foe layer, snapshots, hints and the shot rules need nothing new: they read `side`.
1. **Recruited (a Neutral creature the heroes find):** an `EnemyType` with its `EntityKind`, race, weapon and `FIGURES` entry (frames from `scripts/figure-frames.ts` `buildFigure`, then `npm run atlas`). Spawn it Neutral where it belongs (the knight: `levels/dungeon.rs`, `companion::knight_spot`); `companion::recruit` and `behaviour::tick` (through `companion::regroup` and `act`) already handle it. `companion::recruit` gives it `KNIGHT_ESCORT` (the `escort` field of its `Companion`): a companion with other distances needs its own `Escort` in `defs/companions.rs` and a per-kind selection in `recruit`.
2. **Summoned (an ability makes it):** also a profile in `defs/companions.rs`, a `spawn` in `run/wolf.rs`' style (it behaves through `companion::regroup`/`act` and the melee base AI, with no script of its own), its own ability arm, an arm in `kill_monster`'s death hook, and a `Companion { summoned: true, .. }` so `update_leaders` removes it with its summoner.
3. **Tests:** the leader hierarchy, that no hero attack hurts it, that enemies fight it, and (recruited) that it is untouchable while Neutral. Re-record the fingerprint tests only if spawning it changes the simulation, and say so.

**How to add a creature behaviour** (see "AI" in §5). A creature is role + base AI + modules; pick the layer the new thing belongs to.
1. **A role** (whom it fights and what it does with no foe): a variant of `Role` (`run/behaviour.rs`), a line in `role()` (its order matters: the first match wins), and an arm in `behaviour::tick`. A role that needs state gets a field on `Monster` (as `companion`, `wild` and `grudge` did). The side and grudge rules (`run/targets.rs`) say who counts as its foe; no hook needs to change for that.
2. **A base AI** (how it moves and attacks): a variant of `BaseAi` (`defs/enemies.rs`), handled in `ai::tick_monster` next to `Melee`/`Ranged`/`Stationary`. A def sets `base_ai` and `wanders` (`EnemyDef`); `Monster.base_ai` and `wanders` copy them at spawn. A new way to walk is a `Chase` variant in `chase_of`/`chase_step`.
3. **A module** (what an ability brings): a variant of `Module` and `AbilityDef.module: Some(..)` in `defs/abilities.rs`, then fill the hooks of `run/modules.rs` it needs. `on_sight`, `on_act` and `on_windup_end` are a `match` on the module, so the compiler lists the arms to fill (return true when it claimed the tick); the base AI asks `on_sight` and `on_act` in kit order, so the order of the kit is the order of the modules. `on_dash_end` and `on_danger` are Leap-only today (the first returns early for any other module, the second finds the Leap ability by itself): a new module that needs them means restructuring those two, not just a new arm. Put the module's code in its own file (`ambush.rs`, `minions.rs`) or in `modules.rs` (the Leap).
4. **Tests:** the hook with a small `Run` (the tests in `modules.rs` and `ai.rs` are the pattern). Re-record the fingerprint tests only if the change moves a spawn or the simulation, and say why.

**How to add a light source** (see "Light sources" in §5). No client change is needed: the client draws whatever the registry sends.
1. **A new kind:** a row in `LightKind` and `def()` in `defs/lights.rs` (radius in tiles, default colour, intensity, flicker). Add it to `LightKind::ALL`. `cargo test` regenerates `LIGHT` in `defs.ts`.
2. **A static source** (fixed on the map: a brazier, mushrooms, goo, a fountain): `run.add_light(kind, Anchor::Tile(x, y), colour)` while the run is built (it goes out in `RunStartInfo.lights`) or later (it sends `Ev::LightAdd`). If it can be switched, keep its id and call `set_light(id, on)`; `remove_light(id)` deletes it. Torches are the example: a world object that owns the id (`ObjState::Torch.light`) and calls `set_light` from `set_torch`. A source on a tile that changes through `Run::set_tile` should be added and removed there, as lava is.
3. **A moving source** (a glowing enemy, a hero spell or item, the carriage lantern): either list the entity kind in `kind_light`, which attaches the light at spawn for every projectile and hazard, or call `add_light(kind, Anchor::Entity(id), colour)` explicitly (e.g. `Anchor::Entity(hero_id)` for an own hero light). The sweep removes it when the entity is gone.
   - **A flash** (an explosion, a spell burst): `run.add_flash(kind, pos, secs)`, a light on a spot that the sweep removes after `secs`. `Blast` (explosions) and `Holy` (the healing wave) are the examples.
4. **Colour override:** the `colour` argument of `add_light` (`None` = the kind's colour), or `set_light_colour(id, colour)` at any time (a cursed torch turning blue); a static light sends `Ev::Light` with the new colour, a moving one changes its `MovingLight` entry.
5. **Asking:** `run.is_lit(pos)` for game rules (the shadow, the torch-extinguishing enemy; those also use `set_light`).

**How to give a creature darkvision or longer sight** (see "Sight" in §5). No client change is needed.
1. **A whole kind:** change the race's `Sight` in `race_def` (`defs/races.rs`): `vision` in tiles, `darkvision` as a share of vision (1.0 = it sees an unlit target as far as a lit one).
2. **One enemy or hero class:** set `vision` and/or `darkvision` in its `Adjust` (`defs/enemies.rs`, `defs/classes.rs`); the profile takes it. An enemy's `vision` is in tiles.
3. **A boss:** `defs/bosses.rs` (25 tiles today); `bosses::target` already uses the same rule.
4. Add or adjust a test for the new numbers (`perception.rs` has the pattern: a dark map, a target at several distances). The hero filter and the greyscale follow `Player.sight` and the `SelfW` value by themselves.

**How to add an effect** (see "Status effects" in §5).
